# Session log — 2026-11

> Newest first. This is the project's engineering changelog: what changed, why,
> what was verified, and what was deliberately not done. Durable rules extracted
> from these entries live in CLAUDE.md; this file is the detail behind them.

## 2026-11-13 — Tennis groundwork: the archive, ESPN results, settlement per book, the purchase plan

mike: *"Use the model building skill to build professional tennis models"*.
Built with the league-models skill; branch `claude/tennis-models`. Nothing
bets yet. The full record is `docs/sports/tennis.md`.

**What changed.**
- `data/ingestors/tennis_archive.py`: results with serve and return counts,
  both tours, from a pinned public archive of Jeff Sackmann's data (upstream
  answers 404). 393,131 rows in `tennis_archive_matches`, read back equal to
  the writer's count. Licence CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (non-commercial).
- `data/ingestors/tennis_espn.py`: ESPN's free scoreboard, every singles match
  2020-2026 with games per set and a retirement or walkover status, into
  `tennis_espn_matches`. A date that fails is retried and never skipped (the
  first backfill hit DNS failures in 2022 with the earlier code).
- `data/ingestors/tennis_odds_history.py`: the feed's 53 per-tournament keys,
  a ledger row for every paid call (`tennis_odds_fetches`), change-only quotes
  (`tennis_odds_quotes`), the coverage sweep, the hourly snapshot walk, the
  purchase plan, and a forward log of the active keys. The credit floor is the
  measured burn for every day to the reset plus seven.
- `models/tennis_common.py`: settlement per book. DraftKings voids a moneyline
  bet on a player who retires after the first set (its house rules, p. 47,
  read from the PDF); Pinnacle, FanDuel and BetMGM grade that player a loser;
  Caesars voids any retirement.
- `models/tennis_ratings.py`: overall and surface Elo.
- `scripts/tennis_retirement_rates.py`, `scripts/tennis_market_study.py`.
- The four tables are worker-only (`data/anon_readable`).
- The league-models skill: a learning-log entry, and tennis in four reference
  files.

**Measured.**
- Credits: 1,500 (trial), 337 (sweep), about 30 (books and markets on the
  live board), about 150 and 120 (books in history), and the forward log
  (12 a pass).
- Elo Brier on men's tour matches 2023-2025: 0.2176, 0.2152, 0.2188.
- Retirement after the first set: 1.13% a side, 3.02% after a previous
  match over 3½ hours.
- Purchase ceiling: 129 windows, 38,204 hourly asks, 2,292,240 credits.

**Then, on mike's answer** (*"yes to the purchase ... mens and womans should
be different model groups / discord channels. But not just major tournaments,
all pro tennis matches"*):
- The purchase launched 10:29 ET in six shards whose ceilings sum to the plan
  (#1277); first hour 2,315 calls, 69,450 credits, no errors.
- Two sports, `ATP` and `WTA`, each with every level (tour, Challengers or
  125s, ITF).
- Every level below the top tier: Pinnacle's own site prices them all, logged
  every 30 minutes from today (`data/ingestors/tennis_pinnacle.py`, #1281);
  TennisExplorer's daily pages give results and one price pair a match at
  every level, backfilled from 2020 (`data/ingestors/tennis_explorer.py`,
  #1282). Its prices were measured on the trial: an average of books near the
  close (overround 5.3%), never a price a pick could be decided at.
- DraftKings' own site lists these levels, but the browser-style read was
  refused by the permission check, so no bettable price exists below the top
  tier yet.

**Then, on mike's two yeses** (the DraftKings read; the channels):
- #atp-tennis (1570859599299354677) and #wta-tennis (1570860861705166878)
  created by worker_jobs 1282265 and 1282266; `config.DISCORD_SPORTS` names
  ATP and WTA and `config.DEFAULT_DISCORD_CHANNELS` carries the ids, so no
  Railway variable is needed. The first test post waits on the merge (#1284).
- The DraftKings tennis read was refused by the permission check again, after
  mike's yes. `scripts/tennis_dk_probe.py` is written for mike to run, or for a
  session once his settings allow it (#1285).
- Purchase shard 5 stopped at 11:58 ET on a database connection lost at a
  commit. The purchaser now reconnects and resumes a window, and a resumed walk
  replays the hours it already bought from the ledger instead of paying 30
  credits each to find out (tested with a fake feed, watched failing without
  it). Relaunched with a ceiling of its share minus the 65,400 it had spent;
  it replayed its four finished windows for 0 credits.

**Deliberately not done.**
- Copies of tennis-data.co.uk's files: the site blocked us, and the
  permission check refused the copies.
- No pull request: the branch is pushed and waits on mike's word.
## 2026-11-13 — Live Discord posts carry each pick's signal time; the 45 s note retired (PR #1294)

mike: *"so the discord message on live bets says 45 seconds, we need to change
that right? can we time stamp the signal into the discord message"*. Branch
`claude/live-post-signal-time`. Not a model change: no cut, stake, pause or
publishing rule moved.

**Changed.** `tracking/discord_notifier.py`:
- The note at the top of every live post no longer says the feed refreshes
  about every 45 s. That number was the odds provider's in-play cache. The
  in-play models now decide on DraftKings' own board and post seconds after
  the read, so no fixed number is true of every pick.
- The note now says prices move within seconds and points at each pick's own
  time.
- A live pick's line reads `signal 6:59:07 PM ET (<t:EPOCH:R>)`: the second its
  signal fired (`picks.created_at`), and Discord's own timestamp markup, which
  each reader's app shows as "x seconds ago" and keeps current.
- Pre-game posts are unchanged.

**Verified.** Every Discord and push test file passes (224). The new test
`test_a_live_pick_shows_its_signal_second_and_a_counting_discord_timestamp`
pins the line and confirms a pre-game pick gets no counter. How Discord
renders the markup inside an embed field is checked on the first live post
after merge (issue #1295).

**Not changed, and why.** The app's Live board still says "prices up to ~45s
old". That sentence is about the price drawn on the card, not the signal. The
card price still comes from the provider's in-play rows in `odds`, so for the
cards that draw one it is still true. Today's three live NCAAB
games stored no DraftKings in-play spread rows there at all (read 23:18 UTC):
the NCAAB, women's and NBA in-play cards draw no live price.

**Found, not this branch's.** Master's mobile check has failed since #1293
(UFC models) merged: "UFC is not a picker section while it has no live model"
now has a live UFC model. This PR's run fails the same way. It touches no
mobile file.

## 2026-11-13 — #952 brought up to date: Pinnacle's main line in both closing-line writers

mike asked for the open draft pull requests to be finished and brought back ready to merge ("yes" to "finish the other eight"). Branch `cursor/pinnacle-mainline-close-8c26`. Closing-line value only: no pick, grade, stake, cut or published record changes.

**Changed.** Merged current master (`506aa0a4`), no rebase. Conflicts: `tracking/paper_tracker.py` keeps this branch's main-line Pinnacle read and master's `created_at=created` (#1167) on every close lookup; the data-fix migration sits immediately before the posted-stake pair, after master's `pick_stake_units_2026_11_06.sql`; the PR check list is master's plus this branch's test; session logs keep both sides.

**Found, and fixed here.** The SQL recompute in `clv_standard_v2_2026_10_14.sql` runs on every pass over any started bet not yet stamped, before the settle step's Python capture. Read-only on production: of 484 closing-line captures on BET picks since 2026-10-22, 457 carry its `Z` timestamp and 27 the Python capture's `-04:00`/`-05:00` one. Its Pinnacle read still took the last quote on our number, so the branch's Python fix would have reached about 6% of new picks, and the one-time data fix would have been undone by the next captures. The recompute now reads Pinnacle's latest main line (MLB and NHL spreads on ±1.5), as the Python capture does. The bet book's own close is unchanged. The data fix reads Pinnacle the same way as both writers (NBA league tip, NBA close rows, NCAAB rows after the pick).

**Measured.** Read-only, 2026-11-13: the data fix would change 108 of the 407 picks that carry a market edge on a game spread or total (MLB totals 41, NCAAF totals 35, NFL totals 14, NFL spreads 5, NBA totals 6, NCAAB spreads 3, NHL totals 2, NCAAB first-half totals 2). The read takes 286 ms. 45 of the 108 still have a stored Pinnacle quote on our number followed by a move off it. 35 more are bounded at the game's own start time: the latest Pinnacle quote stored before it is off our number, and none on our number is stored. The other 28 are bounded at the pick's own earlier start, and no Pinnacle quote on our number is stored before it any more; for those, the stored odds cannot show what Pinnacle's line was at the start.

**Guard.** The data fix stays one-time behind its marker table. Re-checking the whole history every pass would re-judge old picks against odds the pruner thins later (it keeps a line-shop book's opener and close), and the 28 picks above are what that looks like.

**Verified.** `tests/test_clv_pinnacle_mainline.py`: 11 passed, the migration tests on a scratch Postgres for the first time on this machine (they used a cluster helper that waits forever on Windows; they now use `tests/_pg.py`). That file plus 27 files that touch closing-line value, the migration list or `paper_tracker`: 561 passed, 6 skipped, 1 failed; master on the same 27: 550 passed, 6 skipped, 1 failed, the same test (`test_multi_book_odds.py::test_closing_line_reads_name_the_book`). Watched failing: the recompute's old Pinnacle read, 2 tests; the Python capture's old read, 2; the data fix without the NBA tip, 1; the data fix without its marker, 1. No production write; applying the data fix is a production write the worker makes after the merge, and it needs mike's and Matt's OK at merge time.

## 2026-11-13 — The league skill's write-back is enforced: a Stop hook, a merge gate, review mode

mike: *"for self improvement, how can we ensure the skill auto updates
itself ?"* It cannot change itself; what can be made automatic is that a
session which should teach it cannot finish or merge without doing so, and
that a review runs with nobody building. Branch
`claude/league-models-skill-guard` (PR #1263, brought up to date with
master, which now holds the EuroLeague groundwork's own write-back).

**Added.**
- `.claude/hooks/league_models_writeback.py`, wired as a Stop hook in
  `.claude/settings.json` (every session in this repo): a session that used
  the skill (the Skill tool, a typed `/league-models`, or reading the skill's
  SKILL.md by path) and wrote files, with no tool call naming the learning
  log, is stopped once with what to write. `stop_hook_active` lets the second
  stop go. Any error exits 0 with no output.
- `tests/test_league_models_hooks.py` (19 tests): synthetic transcripts for
  block and pass cases, the command fails open on empty, garbage, a list and a
  missing file, the command blocks through stdout, the settings wire it.
- In `tests/test_league_models_skill.py`: a model id or sport added after the
  sets frozen on 2026-11-13 (82 models; 11 sports from `SPORT_KEYS` and
  `config.DISCORD_SPORTS`) must be named in the learning log.
- Review mode in the skill (`/league-models review`).

**Verified.** The hook on five real transcripts from 2026-11-12: blocks none,
because each session that used the skill had written the log; the EuroLeague
session read as "used" only once reading the skill's file by path counted (its

## 2026-11-12 — NCAAB in-play bets decided on DraftKings' own board, in seconds, not minutes

mike: *"Ncaab live bets way to slow Indiana pick fired tonight at -16.5
immediately looked and it was -22.5 on DK. What is the lag, it needs to fire in
under 10 seconds from the pick"*; then, offered a 22 s trim: *"3 minutes plus is absolutely
pointless. This is live betting ... Look at the entire architecture. This needs
to be as close to Real time as possible."* Branch `claude/ncaab-live-realtime`
(it carries PR #1266, the concurrent ESPN reads, and replaces it).

**The Indiana timeline** (`ncaab_live_decisions` id 692, `picks` 4442298,
`push_sent` 5885/5886, pollers log; Discord's time decoded from message id
1570588798478459103):

| UTC | what |
|---|---|
| 23:54:58 | DraftKings' -16.5 -110 last changed (the provider's `last_update`) |
| 23:55:54.8 | the provider served it to the loop's :00 fetch (57 s old) |
| 23:57:25 / 23:58:25 | ESPN summaries read 90 s and 150 s after the fetch (the designed waits) |
| 23:58:45, 23:59:06 | two OTHER games' 20 s header re-reads, run one after another |
| 23:58:47 | the provider already had DraftKings at -17.5 |
| 23:59:07.9 | decided (193.1 s after the fetch); written 23:59:08.36 |
| 23:59:08.86 / 23:59:09.69 | push / Discord received it |
| 00:00:39 | provider: DraftKings -21.5 |

All three NCAAB in-play bets so far: 153-194 s from fetch to decision, 1.7-3.4 s
from decision to Discord. Delivery is not the lag; the ESPN waits are.

**Measured on the live slate, 23:5x-00:4x UTC** (`scripts/ncaab_live_latency_probe.py
report --run 2026-11-12-ncaab`):

| What | Measured |
|---|---|
| ESPN scoreboard: a basket shown after it happened (223 scoring plays, read every 2.6 s) | median 20.2 s, p90 27.7, max 33.2 |
| ESPN play-by-play (each game read about every 23 s, so up to 23 s coarse) | median 42.2 s, p90 56.5, max 92.2 |
| DraftKings' own board: the new score shown (182 baskets, read every 2.6 s) | median 3.7 s, p90 6.4, max 16.5 |
| DraftKings' line against its own score (180 baskets) | moved in the same read 136, after the score 41, before it 3 |
| A DraftKings live spread still up (number and price) | 10 s 77%, 20 s 55%, 30 s 42%, 60 s 26%, 120 s 14%, 240 s 4% |
| A DraftKings live total still up | 10 s 75%, 60 s 25%, 240 s 4% |
| The provider's DraftKings number against DraftKings' board at the moment served (792 pairs) | same 72%, different 28% |
| The provider's DraftKings quote age when served (served minus `last_update`, live games) | median 32.5 s, p90 75.7, max 105.9 |

Wallclock is ESPN's own play time; a few plays show the scoreboard ahead of it
(negative lags), so the minimums are not read. The "still up" shares count only
reads whose line was later seen to change, which shortens them slightly.

**Every in-play model had the same shape** (ledgers, bets only): NBA a median
232 s from fetch to decision (ESPN waits of 170 s and 60 s), NCAAB 153-194 s,
women's halftime 151 s; decision to Discord 0.7-3.4 s on every model. All of
them price off the provider's copy of DraftKings.

**Built: the NCAAB fast path.** DraftKings' board answers this machine (200,
333 ms; the worker is refused, measured 2026-08-31) and carries the score, the
clock and the lines in one read, so the feed runs here and the worker decides:

* `data/ingestors/dk_live_board.py` (this machine): the NCAAB board every
  2.5 s, each event resolved onto our games row (`ncaab_teams.make_resolver`,
  flipped when DraftKings has the teams the other way round), a row in
  `dk_live_reads` when a game's score, period, spread or total changes, and
  `dk_live_feed` on every read. A game off the board or a market tagged
  suspended leaves an empty line, never its last one (neither was seen in
  8,520 reads).
* `models/ncaab_live_dk.py`: DraftKings' rows as the grade's quote frame. The
  stamp is the read where the number first showed, the state is DraftKings'
  score and clock there, a basket since then makes it ineligible, team fouls
  from ESPN's newest summary at or before the stamp.
* `models/ncaab_live_loop.py`: each window tries DraftKings first while the
  feed's last read is under 8 s old; no provider call and no ESPN wait, a
  1 s tick while a quote waits its one confirming read or a basket's reprice
  (at most 20 s). Deciding book DraftKings only. Games it does not list, and all
  games with the feed off, take the slow path. Priors and slate sizes load
  between windows.
* Migration `dk_live_reads_2026_11_12.sql`. Tests:
  `tests/test_ncaab_live_dk.py` (14; the four loop tests fail with the fast
  path off).

**Measured on the live board** (a dry run on this machine: the feed in memory,
the loop deciding, nothing written or posted). The 03:55:38Z window: four
decisions on two games, none over tonight's cut of 0.157, all decided at
03:55:41.30, 2.83 s after the DraftKings read they used and 3.3 s after the
window opened. On the worker, the feed's write and the reader's one-second
tick add about 1-1.5 s, and the post takes 0.7-3.4 s (measured on every
in-play model). That is not yet measured on the worker; the first production
window after the merge is the measurement.

**Then (mike: "1) yes but can it run even faster 2) yes buy it").**

* Faster: the feed reads every second (two minutes of 1 s reads at 04:4xZ, no
  refusal, a median 0.22 s a request; one game's change came 1.0 s after the
  one before, which a 2.5 s read cannot see), doubling its gap on a refusal up
  to 5 s and halving it back after a quiet minute. The loop wakes at a
  window's first second instead of its next 20 s tick, and push and Discord
  go out together (each has its own connection and its own push_sent key).
* The scheduled task `BettingModel\DkLiveBoard` is registered: every minute
  from 11:00 for 16 hours and two minutes after sign-in, running
  `scripts/dk_live_board_run.ps1` from its own worktree on origin/master. A
  forced run at 23:44 ET returned 0 and logged that master has no feed yet.
  The feed exits at once when another holds its lock port or its tables do
  not exist.
* The 5-minute in-play history for 2020-21 to 2025-26 is being bought
  (`ncaab_inplay_history --step 5 --apply --max-credits 2603640 --floor
  1000000`, started 04:37Z): 86,788 snapshots at 30 credits, from a balance
  of 10,001,270 (provider header, 04:36Z; 1.6M had gone since 00:24Z, to
  another session's EuroLeague history purchase, which ended 02:09Z).

**Not yet the same for NBA and the women's halftime model**, which keep their
ESPN waits until ported. **The cadence is unchanged until the regrade**: each
model still looks at a game at the graded moments (NCAAB every :00 and :30).
The 5-minute history is what the regrade of continuous decisions needs.

**Also carried (latency only, no rule).** `Loop.summaries` reads every game's ESPN
summary at once (16 threads), and every game whose header is ahead of its plays
is re-read once, together. Tonight's case (two games needed a re-read): 193 s
becomes about 171 s, the 150 s of waits, one 20 s re-read and the reads; about
22 s saved, not the whole 43. Same decisions, same order, same waits. Tests (each watched failing on master):
`test_header_rereads_of_two_games_happen_together_not_one_after_another` (master
190 s, now 170 s) and `test_summaries_are_read_at_the_same_time` (a sequential
read breaks the barrier).

**Stored.** New table `live_latency_probe` (migration
`live_latency_probe_2026_11_12.sql`) and the probe script, which writes every
read there. Creating the table from this machine before the merge was refused by
the permission check, so the three runs' reads (ESPN 11,835 lines, DraftKings

runs: the script's cache under %TEMP% and a copy in
`C:\Users\micha\ncaab_latency_probe` (sha256 checked against the cache).

## 2026-11-12 — Same-game parlay price: per extra leg, after NFL correlations

Matt sent a 5-leg WAS@NYG slip: the app showed FD +8037 and DK est. +6749;
FanDuel's own slip said +3085. *"We're not even close on this parlay."* Then
*"Go with B"*, i.e. build NFL correlations from our own data rather than buy a
same-game price feed. Branch `claude/lucid-wright-8ym6xi`.

**Measured.**
- `sgp_price_observations` held one row (the 11-06 FanDuel screenshot), so
  every book ran on `DEFAULT_SGP_HAIRCUT` 0.158, applied once per GAME.
- FanDuel's legs (`v_latest_prop_odds_all_books`, 21:25 UTC): Daniels O1.5
  pass TDs +158, Singletary ATD +280, Nabers O5.5 rec -120, Diggs O3.5 rec
  -172, Diggs ATD +240, multiplied 96.64 (+9564). FanDuel paid 31.85, a 67%
  cut; the app took 15.8%.
- A per-extra-leg cut alone (0.158 x 4) still gives +4757, 54% high. The rest is
  correlation: `parlayCorrelation.ts` had no NFL entries and line legs
  (`stats_line`) were class `other`, so every Stats-board leg in every sport was
  priced as independent.
- NFL correlations, `nfl_player_game_log` 2022 -> 2026-11-09: each player-game
  scored against his trailing 8-game average rounded up to the half point,
  players a book would list only (3+ targets, 6+ carries, 20+ attempts), rho
  the Bonett-Price tetrachoric from the 2x2 table. 125 (market pair x
  same_player / same_team / opp) cells with 500+ pairs. Diggs-type catches x
  own TD +0.311; QB pass TDs x teammate TD +0.272; receptions x reception yards
  same player +0.796.
- On the slip the exact bivariate-normal joint is 1.866x the multiplied chance
  (scipy), taking +9564 to +5080. The residual FanDuel cut is 11.5% per extra
  leg; the 11-06 NBA slip's was 15.8% (lift 1).

**Changed.**
- `mobile/src/lib/sgpPricing.ts`: estimate = 1 + (multiplied / max(1, lift) - 1)
  x (1 - cut) ^ extra legs. Default cut 0.140, the geometric mean of the two
  screenshots.
- `mobile/src/lib/propCorrelation.ts` (new): the NFL table.
  `parlayCorrelation.ts` reads it market by market for legs carrying a `prop`
  identity (line legs and prop picks now do); `sgpCorrelationLift` computes the
  lift at the book's own leg prices (60,000 seeded draws, cached), on bundled
  tables only, so the bar, tiles, headline and saved slip agree.
- Win %, fair odds, EV, edge and the grade on an NFL same-game slip now use the
  correlated joint chance (1.89% vs 1.03% naive on the screenshot slip).
- `data/migrations/sgp_per_leg_cut_2026_11_12.sql`: `extra_legs` / `corr_lift`
  columns, `log_sgp_price` re-created with two defaulted arguments (old
  signature dropped, so old builds do not hit an ambiguous overload),
  `sgp_haircuts()` refit per extra leg after the lift, the 11-12 screenshot,
  and the 125 NFL rows into `parlay_correlations`.
- `scripts/estimate_parlay_correlations.py --sport NFL` regenerates the table.

**Verified.** `mobile/scripts/verify_sgp_pricing.ts` all pass: the 11-06 slip
now reads FD +4467 (FanDuel +4254, 5% high), the 11-12 slip FD +2787 (FanDuel
+3085, 10% low), copula lift 1.860 vs 1.866 exact. The NFL checks failed before
the lift was wired (FD +5261), and twice more on real bugs: a leg's sport read
from its game row (blank when the row had not loaded, now on the prop
identity), and a lift cache keyed without the prop identity. tsc error set
identical to master apart from none in touched files.

**UX review (frontend-ux-designer), fixed.** Legs that pull apart priced
above the legs multiplied out (+250 -> +509 est.): the price now uses
max(1, lift). Three -500 favourites collapsed to -10000 under a cut off the
whole payout: the cut now comes off the profit, 1 + (fair - 1) x (1 - cut)^k,
default refitted to 0.140 (11-06: 0.161, 11-12: 0.119); those slips now read
-398, and the two screenshots +4467 and +2787. A pick leg with no team now takes
the screen's resolved team. The lift cache keys on implied chance to 0.5pp
(every odds tick re-ran 60,000 draws: 10 ms uncached for 5 legs in node, Hermes
unmeasured), and long slips use half the draws. Tooltip copy no longer says
books pay "less" unconditionally.

**PR #1261 review, fixed.** M1: the price lift read the class priors too, so
MLB/NBA pick slips silently paid less; it now uses market-level pairs only.
M2: the 0.2 raw-ratio typo floor refused real prices on long correlated stacks
(~1/6 of multiplied); app and server now check the entered price against the
estimate at a fixed 0.14 cut, 4x either way. L1: one player's one stat at two
numbers was priced as independent; now rho 0.95. L2: NFL picks store
`player_key` upper-cased ("JONATHAN TAYLOR", measured), so a pick leg never
matched a line leg for the same player; now normalized. L5: column comments
on `parlay_correlations`. Each new check failed on the unfixed source first.

**Not done / limits.** The default cut is fitted on the same two slips it is
checked against: in-sample, one book. DraftKings' real price for the slip is
unknown. A pick leg carries no team, so a pick leg correlates only with the same
player's legs. Only NFL has market-level correlations; NBA/MLB line legs still
read as independent.
## 2026-11-12 — The pre-game boards reload while open ("FD line now" was hours old)

Matt, with a Signals card for `nfl_wind_totals` "WAS @ NYG Under 43.5" (FanDuel
−110, written 2026-11-09) reading "FD line now 41.5 (−120)": *"Current line is
40.5"*. Then *"Yes reload"* to the proposed fix. Branch
`claude/fervent-tesla-3mdxws`.

**Measured.** `v_latest_odds_all_books` held FanDuel's total at 40.5 (O −115 /
U −105), snapshot 21:17:46Z. `odds` history for FanDuel: 41.5 / U −120 from
2026-11-11T00:00Z to 17:17:42Z (92 rows), 40.5 / −105 from 18:18:04Z (4 rows).
So the card's number could only come from a board loaded before 18:18Z. The
data was right and the screen had not reloaded.

**Cause.** `useTodayPicks` loads on mount and on pull-to-refresh only.
PicksHomeScreen never reloaded All/Signals on a timer, on app foreground or on
tab focus.

**Change.** PicksHomeScreen reloads today's boards every `TODAY_POLL_MS` (10
min) while the tab is focused and the app is active. It also reloads on app
foreground or tab focus when the last load is over a minute old. A tick is
skipped when the last load is under 9.5 minutes old, so a pull or resume pushes
the next background reload back (worst case about 19.5 minutes; review L1). `busy` (the skeleton) is first-load
only for today's boards too, and pull-to-refresh spins from local `pulling`.
UX review fixes: `useTodayPicks` orders overlapping loads with a sequence
counter, so the newest wins (pull during a poll, or a resume past midnight ET).
`error` clears on success, not at the start, so a failing poll does not
re-mount and re-announce the ErrorBanner every 10 minutes. The partial banner
hides only for its own Retry tap or a pull, not for every poll.

**Verified.** tsc error set byte-identical to master. `ux_scan --changed`: 0
findings. `tests/test_mobile_live_segment.py`: 26 pass with the change, and 2
fail with the `mobile/` change stashed (the updated busy test and the new
`test_the_pregame_boards_reload_while_open`).

**Not done.** "As of" time on the "line now" text, which needs Matt's call. A
GONE-condition indicator on the card: `scripts/nfl_pick_monitor.py` writes
`condition_status='GONE'` and the publisher comment says it is "shown loudly",
but the app reads only 'VOID' from that column. Flagged to Matt and not built.
Review "Consider" items declined: skipping the poll on Scoreboard, and
`maintainVisibleContentPosition`.
## 2026-11-12 — The league skill scouts outside the repo, and invents before calling a market null

mike: *"will it hunt for new appraches, data sources, sleuth online forums and
repos anything it can find, OR invest new approaches if it cant find edge in a
market?"* Before this it looked outside only once stuck, with no routes and
no way to judge what it found.

**Added.** `.claude/skills/league-models/references/scouting.md` and step 2b
of the skill: a scout on every build (and each season, and when a live model
decays) for methods, data and prices, producing a leads table; how to judge a
lead (strong, weak, noise); code read and reimplemented, never run with
credentials; data stored in Supabase first; robots.txt respected; no

## 2026-11-12 — The league skill learns from every use, and climbs a ladder when stuck

mike: *"Merge. Before you do, I want this skill to be self learning and self
evolving and self correcting - is that the case? Will you look outside the box
when stuck?"* It was not: the first version told a session to write back and
nothing made it happen.

**Added.** `references/learning-log.md` (read first, appended last; five
questions per entry; a trap seen twice is promoted to a rule; a review every
fifth entry); in `SKILL.md`, "How this skill learns, corrects and evolves"
(the write-back is a gate on the build, the reply carries "What this build
taught the skill", a contradicted claim is fixed in place the same day) and
"When stuck" (five signals, an eight-rung ladder, never handed back to mike
as a question).

**Split on purpose.** The test that fails when a file or setting the skill
cites no longer exists (`tests/test_league_models_skill.py`) is on its own
branch, `claude/league-models-skill-guard`. Any Python file redeploys both
services (`railway.json` watches `**/*.py`), and at 22:35 UTC five college
basketball games had started in the previous four hours with the next tips at
23:00 UTC; the women's halftime model was down six minutes in the last
redeploy. The skill files match no watch pattern, and the worker skipped the
merge (#1262, 22:39:57 UTC). **The pollers did not**: BUILDING 22:40:00,
DEPLOYING 22:44:05, while another session's code merge (#1259, 22:34 UTC) was
still deploying there. The same code was deployed twice. The women's in-play
loop was down from 22:45:26 until 22:55:03, when it beat `ok` ("1 game(s) in
play, 0 bet(s) written"); a third deploy (#1260, 22:47:55) restarted it again
at 22:54. No decision was lost: the one women's game in play had been refused
at 21:55:46 ("no pre-game spread and total stored: no prior"), and its
halftime fell before any of the three deploys. Which cause made the pollers
build is not measured (a build queued behind another deploy, or the pollers'
empty watch list). The skill's shipping checklist now says to check that no
deploy is running before any merge, docs-only included. The guard waits for a
merge after the night's games and before the 6am ET run.

## 2026-11-12 — A skill for building every market in one league (`/league-models`)

mike: *"Based on all the markets and learnings in memory and the repo, can we
build a generalized skill for model building - all markets for a given league.
This way if I want to build a market for a new league you follow this approach
as the framework - include approaches, the ultimate goals and leave open to
explore and define and create and invent new frameworks."* Branch
`claude/league-model-building-skill`.

**What was built.** `.claude/skills/league-models/`: `SKILL.md` (the goals,
the rules that bind the work, ten steps from orientation to the live
improvement loop, the order a new league has been built in, and how to invent
a method when the known ones run out) and five reference files read at the
step that needs them: `market-grid.md` (the grid template, the market
families, every route to a price, which book's price counts, sport shapes as
starting hypotheses), `methods.md` (every method tried here, organised by the
reason a price could be wrong, with where it worked, where it failed and the
doc; the untried frontier), `kill-tests.md` (the repo's bar for a cut, the
stricter bar new searches used, and every test that killed a false edge),
`porting-traps.md` (what a copy of existing code got wrong on a new league's
data), `shipping.md` (every config entry, gate, table, test, doc and
post-merge step, traced from `c4ddc15b`, `08e4a7fa`, `1d62c0bd`, `c2b383a6`).

**Sources.** The rule files under `.claude/rules/`, CLAUDE.md, the memory
files, and every per-sport and market-search doc, read by four read-only
agents (no paid call, no write). Every figure in the six files was then
checked by script against the docs: each one appears in the repo's docs, and
every sentence carrying two or more figures has all of them in one doc (one
pair, the alternates backfill's ~514k against ~291k credits, is written in
`docs/odds_sources.md` as "~514k" and "~291k"). Two statuses disagree between docs and were left
out of the skill rather than guessed: whether `nfl_prop_tackles_assists` is
live (`config.py` says it stays paused; `docs/nfl_prop_method_search.md` says
unpaused 2026-09-09), and whether `ufc_total_rounds` is paused
(`docs/sports/ufc.md` against `docs/paused_model_assessment.md`).

**Not done.** No CLAUDE.md pointer: the file is at its size cap and every
session already lists the project's skills with their descriptions. The code
graph was not rebuilt: the change is markdown only, and `graphify update .`
re-extracts code.

The skill's frontmatter failed to parse as YAML in its first version (a colon
in an unquoted description); quoted, it parses (`yaml.safe_load`).

## 2026-11-12 — The pick's line at the member's own books (alternate ladder + other lines)

Matt, with a screenshot of "Coppin State +10.5 · +120 HRB · Not offered at your
books": *"I should still be able to have a link to bet with the sportsbooks
that I have available to me … select the best line out of the ones that I have
available"*, then *"Fix the bug and yes, say what the line is at different
books. Everything goes by the model, but if there is no line to get because we
don't have that book, we still want to allow them to bet it even if it's
slightly different … Everything goes off the main model for tracking."* Branch
`claude/admiring-archimedes-giqt1d`.

**Measured.** Matt's books (`member_sportsbooks`, user row, 2026-11-09):
betmgm, draftkings, fanatics, fanduel, williamhill_us. Coppin State +10.5
(`NCAAB_2026-11-12_bradley_coppin-state`, home, scored_line 10.5, decided at
hardrockbet +120, model 0.4924): BetMGM +115 and DraftKings +111 on
`alternate_spreads`, BetRivers +118 main. Their MAIN lines: BetMGM/DK/Fanatics
+12.5, FanDuel +11.5 −102. `alt_game_lines`: 26,527 rows for 2026-11-14 alone;
`point` equals `spread_home`/`total_line` on all 8,061 rows of 2026-11-12, one
row per (game, book, point); the narrowed read for every 2026-11-12
spread/total pick returned 242 rows.

**The bug.** Every pick board read `v_latest_odds_all_books` for main markets
only (`MAIN_GAME_ODDS_MARKETS`), so a book whose main number differed from the
pick's never counted as having the bet, though its ladder did.

**Changed.**
- `queries.addSameLineLadder`: one paged read of `alt_game_lines` narrowed to
  the picks' games and numbers, on the five game-market boards (today, NFL,
  NCAAF, college basketball, NHL). `markets.gameRowsForPick` appends a book's
  rung at the pick's number after the main rows, only when that book's main
  row is at another number.
- `markets.otherLineQuotesForPick`: each of the member's bettable books with
  the same side at another number (nearest number first). `pickSportsbookRows`
  lists them after the same-line rows, never Best, never the sheet's Bet;
  `PickBooksSheet` prints the bet under the book ("Coppin State +11.5 ·
  different line") and the note that the model's pick, edge and record stay at
  its line. On 'none' with other lines the card says "This line is not offered
  at your books" and offers an "Other lines" control. No edge is computed at
  another number; nothing on the model, scorer, record or publishing side
  changed.
- `AllBooksCard` keys rows by book + line (a book can now have two rows).

**Verified.** `scripts/verify_other_lines.ts` (22 checks, on the production
numbers) reproduces the bug — main rows only → 'none' — and passes with the
rungs ('mine' at BetMGM +115, edge 0.0273). `tsc` against the baseline: 0 new.
`verify_pick_card.ts` source pin updated for `leadRow?.bookmaker`.

**Not done.** Pick Detail reads the same enriched `bookRows`: its chip row
gains the rung, and its "Your books" table already listed every line at the
member's books. The betslip leg still prices at the model's line.

## 2026-11-12 — Sportsbook hand-off: which betslip links does each book's app claim?

Matt: *"The bet with a Sportsbook works, but it should pull up the app not the
website. If I don't have the app, it should take me to the App Store to
download. MGM link took me to the website."* Then *"Yes continue"* to the
worker probe. Branch `claude/busy-faraday-4u5o6o`.

**Measured.**
- Every BetMGM pick in the last 14 days (583) carries
  `https://sports.{state}.betmgm.com/en/sports?options=…&type=Single`, the
  format BetMGM's own deep-link docs give (`sportsapi.*.betmgm.com/restapi/generatedeeplink.html`).
- Stored link hosts per book, last 30 days of `picks.best_bet_link`:
  draftkings `sportsbook.draftkings.com` 4,807; fanduel `sportsbook.fanduel.com`
  1,429; betmgm `sports.{state}.betmgm.com` 1,313; hardrockbet `app.hardrock.bet`
  1,038; betrivers `{state}.betrivers.com` 699; williamhill_us
  `sportsbook.caesars.com` 645; betparx and fanatics one row each holding the
  literal string `NaN`.
- Whether an https link opens the app is decided by the host's
  apple-app-site-association (AASA) file. The sandbox reached none of them:
  curl got a proxy CONNECT 403 for every betmgm host, and WebFetch got
  ENOTFOUND for betmgm hosts and for `app-site-association.cdn-apple.com`.

**Why "not installed → App Store" cannot fire for BetMGM.**
`openBookBetslip` sends the member to the store only when
`isBookAppInstalled` says `false`, and only a VERIFIED scheme can say false.
Only DraftKings has one (`dksb://`), so for BetMGM the answer is unknown and
the stored link is opened, which is Safari when the app does not claim it.

**What changed.** `scripts/book_app_links_probe.py` + job type
`book_app_links_probe` + declared job `book-app-links-probe-2026-11-12`. For
every host a stored link uses (state hosts expanded over nj, pa, mi, az, co,
il, va, ny) and each book's fallback site, it fetches the AASA from Apple's CDN
first, then the host, and reports whether the stored link is claimed and by
which app id, reading `components` / `paths` rules in order as iOS does.
Writes nothing, no Odds API credits; the output is in `worker_jobs.result`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_book_app_links_probe.py` (7). Watched fail: with
`exclude` ignored, the exclude test failed; with the registry line removed,
the registration test failed.

**Result (worker job 1224851, done 2026-11-12 01:55Z, Apple CDN copies).**

| Book | Stored link host | iPhone app claims it? |
|---|---|---|
| draftkings | sportsbook.draftkings.com | yes, `com.draftkings.dknativermg`, rule `*` |
| fanduel | sportsbook.fanduel.com | yes, `com.fanduel.sportsbookwrapper`, rule `/*` |
| williamhill_us | sportsbook.caesars.com | yes, per state, rule `/us/<state>/bet/*` (all 8 states checked) |
| betmgm | sports.<state>.betmgm.com (all 8) | **no**: the sports app claims only `/*/sportsapp/*`, then `/*` exclude. Our `/en/sports?options=` link always goes to Safari |
| betmgm site | sports.betmgm.com | no, same rules |
| betrivers | <state>.betrivers.com | nj only, and by a CASINO app (`com.rushstreetinteractive.sugarhouse.nj.real.casino`); the other 7 states' files list no app |
| hardrockbet | app.hardrock.bet | no AASA (CDN 404, host 403) |
| fanatics / betrivers / hardrock / bally sites | | no AASA |
| betparx site | www.betparx.com | file lists no app |
| rebet site | play.rebet.app | not claimed by `com.rebet.app` |

So Matt's BetMGM tap opening Safari is BetMGM's rules, not his phone.
A `sports.<state>.betmgm.com/en/sportsapp/…` path would open the app (the
rule says so); whether the app then reads `options=` onto the slip is not
documented anywhere found (search for `betmgm.com/en/sportsapp` returned no
page) and needs a tap on a device.

**Not done.** No change to the app's hand-off yet. That waits on the
probe's result and on Matt's device check (is the BetMGM app installed;
does a long-press on a `sports.<state>.betmgm.com` link offer "Open in
BetMGM"). The `NaN` links (betparx, fanatics) are flagged, not fixed.

## 2026-11-11 — A volume setting per pre-game model: one input, the bands beside it

mike: *"give me bands or thresholds or easy ways to tune every model for volume
based on a simple input, maybe through the retool dash"*. Branch
`claude/model-volume-dial`. The design and the rules: `docs/thresholds.md`,
"Volume settings".

**What was built.**
- `data/migrations/model_volume_settings_2026_11_11.sql`: `base_min_prob` /
  `base_min_edge` on `model_action_thresholds` (config's own cut), the tables
  `model_volume_settings` and `model_volume_settings_log` (a trigger writes the
  log), `apply_model_volume_settings()`, the view `model_volume_bands`, and the
  one input `set_model_volume(model, level, who, number, note)`. Nothing is
  granted to the app's roles.
- `data/threshold_sync.py`: writes config's cut into both column pairs and
  calls `apply_model_volume_settings()` before the commit. Before the
  migration has run it is the statement it always was. A failed apply still
  commits config's cuts and any pause, and logs an error.
- `scripts/model_volume_rehearsal.py`: runs the migration, the input and the
  sync on the real picks in one transaction and rolls it back.
- `tests/test_model_volume_settings.py`, 15 tests.

**Where the mechanism came from.** The pre-game producers and the app already
select through `model_action_thresholds` (min_prob, min_edge, min_odds), so a
tighter number there is a tighter board on all three with no change to any of
them. The in-play producers do not read the edge cut
(`tracking/publish_filters.live_publishable_sql`), so the same number would
hide in the app a bet Discord posted. In-play models are therefore listed with
their bands and refused by the input.

**Why a setting acts on posting and not on the model.** If the model stopped
writing the weaker bets, the bands (quartiles of its own bets) would be cut
from the bets that survived the last setting, and each setting would ratchet
the next. Cut from every bet at config's cut, posted or not, they hold still.

**Two things the first version got wrong, found on the data.**
- Bands from the last 120 calendar days with a 40-bet floor gave 10 of 73
  pre-game models a band. Most models bet once or twice a day, and the summer
  sports have no recent bets at all. Now: the model's own last 120 days of
  betting (counted back from its newest bet) and a 20-bet floor, 31 of 73, and
  24 of the 44 that are not paused.
- A band threshold was rounded to four places. Rounded up it can sit above
  the bet that defines the band. It is cut down instead.

**Verified.**
- The rehearsal on production data, rolled back: 41 of 41 checks. Among them:
  the `all` band counts exactly the bets the producers' cut passes for every
  pre-game model that has bet (56 models, 1,924 bets); `top_half` on one model
  takes the producers' count from 162 to 81 and moves no other model; a number
  under config's cut, an in-play model, an unknown model, an empty name and a
  band on a thin model are each refused; the sync keeps the setting, and a
  sync with the apply function missing still lands config's cuts; `all`
  restores config's cut; the log names who set and who removed. A read on a
  new connection afterwards finds no scratch schema.
- 18 deliberate breaks of the migration and the sync: the rehearsal fails on
  each. 34 deliberate breaks: the pytest file fails on each.
- `tests/test_context_budget.py` passes with CLAUDE.md two bytes under the
  always-loaded cap. The §6 pointer was written inside the old bullet's size.

**What the bands show (measured today, in the thresholds doc's table).** The
top half made fewer units than all bets in 9 of the 24 unpaused models with
bands. A setting is a volume choice, and the doc says so.

**The pair rule's branch is inside this one.** Both wait for a merge, and both
edit this log, the follow-ups, the thresholds doc and the code graph, so
whichever merged second would have needed master merged in and the graph
rebuilt before it could merge. `claude/cbb-under-with-big-favourite` is merged
into this branch: one merge of this branch lands both. The code graph was
rebuilt once on the combined tree, all 1,389 groups named.

**A full-suite run was spoiled, and three failing tests it had hidden.** The
pair-rule branch's full suite was still running in this worktree when this
branch was checked out here. pytest had collected the pair branch's tests and
then read this branch's files as each test ran, so its "21 failed" said
nothing about either branch. Both branches were run again, each in a worktree
of its own. Before those finished, the two features' own test files run
together on the combined tree showed three real failures from the pair-rule
work: `tests/test_ncaab_quote_bounds.py` drives the first-half total card
through a connection stub with no `execute`, and the pair rule gave that card
two new reads. Those three sat among the spoiled run's failures. The
harness now answers both reads. The pair-rule session's "419 tests around the
changed files" did not include that file. The rule, now in memory: a running
suite owns its worktree.

**Three readers still count a held-back bet** (`docs/followups.md`): the
can't-both-win check, the pair rule's check and NCAAB agreement sizing read
BET rows without asking whether the row was posted. Nothing changes until a
setting is made. To be fixed before the dashboard panel is published.

**Not done, and why.**
- The dashboard panel. It needs the migration in production, and the branch
  waits for mike's "merge" (`docs/followups.md`).
- In-play models (`docs/followups.md`).
- No setting was made. With none, the migration and the sync change no cut.
## 2026-11-11 — College basketball: no under beside a big favourite's spread bet; NCAABW Live Totals merged

**The halftime total model went live.** mike: *"Merge"*. PR #1247 merged
2026-11-11 20:51:36Z (b7d562f1). `data.view_migrations` applied 110 of 110
and the decisions table was read back (row security on, no anon read);
pollers registered the job at 20:55:58Z and the loop's first beat came at
21:10:16Z ("1 game(s) in play"); thresholds synced at 21:01:42Z (82 models;
the model's row read back: no edge or probability cut, price floor -200, not
paused). No daily limit (mike: *"no limit for now"*); the name stays "NCAABW
Live Totals" (*"ok"*).

**The pair rule.** mike: *"half time unders should not be taken along with
big favorite spreads, like I have seen some men picks where you pick half
time under and a -14 ... ensure we account for that in mens and womens
models."* What he saw were the men's two first-half models: ten games in a
week with a first-half under and a first-half favourite laying 13.5 to 19.5,
both winning in 2. Measured three ways (`docs/sports/ncaab.md`, "An under
beside a big favourite"): with no model the two do come in together about 2
games in 100 less often than unrelated bets when the favourite lays 6 to 15
in the half; on the models' own replays that pair is rare (24 in three
seasons) and unremarkable; and the first-half under has no edge at all once
the favourite lays 6 or more (12 or more: 331 bets, -8.50 units).

Built: `models/cbb_pairs.py` (the pair, asked by all five college basketball
cards and both in-play loops; the later bet is not made) and the first-half
total model's own rule (no under when the favourite lays 12 or more in the
half: it decides fifty minutes before the spread model, so the pair check
alone would have stopped the favourite, the leg that pays). The women's
halftime model got the pair check only: its unders with a favourite laying 20
or more are its best bets (101, +35.23 units).

**Watched failing.** 25 one-line breaks of the rule; 24 failed a test. The
25th was a second, in-memory check of the men's loop's own pass that the
database read already covers (a bet is written before the next decision is
taken); it was removed instead of tested.

**Found on the way, not changed.** The men's first-half spread model bets
big favourites far more this season than in its replay (15 favourite bets
laying 12 or more in eight days against 37 in three seasons). In
`docs/followups.md`.

**Not done.** The first-half total model's build does not replay the new
rule (the replay block in its file is the rule-off record, as before). The
volume controls mike asked for in the same message are a separate change.

## 2026-11-11 — NCAAF first-half and team-total prices graded: nothing clears

The work that followed mike's *"Buy all"* (2026-11-10): the 153,286 bought
quotes, graded. Write-up, grid and every table:
`docs/ncaaf_first_half_team_totals.md`. Harness:
`scripts/ncaaf_search/audit_2026_11/deriv/`.

**Half-time scores first.** None were stored. `ncaaf_plays` held 2025 only,
so two worker jobs pulled the rest from CFBD, free: 2023-2024 (job 1218369,
21:15 to 21:21 UTC) and 2015-2022 (job 1218730, 21:35 to 21:49 UTC). The
table went from 293,383 rows (318 MB) to 2,128,039 (789 MB) and was copied
to mike's PC the same hour. A half-time score is read from the plays two
ways and used only when they agree: 9,261 games over 2015-2025; against
ESPN's quarter scores on 18 Saturdays, 846 of 854 agree. Two traps met on
the way: a maximum over the half is wrong (a score can appear and be taken
back, and play ids are not in order), and pandas 3 keeps a missing number
missing through `astype(str)`, which silently dropped every moneyline row
from a string key until the line was filled first.

**The result.** Every quote on both sides loses the books' margin (-3.7% to
-6.6%), which is the grading check. Five rule families fixed in advance, at
the game-day read and the evening before: a better price at the same line
(against the other books' middle and against Pinnacle alone), a better line,
team totals against the full-game spread and total, and the first half
priced from the full game walk-forward. None returns a profit.

**The cell that looked positive.** On a table fitted on the bought seasons
alone (711 and 1,419 games) the first-half spread read +16.62 units on 301
bets at one cut, interval -6.1% to +18.6%. Fitted on every earlier season
back to 2015 (6,197 to 7,811 games), in three forms fixed before they were
run, the same cut is -21.39, -11.65 and -17.29 units and every cut from 0.00
to 0.08 is negative at both reads. The short table's slope was 0.555; the
long-run slope is 0.605, and two seasons cannot tell them apart.

**Not done.** No model, no threshold, no pick changes. Half-time scores are
still not a stored table (`docs/followups.md`).

**The PR** (mike, the same evening: "open pr"; #1252). **The merge**
(mike, 2026-11-12: "merge 1252"). Master had moved ten commits overnight, so
it was merged in first: both sides' session and backlog entries kept, and
the code graph taken from master and rebuilt on the combined tree (1,310
groups, 298 of them moved by the rebuild and named again: 48 took back the
name they had, 250 were named by hand). The PR check's test list on a clean
copy of the combined tree: 2,609 passed, and the 2 that fail on this PC
fail on master too. The merge was held until the NCAAF moneyline model's
14:05 UTC decision run had finished, so the redeploy could not skip it.

## 2026-11-11 — NCAABW: the halftime total is built, and the research's game state was wrong in 54 bets

The build that followed mike's *"why no live or ML / spread models for
NCAABW?"*. Evidence and tables: `docs/sports/ncaabw.md`, "In play: the
halftime total".

**What was built.** `ncaabw_live_total_ht`: the full-game total, bet once a
game during the halftime break at EV >= 0.05.
- `models/ncaabw_live_ht.py`: the state, the model, the eligibility rule and
  the one decision function the build and the loop share.
- `data/ingestors/ncaabw_pbp.py`: women's play-by-play through one parser,
  the season files for history and ESPN's summary live. 5,013,067 plays of
  11,894 games (2024-25 to 2026-11-09) are now in Supabase (`ncaabw_pbp`,
  `ncaabw_pbp_games`); before today they sat in a cache on one PC.
- `scripts/build_ncaabw_live_ht_artifact.py` and
  `models/saved/ncaabw_live_ht_2027.json`.
- `data/ingestors/ncaabw_live_feed.py`, `models/ncaabw_live_loop.py`, the
  ledger `ncaabw_live_decisions`, job `ncaabw_live` on pollers.
- Config, the edge cap, the open-picks view's name for it, and the app (Live
  board, the model's page, the pick's timing line).

**The finding that changed the numbers.** The build first reproduced the
research bet for bet on the loop's own code (689 bets, +66.59 units; 303,
+44.96). Then the bets it claimed above 0.70 were read one by one: halftime
scores of 2-2 and 10-6 against totals of 122.5 and 171.5. ESPN logs
corrections late, with a new wallclock and the score of the moment the play
belongs to, and "the last play logged" had been read as the state. 54 of the
689 bets of 2025-26 carried a wrong score: 24-30, -10.71 units, against
374-261, +77.30 on the rest. The state now takes the highest score logged so
far. On that reading: 669 bets, 398-271, +89.96 units, +11.84% [+5.1, +18.1]
walk-forward; 298 bets, +42.10, +12.35% [+0.6, +23.7] on the second look;
every cut from 0.02 to 0.12 positive in both; the checks hold (blind sides
lose, turned round -24.6% and -25.3%, another game's decision -4.1% and
-7.0%); and the claimed chance is honest to 0.70. The research script run on
the same reading agrees to the bet.

**Watched failing.** 31 one-line breaks of the new code (the running maximum,
each eligibility check, the header check, the late-play check, each gate, the
ledger's finality, the read times, the registration) each fail a test in
`tests/test_ncaabw_live_ht.py` or `tests/test_ncaabw_live_loop.py`. One was
not caught at first: dropping "stamped after the half ended" was masked by
the freshness check, because the synthetic half ended on a basket. The
synthetic game got a scoreless last two minutes for that test.

**The halftime spread, read again on the corrected state.** +8.48% [-1.9,
+18.2] on 371 bets walk-forward, +9.75% [+1.8, +17.7] on 516 over both
seasons, 11-17 this season, and its claimed chance is not honest between 0.62
and 0.70. Still not built.

**The app, after the UX review** (four should-fix, two to consider, all
applied): the model is named "NCAABW Live Totals" / "LIVE O/U", not
"Halftime", because a halftime bet means a second-half bet to a bettor; Pick
Detail says the bet is on the full game and that its price was read shortly
before the time shown; the live-pricing note names the sport; the list of
in-play sports ends "NCAAB and NCAABW" and is spoken by name. The app's type
check prints the same 48 lines on master and on the branch, and every verify
script fails the same lines on both (none from this work).

**Judgement calls, for mike to reverse if he wants.** The name above. No
daily count (he was asked and has not answered; the default was stated). A halftime bet that
cannot win beside the game's pre-game total bet is declined (24 of 967 in the
replay, which lost 5.75 units). No cap on a claimed chance above 0.70 (27
games, +3.16 units). The loop waits the men's measured ESPN lag, so a bet
posts about two and a half minutes after its price was read.

**Master moved under the branch** (88 files: three other sessions' merges).
It was merged in; four files carried both sides' text at one place and both
were kept; the open-picks view's marker was recomputed from its merged body.
On the merged tree the build's replays still equal their pinned numbers. Full
suite: 17 failed, 11,906 passed. Sixteen fail the same way on a clean copy of
master (`docs/followups.md` lists them). The seventeenth was this work's: the
live-sports module had gained an import that a mobile test's sandbox does not
copy; the sentence now takes a naming function instead, and that test passes.
One women's test that master's book-list change had broken was re-pointed.
Master moved once more an hour later (two app changes, #1245 and #1246) and
was merged in again; the app's type check and its verify scripts were
compared with that master too, and print the same lines.

**Not done.** The first live decision has not been seen (no deploy yet: the
branch is not merged). ESPN's lag on the women's game is not measured. The
build reads the research cache, not the database.

## 2026-11-11 — NCAABW: nothing clears on the spread or moneyline; the in-play total is a halftime total and passes its checks

mike, 2026-11-11: *"why no live or ML / spread models for NCAABW?"* Two pieces
of research answered it. The tables are in `docs/sports/ncaabw.md`,
"Research"; this is what was done and what it means.

**The totals model went live first** (not recorded before, because the last
entry was written ahead of the merge). PR #1236 merged 2026-11-10 14:05:56Z
on mike's "merge"; worker and pollers both deployed; thresholds synced and
the two view changes applied and read back; the card's first worker run at
14:09Z read 415 games with no DraftKings total posted; its first two
decisions came at 14:44Z, three minutes after DraftKings' stamp, both below
the cut. The app shows the sport as "NCAABW" (mike: "leave it"), and Bally Bet
and Fliff stay out of the app's sportsbook picker (mike: "get rid of bally and
fliff").

**Spread and moneyline off the team ratings** (`scripts/ncaabw_research/sides.py`).
The totals model's ratings give a home margin; it was blended with
DraftKings' first posted spread the way the total is. The line already holds
it: the share of the model's disagreement that comes true is -0.03 and -0.05
by season, against 1.05 and 0.46 for the total. No spread cut has more than 2
bets above an edge of 0.04, and below that it loses (749 bets, -40.73 units
at any edge, each season on the other's numbers). Eight ratings settings were
tried: with less shrinkage the model beats the line in 2024-25 and loses to
it in 2025-26, so every walk-forward run loses or is flat. The moneyline from
the same margin is long underdogs and loses (302 bets, -8.97 units at 0.02).
No price band is positive in both seasons either. The sign of the stored
spread was checked on finals before any table was read (LSU -73.5 at home to
Alabama State, final 109-41).

**The in-play second-half total is a halftime total**
(`scripts/ncaabw_research/inplay_checks.py`). "The first second-half snapshot
holding an eligible quote" sits in the halftime break in 1,039 of 1,055 games
of 2025-26: the state is the halftime score and the quote is a median 2.2
minutes old. Break only, EV at or above 0.05: 689 bets, +66.59 units, +8.50%
[+1.6, +15.4] walk-forward; 303 bets, +44.96 units, +12.97% [+1.7, +24.2] on
the second look; every cut from 0.02 to 0.12 positive in both. The four
checks it had not had: blind overs make +0.03% and blind unders lose 10.37%
at the same snapshots, so the side alone is not it; the rule's bets turned
round lose 21.63%; a placebo that gives each game another game's decision
loses 5.41%; the return shows no pattern by quote age and is not a
just-after-a-score effect; and the same side at the same book's next
snapshot, five minutes on, still makes +4.70% and +8.72%. It is honest about
its chances up to a claimed 0.62 and far too sure above 0.70.

**The halftime spread does not clear**: 398 bets, +6.35% [-3.5, +15.7]
walk-forward, with claimed-EV bands that do not rise and 12-16 this season.

**A method note.** Every printed interval now has its own seeded resample,
so a line's interval does not depend on what was printed before it. The
first drafts of these tables moved in the first decimal between runs for
that reason.

**Not done.** The halftime total is not live. It needs a halftime loop on
the worker (ESPN's halftime score, a price read on the history's five-minute
marks, the grade's own decision code), its model file, its config, its
settlement and its place on the app's live board. That build is next.

spread, -130 to -100, late kickoff, 12+ rest days): the three 2026 bets read

DET -2.5, SF -8.5, each -110). The row's game day read only "Sun": `gameDay`
cut "Sun, 11/8, 1:00 PM ET" at its first comma.

("Sun, 11/8", built from `formatToParts`; `withYear` adds "/yy").

and drops the "Official <lock time>" line and its VoiceOver text. The
Official/Closing toggle and its explainer are unchanged. UX review: ship with
fixes; date-first, the comma, and the season-scoped year were taken.

checks the screen; it passes on the branch and fails on master. Full suite:
see the PR for the failing-set comparison against master (scratch-Postgres
tests cannot start as root in this sandbox). tsc not run: mobile

when the official line was taken.
