- NBA did-not-play rows are read from `nba_player_dnp` (1,199 rows on
  2026-11-11) and merged into the loaded date span. The anon SELECT policy is
  `data/migrations/anon_read_nba_player_dnp_2026_11_11.sql` and is not applied.
  WNBA and NCAAB have no did-not-play table; their ingestors drop those
  athletes, so nothing is invented.
- H2H and defense-tier games outside the last 50 are asked from that sport's
  game log by game id, or by date when the head-to-head row has no game id.
- NFL, NCAAF, and MLB are unchanged. NHL player detail is still closed.

Verified: `verify_player_log.ts` (new cases for the threshold, DNP, average,
median, and sport gating), the other mobile verify scripts' failure set matches
master, tsc has no diagnostic in the touched files, `ux_scan` 0 findings. The
UX review's four should-fixes were applied. Scrolling the outlined row into
view was declined: the caption already names the game, and the list sits under
several cards. No EAS build or update. JS only, runtimeVersion policy
`appVersion` (1.3.0), so an OTA can carry it.

## 2026-11-10 — NBA All tab shows every scored game, not only bets

Matt (screenshot, NBA: All (7) = Signals (7)): *"NBA all tab should have all
bets. Not just signals."* The app was right: the All tab draws every row in
`picks`, and every NBA model since 2026-10-20 had written BET rows only (0
NONE / AVOID rows; NHL and NCAAB write hundreds). On 2026-11-09 the three
game-total cards scored 8 games each and logged 18 no-bet decisions to their
worker-only ledgers, which the app never reads. Matt chose "game models, full
card"; props stay bet-only for now.

- `scripts/nba_none_rows.py` (new): a scored, un-bet decision -> a NONE row at
  the best offer on the model's side, no stake, `model_probability` = the
  market-fair number (no uplift; the uplift is the gap bucket's), or the bet's
  own claim when a floor or the one-total-a-game door dropped a bet. Games
  declined for missing inputs or with no priced offer get no row.
- `models/nba_total_luck.Decision` now carries the best over offer (book,
  line, price, p_fair, p_model), as the team-total and first-half decisions
  already did.
- The three cards' `publish` write the NONE row in the same transaction as
  the decision; the ledger's `n_bets` still counts BETs only.
- Survives to tip: the scorer's no-bet clear has touched only its own models'
  rows since 2026-11-06 (`_pass_rewritten_models`). Settlement grades BETs
  only, as for NCAAB's NONE rows.

Verified: 9 tests (3 new full-game, 6 updated team-total / first-half) fail
on the code without the change and pass with it. Full suite compared with
origin/master below. Not live until the worker redeploys from master; first
NONE rows land on the next NBA slate after merge.

## 2026-11-10 — NCAAF moneyline model built live (`ncaaf_moneyline_market`); it did not clear and is mostly road underdogs

mike, 2026-11-10, asked whether he wanted a moneyline model live although
nothing in the audit cleared: *"ML model yes"*. Real money, like every model
(CLAUDE.md §2). Reference: `docs/sports/ncaaf.md`, "The NCAAF moneyline
model".

**What it is.** The best deciding-book moneyline against the win chance the
ten books' consensus spread implies, decided once a game at 14:00 UTC on its
Eastern date (9am ET in November), bet when the edge is above 0.02 with at
least three other books in the consensus, never shorter than -200. It is the
audit's research rule ported, not improved: `models/ncaaf_moneyline_market.py`
(the rule), `scripts/ncaaf_moneyline_card.py` (the card, ledger
`ncaaf_moneyline_decisions`), `scripts/ncaaf_moneyline_market_build.py` (the
frozen 2026 numbers and the replay). The paused classifier `ncaaf_moneyline`
is untouched.

**The acceptance test.** The build replays 2023-2025 through the card's own
decision function and matches the research's 311 bets game by game: same
game, side, book and price on all 311, largest edge difference 0.0, on the
first run. 122-189, +14.70 units on 188.76 risked; 2023 140 bets -2.77, 2024
78 bets +16.66, 2025 93 bets +0.81.

**What mike was told before it shipped** (the price-range rule, and a fact
the audit reply had left out for this rule): it bets underdogs only, +110 to
+430, median +146, a fifth at +300 or longer; 294 of the 311 are road teams,
the fitted home/away offset at work. With the offset at zero the same cut is
212 bets, -9.22 units; every road underdog priced +110 to +430 at its best
price was 813 bets, +6.94 units; without its 10 best dates the rule is -9.3
units. He was offered "drop the ML model" and did not take it.

**Read on the running system before the PR.** The card run read-only against
production as of 14:05 UTC on 2026-11-10 (`run_card(do_publish=False,
now=...)`): three games, each priced off a ten-book consensus from quotes
stamped 13:16-13:17 UTC, all below the cut (best edges +0.0047, +0.0013,
+0.0007), nothing written. The hourly refresh stores every book's NCAAF
moneyline and spread at :16-:17 past each hour (63-64 of 64 listed games per
book that morning), so the quotes a 14:00 UTC decision reads are about 43
minutes old.

**Not done, and why.** The two-bets-on-one-game rule is one-way: the card
skips a game the opening-spread models have bet, and the scorer does not yet
check this model's bet before writing a spread bet (those two models have
bet once all season). No forward record exists; gate standing 0 settled of
50.

## 2026-11-10 — NCAAF after the audit: first-half and team-total prices bought, the totals fit averaged over eleven seeds, a weather hold

mike answered the audit's decisions the same day: *"1) Buy all 2) yes 3) ML
model yes"*, then, on the question the refit raised, *"yes wait for a
weather forecast"*. This entry is the first two; the moneyline model is its
own entry. Reference: `docs/sports/ncaaf.md`, the two 2026-11-10 sections.

**The purchase** (`data/ingestors/ncaaf_derivative_history.py`, job type
`ncaaf_derivative_history`). First-half moneyline, spread and total and the
team totals for every 2023-2025 NCAAF game that holds a stored full-game
pull, at the two times those pulls were taken (14:00 UTC on the game's
Eastern date, 23:00 UTC the evening before), stored in `odds` under
`odds_api_ncaaf_derivative_am` / `_pm` with a ledger
(`ncaaf_derivative_fetches`) so nothing is bought twice.

- The plan, read from production by the tool's dry run: 2,510 games on 180
  dates in the morning read, 2,435 on 186 the evening before, at most 198,166
  credits. The audit had said 185,530: it counted games with a final only.
- A 50-game sample first (1,933 credits): 49 held a quote, 1,484 rows.
  First-half moneyline on 46 of the 50, first-half spread 48, first-half
  total 48, team totals 48. One game read by hand beside its full-game pull
  of the same minute: first-half spread +6.5 against a full-game +12, total
  24.5 against 47.5.
- The full buy was started detached at 17:20 ET from its own copy of the code
  (`C:\Users\micha\ncaaf_research\buy_wt`, no `.env` in it; the variables
  were passed to the process), balance 11,920,961, floor 596,043 (seven days
  of the measured burn).
- One read of mine timed out and is the reason for a note here: `odds` has no
  index on `source`. Count bought rows through the ledger's game ids.
- Not done: half and quarter scores are still not stored, so a first-half
  market cannot be graded yet. CFBD's game records carry them.

**The totals refit** (`models/seed_average.py`,
`scripts/ncaaf_margin_eval.py`, artifact
`models/saved/ncaaf_over_under_20261110_161059.pkl`). The fit is the mean of
eleven seeds, fitted offline from the frame cached that morning; fitting no
longer registers, and `--register-totals VERSION` is its own step for after
the deploy. The walk-forward reproduces the audit to the game: 235-191 at
the gate on 2022-2025 against 244-230 for seed 42 alone.

**What the refit found.** A game with no weather forecast is served with its
three weather inputs missing. One training game in 7,570 has no weather, so a
fit never learns what that means: the August fit reads such a game 1.89
points low, the eleven-seed fit 11.66 low (547 of 768 holdout games at the
under gate). Re-measured here on the 2025 and 2026 frames with both
artifacts before it was taken to mike. So the refit ships with a hold: the
totals model does not decide a game until its forecast exists
(`models/scorer.totals_weather_hold`), and registration refuses the artifact
without it. A dome is served the dome's own weather and is not held.

**What the hold changes in practice** (read-only SQL on `picks`): the 9 kept
totals bets placed more than 7 days before kickoff this season went 4-5,
-1.55 units, and none has been placed that early since the schedule hold of
2026-10-20.

**Not done, and why.** The artifact is not registered: that happens after
the merge deploys and the worker is seen to load the file. The forecast step
was not added to the hourly pass (a game first priced mid-day waits for the
next morning). The loader still re-reads the artifact for every game, about
half a second each with the larger file.

## 2026-11-10 — NCAAF market audit: nothing new clears, the totals fit is noisier than its record, NCAAF keeps its price path

mike, 2026-11-10: *"Do a full audit of NCAAF markets - I dont see spread or ML
models or other markets firing; what optimizations can be made to maximize
units but also not too much crazy volume ... use what you have learned by
building recent models."* The write-up, with every table, is
`docs/ncaaf_market_audit.md`. This entry is how it was done and what to trust.

**How it was read.** Everything came off the local copy of the database
(`betting_local_copies\supabase_mirror`, parquet), not production: 3,170,356
NCAAF `odds` rows, 531,456 college prop rows, games, picks, play-by-play and
the live state log, pulled once with pyarrow into a cache outside the repo
(`scripts/ncaaf_search/audit_2026_11/paths.py`). Production was read three
times: the repo's frame builder twice (the totals walk-forward and the 2026
frame) and small read-only SQL for the 2026 records.

**What was run.**

- *The four NCAAB market rules on 2023-2025* (`lab.py`): spread, total and
  moneyline line shopping against the consensus, and each book's moneyline
  against its own spread, with `models/ncaab_margin_dist.py` unchanged and
  walk-forward tables. Eight reads a game (the morning and evening pulls, game
  day back to five days out), the game-day morning read named as primary
  before any result.
- *The check that mattered* (`spread_dd.py`, `sym.py`). The spread rule read
  +29.4 units on 338 bets. The breakdown by side showed 337 road teams. Three
  controls followed: every road team at the consensus (-4.4%), the same table
  with each side set equal at the consensus line (22 bets at 0.02), and a
  version with no table at all (a number a point better than the consensus
  lost 8.8%). A ported rule is checked for side balance before its return is
  read.
- *The totals fit under 11 random seeds* (`totals_seeds.py`,
  `totals_seeds_fc.py`). Found while reproducing the published walk-forward:
  today's refit under seed 42 reads 51.5% at the stored close where the
  record says 55.9%. Same frame, same training rows (5,259 / 6,023 / 6,798, as
  the 2026-09-07 entry lists), a newer xgboost. The seed alone moves a game's
  prediction 1.49 points.
- *Two background studies*, each with definitions fixed first and a placebo:
  college props on 2026 and 2025 (648 cells), and a market-anchored in-play
  game-state model fitted on 2025 and read on 2026.
- *Every scored 2026 pre-game row graded* (`grade_2026.py`), because the
  graded-universe view holds no NCAAF rows.

**What was changed.** `data/prune_odds.PATH_KEPT_SPORTS` gains NCAAF: a
settled 2026 game held a median of one spread row at each book but
DraftKings, so no 2026 forward read of a cross-book rule was possible (the
spread rule formed 11 bets on the whole season). Tests: the pinned tuple test
and a new NCAAF thinning test, both watched failing before the change.
The audit's scripts are committed. No model, cut, pause or stake was changed.

**Tests.** Full suite on the branch in the main checkout: 23 failed, 11,521
passed, 107 skipped. Thirteen are master's known failures; the other ten
fail only in a checkout that has other checkouts nested in it, and pass or
skip on clean copies of master (153 passed) and of the branch (154 passed,
the one more being the new NCAAF thinning test). `docs/followups.md` lists
them.

**What was deliberately not done.**

- No spread or moneyline model was built: nothing measured clears, and
  switching one on regardless is mike's call.
- The totals artifact was not refitted as a seed average. It is a model
  update, and 2026 does not separate the average from a single fit.
- No credits were spent. The first-half and team-total purchase is sized in
  the write-up (at most 92,765 credits a read) and waits for mike.
- The graded-universe view's missing NCAAF rows and the in-play score log's
  75-second lag behind DraftKings are flagged, not fixed.

**Read before trusting it.** The 2026 totals replay is built from the
season-end feature rows and shares 47 of 94 games with the bets really placed,
so it is a second draw of the rule and not a re-grade. The in-play "best five
a night" result (+12.8% on 109 bets) is one half-season. The props and in-play
studies were run by background agents and the numbers quoted are theirs:
`docs/ncaaf_market_audit_props.md` and `docs/ncaaf_market_audit_inplay.md`,
scripts under the audit folder's `props/` and `inplay/`. Two cells of each
were re-read here against their printed output; neither was re-derived.

## 2026-11-10 — NCAABW totals model built to go live; five things a copy of the men's card got wrong

mike, 2026-11-09, after the foundations report: *"merge the branch and
continue"*. PR #1235 (the foundations) was merged and both services deployed.
This entry is the first live women's model, `ncaabw_total_blend`, on branch
`claude/ncaabw-totals`. The reference is `docs/sports/ncaabw.md` "The totals
model"; this is how it got there.

**After the merge.** Worker and pollers both deployed SUCCESS (04:18Z and
04:17Z). The worker's first women's capture tick logged `events=3 started=3
inserted=0` and exited 0. The women's schedule for the next 14 days was
loaded from this PC (758 games).

**The box feed** (`data/ingestors/ncaabw_espn_box.py`, step `ncaabw-box` on
the hourly passes). A fork of the men's. A women's game is four quarters, so
a regulation game must show four periods, overtime is a fifth, and the game's
length is 40 minutes plus 5 for each period past the fourth
(`ncaabw_teams.regulation_periods`; the men's rule would have refused every
women's game as an overtime mismatch). No player box. First real run, the
2026-27 backfill: 525 finals due, 519 stored, 6 refused (5 where the box's
made shots do not add up to the final, 1 with no shooting numbers); 2,085
period rows, four a game and nine overtime periods.

**The model, the card, the build** (`models/ncaabw_total_blend.py`,
`scripts/ncaabw_total_card.py`, `scripts/ncaabw_total_blend_build.py`,
`models/ncaabw_quote_bounds.py`). The men's method and settings unchanged.
The build reproduces the research to the bet: 402 bets, 225-177, +29.96 units
for 2025-26 on numbers fitted on 2024-25; 453 and +55.76 for each season on
the other's blend; 374 and +75.73 on the blend it runs on. It then replays
the card's own decision code on the 903 priced games of 2025-26: 402 bets,
the same games and sides, none held, none refused. The first build was one
bet off (401). The cause: it dropped box rows before 2015-16, so the first
season's shooting reference was its own league rate, not the season
before's. One season of box rows before the first season of games is kept.

**Five things the copy got wrong**, each found by running the fork on the
stored women's tables, each now with a test that was watched failing:

1. *Every team would have been pooled into one.* The model pools a team with
   fewer than 10 games on the season's stored schedule. The men's schedule is
   stored whole (6,009 games to 2027-03-07); the women's held 1,245 to
   2026-11-23. `box_coverage` printed `sched 7/7` for one game's two teams
   and that was the tell. The season was loaded (4,524 more games; 5,769 to 2027-03-06; 363
   Division I teams, fewest 25). The card now stops a game with a Division I
   team under 10 stored games and fails the run (`schedule_short`).
2. *Both box sources would have been counted.* NCAA's rows carry ESPN's game
   id in 2023-24 to 2025-26 only and on none of 2026-27's 958 rows, so the
   men's key (ESPN's id, else the contest id) keeps four rows of every
   current game. `place_box` puts a row with no id on its game by date, teams
   and score, and keeps one source per game. On the stored 2026-27 rows:
   1,996 in, 1,048 kept, every one on a game, 524 of 526 finals with a box.
   The doc's SQL reader was keyed the men's way and is removed; the test that
   ran it now runs `place_box`.
3. *No November blend.* 218 priced November games, under the 300 a fit needs.
   The men's code would have marked every November game `no_blend` and failed
   every run until December. `blend_for` falls back to the every-game fit.
4. *The men's ranges.* Totals 90 to 200, spreads to 85 either way, edge cap
   0.30 (largest replay edge 0.2076).
5. *Agreement sizing.* Removed; one women's model, flat stake.

**Two lines of yesterday's research were wrong and are corrected.** Both
quoted the first week of 2026-27 (the rating model "6.5 points low", and "30
bets at 0.03, 20-10, +9.03 units"). Both were computed from the 487 played
games only, so every team was pooled (item 1). Through the card's own reads
with the whole schedule: 42 priced finals; 14 bets at 0.05, 7-7, -0.75 units;
22 at 0.03, 10-12, -3.25.

**A second box source, daily** (`ncaabw_sdv_import.refresh_season`, step
`ncaabw-ncaa-box` in the daily pipeline). NCAA's file held 5 of the 6 finals
ESPN's box was refused for. It stores only contests not stored yet. Run once
against production: 958 rows in the file, 0 new.

**Wiring.** Config (cut 0.05, EV floor 0.07, price floor -200, own
probability, rule model), the scheduler job `ncaabw_total_card` at minute 4
of every five (UTC; a minute after the men's card, three after the women's
capture), its 20-minute silence bound, the decisions table worker-only,
settlement as a full-game total, the line-movement check's market map, the
closing-line market map (comment bumped so production re-creates the
function), the Discord emoji and the X tag. The worker holds
`DISCORD_CHANNEL_NCAABW` and the bot token (variable names read 2026-11-10).

**Checked against production, read-only.** A dry run of the card read 415
slate games and decided none (no DraftKings women's total was stored yet for
them). Every one of the 5,769 stored 2026-27 games, both sides, produces a
bet row whose label passes the publishers' own check (683 team names, 71 with
digits or punctuation).

**Tests the foundations merge broke, found only now.** The full suite's
second half had not run before #1235 merged. Four tests on master pinned the
men's-only lists that PR extended (the DraftKings-gate exemption, the
schedule-resolved sports, the default pass's last sport, the price-path
sports). The behaviour is intended; the pins are updated here. Five more
fail on master from before that PR and are not this work's:
`test_nhl_totals_card::test_insert_once_under_the_model_lock`,
`test_picks_unique_row::test_the_card_insert_tolerates_the_unique_index`,
`test_price_prefilter_rollback` (two) and
`test_web_site::test_record_math_matches_the_node_suite`.

**The app** (`mobile/src`, 18 files). NCAABW is a sport chip after NCAAB; the
model is labelled "NCAABW Totals"; the Today board reads it 7 days ahead
through one read shared with the men's sport; Stats, Live and Today say in
words that the sport has one pre-game model and no stats. `startsWith('ncaab')`
matched the women's ids in both places a model is filed under a sport; the
women's prefix is tested first and a test fails if a third prefix test
appears. Bally Bet and Fliff moved to the reference-only book list for every
sport (the feed dropped both on 2026-09-20; the only newer prices are the
women's; 0 of 28 stored book sets held only those two). The open-picks view
names the model with the app's label and its marker is re-stamped.

**The UX review** (`frontend-ux-designer`, twice; Mobbin was not connected).
Verdict both times: ship with fixes, no blocker. Applied: a spoken name
("Women's college basketball") wherever a control speaks the sport (the chip
row, the Stats sport button and sheet, the daily recap); women's-only wording
for the Today, Live and Stats empty states; "the pick is for the total and
price shown, not a later one" on the model page and the pick screen; two
comments. Not applied: showing "NCAAW" in place of "NCAABW" (mike, 2026-11-10: "leave
it"). He approved taking Bally Bet and Fliff out of the app the same morning
("get rid of bally and fliff") and said "merge".
Closed by a count: a re-prompt for members whose only books were Bally Bet or
Fliff (there are none). Logged in `docs/followups.md`: ReBet is still
selectable with no prices since 2026-09-20, and the look-ahead read sits 40
rows under the response cap. The scan's one finding (`format.ts`, a date
helper) is on a line master already has. The type check's error set is
unchanged, with nothing in a touched file.

**The odds history store finished** (01:10 ET): 49,527 snapshots in the
ledger; 147,268 pre-game rows in `odds`, the spool's own count game by game
on 2,448 games, so the dropped connection wrote nothing twice; 772,377
in-play rows against 773,059 counted, because in 28 snapshots the feed listed
an event twice and the table keeps the first listing.

**The whole suite on the branch** (2026-11-10, 1:40:36 on this machine): 14
failed, 11,510 passed, 126 skipped. All 14 fail on master too. Twelve fail by
themselves there: the seven known on 2026-11-09 and the five named above. Two
fail only after the tests that reload the scheduler have run
(`test_social_posts::test_the_new_job_does_not_move_the_existing_schedules`,
`test_social_profile::test_the_five_minute_job_applies_the_profile_and_keeps_its_trigger`):
one of those leaves the scheduler in the pollers role. Run in that order on
master at 93b032e3 they fail the same way; run alone they pass on both.
`docs/followups.md` lists the fourteen.

**Not done.** The in-play second-half total's four checks. Importing ESPN's
2022-24 odds. A schedule loader that needs no person. The build still reads
the research cache on this PC. `docs/followups.md` has each.

## 2026-11-09 — women's college basketball (NCAABW): the foundations, the odds history, the first research

mike: *"Take everything we learned about building the NCAAB models and now
build models for the womens NCAABW - ensure all learnings are incorporated,
all odds API buys are auto approved"*. The full write-up is
`docs/sports/ncaabw.md`.

**What the sources hold** (each read off the source that day). The odds feed's
women's history starts 2024-11-12: two finished seasons, 2,293 listed events,
a median 7 a game day, twelve books, no Pinnacle, and no first-half, quarter,
team-total, alternate or player line (17 past games asked). ESPN's endpoints
and sportsdataverse's season files mirror the men's. So the men's two
first-half models have no women's twin, and the men's four odds purchases
became one.

**Built** (branch `claude/ncaabw-foundations`):

- Identity and schedule: `ncaabw_teams.py`, `ncaabw_espn_ingestor.py`, forks of
  the men's on their own tables (ESPN gives a school's women's team its men's
  team id). 61,431 games, 2015-16 to 2026-11-08, written from this machine.
- The odds feed wired for NCAABW in `odds_ingestor.py` (resolve-only, exempt
  from the DraftKings gate, seven extra books stored for this sport only), the
  five-minute capture, the two pipeline steps, retention, worker-only tables.
- The odds history purchase, `ncaabw_odds_history.py`: 49,527 snapshots,
  2,971,620 credits, fetched to a spool and stored to Supabase (pre-game
  change-only rows in `odds`, in-play rows in `ncaabw_inplay_quotes`).
- NCAA box scores 2010 to 2027 (`ncaabw_sdv_import.py`): 188,710 team rows.
- The research scripts, `scripts/ncaabw_research/`.

**Four things a straight copy of the men's code got wrong**, each found by
running it, not by reading it:

1. Overtime. The men's rule (ESPN's period past 2) would have marked every
   women's game as overtime; the women's game is four quarters. Stored
   overtime share is now 3.9% to 4.6% a season.
2. The sport key. Renaming `ncaab` to `ncaabw` turned the feed's
   `basketball_wncaab` into `basketball_wncaabw`, and every call answered 404
   "Unknown sport". Caught on a three-call test buy; pinned by a test.
3. Team names. 27 feed names placed no team (the feed names women's teams by
   the men's mascot, and drops ESPN's "Lady"). All 353 names now place.
4. A clock grid. The feed keeps no snapshot while nothing moves and bills an
   ask for an empty time as the snapshot before it (60 credits each, asked
   four times). The purchase walks the feed's own chain instead.

**Three things about this machine**: writing a season of games an event at a
time took hours from a laptop (the men's ingestor only ever ran beside the
database), so a day's new events are written together, 8 minutes a season; a
new connection per paid call kept four processes CPU-bound, so each thread
keeps one; and a status check that read the purchase's state files made
Windows refuse their replacement 15 times, so that write retries.

**The research** (`docs/sports/ncaabw.md`, "Research"):

- **Totals pass.** The men's totals model, settings unchanged, at DraftKings'
  first posted total. 2025-26 walk-forward: +4.6% to +10.6% across cuts 0.03
  to 0.08 on 516 to 276 bets, each interval's lower bound between -3.7 and
  -0.1. Both seasons, each on the other's blend: +9.6% to +12.7%, every lower
  bound above zero. On ESPN's stored lines for 2022-23 and 2023-24 the rating
  model's error is lower than the line's.
- **The men's spread and moneyline market rules fail** on the women's boards.
- **In play, a first look**: the second-half total passes, the checks the
  men's models had to pass are not done.

**Verified.** The women's tests pass (319 across five files), and each new
test was watched failing with its fix removed. The whole suite is 11,513
tests and takes over two hours on this machine while the purchase runs. At
5,768 tests it had shown nine failing tests. Two were this branch's and are
fixed: literal table names in the purchase's DDL, and a lost-ledger guard
that reads the source marker back. The other seven fail the same way on a

## 2026-11-09 — NHL derivative totals re-graded; how five totals models price a push

mike: *"yes check how price a push"*.

**The re-grade** (`nhl-derivative-totals-regrade-2026-11-09`, done 19:40Z)
clears nothing. The tables are at the top of `docs/nhl_derivative_totals.md`;
the follow-up is closed.

**The push check.** None of the five totals models that bet whole-number unders
since 2026-08-01 gives the push to the under:
- `mlb_total_public_fade`: its probability is the under price's own implied
  number (`scripts/mlb_total_public_fade_card.py`); nothing is modelled.
- `mlb_over_under` (paused): trained with pushes dropped
  (`features/feature_engine.py` returns None on a push), so both sides are
  chances given no push.
- `nhl_over_under`: Pinnacle's no-vig two-way, both sides given no push. The
  module already states true EV = (1 − p_push) x stated EV, same sign.
- `ncaab_first_half_total`: P(over | no push) from its kernel table, both
  sides given no push (module docstring).
- `nfl_wind_totals`: the calibrated under rate counts a push as half a win
  (`nfl/scripts/calibrate_lead.py`, `under = 0.5` on a push), and the EV
  treats the rest as a loss, so its EV is slightly UNDER-stated (about
  0.045 x the push share at −110).
For the three "given no push" models the edge's sign is exact (a push returns
the stake, so EV > 0 iff P(win | no push) beats the price), and the stated EV
on a whole line is high by the push share. Pushes on their whole-line under
bets since 2026-08-01: `mlb_over_under` 3 of 15, `nhl_over_under` 0 of 6,
`ncaab_first_half_total` 0 of 2. No change made.

## 2026-11-08 — Back button: iOS's own arrow, no custom label, on every pushed screen

**Why.** Matt, 2026-11-07, with a Player Stats screenshot: he moved Shai Gilgeous-Alexander's line from
25 to 30, added it, and Back stopped working. It is the third report of the symptom. The first two were on
Settings after a picker had been opened (2026-10-31, 2026-11-06). Matt approved the fix ("Fix").

**What the freeze trail showed.** The first `app_errors` row the 2026-11-06 tracking caught for it:
`restart`, 2026-11-07 23:54:47Z, iOS 27.2, app 1.3.0, screen PlayerStats, `touches_since_nav` 19,
`open_modals` []. The trail shows PlayerStats opened, then AddLineSheet (Compare odds) opened, `modal_shown`,
closed and `modal_dismissed`. Both iOS confirmations arrived. Then 8 taps with no screen change over about 11
seconds, then the app left the foreground and was relaunched. There was no stuck pop-up and no JS stall. So
the JS side was alive and nothing covered the screen. The native Back refused the pop. All three reports
were on iOS 27.2, each after a pop-up on the screen.

**What changed.** `mobile/App.tsx`: the root `Stack.Navigator` sets `headerBackButtonDisplayMode: 'minimal'`,
and every `headerBackTitle` is gone (18 in App.tsx, plus `'My models'` in `ByomDetailScreen`). That is the
workaround for Apple's reported iOS 27 bug, a Back button with a custom title going unresponsive (forum thread
846016, cited 2026-10-31, not re-read). From react-native-screens 4.16.0 `RNSScreenStackHeaderConfig.mm` and
native-stack 7.15.1 `useHeaderConfigProps.tsx`, both read this session: with no custom font and the menu
enabled, minimal mode passes `backButtonDisplayMode` and creates no custom `backBarButtonItem`. Removing the
titles also stops the custom `backButtonTitle`. Every pushed screen's Back is now the arrow alone. A long press
lists the earlier screen by its own title. Guard: `tests/test_mobile_back_button.py`, which fails on
origin/master (both tests) and passes on the branch.
The `Tabs` root now has a title that follows the focused tab ("Picks", "Record", ...). Without it the
long-press Back menu would read "Tabs". The UX review inferred that and it was not seen on a device. `tsc`
error set byte-identical to origin/master (27, 0 in touched files). UX review: ship. ux_scan found 0 issues,
and Mobbin returned Apple Sports with an arrow-only Back.

**Not verified.** Whether this ends the freeze. It cannot be reproduced without an iPhone on iOS 27. It ships
over the air. If it happens again, the next `restart` row on that screen says so. The react-native-screens
upgrade was NOT done. The evidence for it is one reporter's comment on react-navigation #12778 ("appears to be
fixed with React Native Screens 4.17.x"), on a different setup (navigator Groups, iOS 26). The 4.17.0 and
4.17.1 release notes do not mention a Back fix. Expo 54 pins `~4.16.0`, and the upgrade needs a TestFlight
build. Left for Matt.

## 2026-11-07 — NCAAB in-play models live: early live spread, late live spread, late live total; the best few a night

**Why.** mike asked whether there is an NCAAB live model; there was none. Task: research, backtest and, if
it clears the bar, ship one. On the volume: *"3-5 best per night"*. On the purchase: *"1) yes 2) now"*,
then *"half hour"*.

**Data built (all in Supabase).**
- `ncaab_pbp` / `ncaab_pbp_games` (`data/ingestors/ncaab_pbp.py`): ESPN's men's play-by-play with
  wallclock from sportsdataverse's free release, 36,893 games, 14.4M plays, 2020-21 to 2026-27. 2020-21
  has a wallclock on 47% of plays; 2021-22 on, 99%+.
- In-play prices (`data/ingestors/ncaab_inplay_history.py`): no past NCAAB in-game price was stored (0
  after-tip rows on the 155 games of 2026-02-07). The game-lines purchase had bought every hour of six
  seasons and its keeper dropped every in-play row. Re-bought: 17,117 bulk snapshots, every :00 and :30 a
  game was in progress (tip + 2 to tip + 160 min; 10.6% of games end past tip + 135), 1,998,541 in-play
  rows, 513,510 credits at 30 a snapshot (x-requests-last). Source `odds_api_ncaab_inplay_history`
  (protected in `prune_odds`), own ledger `ncaab_inplay_fetches`; the 19 probe snapshots (570 credits)
  are stored too. The feed served each snapshot 300 s early in 2020-21 / 2021-22 and 261-263 s from 2022-23.
- Each row's `odds.created_at` equals its snapshot's `pulled_at` (494,828 of 494,828 rows for 2025-26),
  so every quote is graded at the snapshot that served it.

**Measured.** The game-state model (`models/ncaab_live_ig.py`), walk-forward: mean error at most 0.49
points (margin) and 0.90 (total) in every time-left band, spread ratio 0.93-1.04. The grid, 3 markets x 2
halves x 3 EV cuts x 2 cadences, under the house standard: at the half-hour cadence the first-half spread
passes at every cut, the second-half spread at 0.05 and 0.08, the second-half total at every cut, the
first-half moneyline at 0.05 and 0.08; the first-half total and second-half moneyline fail. The shipped
selection (each night's cut from earlier seasons' decisions and the slate's size, cap 5): 2,533 bets on
588 nights 2022-23 to 2025-26, +290.4u, +10.1% (95% +6.4..+13.8), every season positive, placebo 95th

-6.4..+9.0).

:00/:30 minus 262 s, ESPN read +90 s and +150 s, decided once per game and rule into

## 2026-11-07 — Player page: filter the chart by opponent defense toughness

**Why.** Matt: *"when you click on a player can you select a toughness so that I can see how they do
against similar tough opponents"*, in all sports, starting with this view. He approved a mockup the same
day (chips over the chart, the next opponent's tier tagged NEXT and selected on open, last 3 seasons
played) and the rollout NBA, WNBA, NFL first.

**Changed.** New `player_vs_defense_{nba,wnba,nfl}` (`data/migrations/add_player_vs_defense_rpcs_2026_11_07.sql`):
one player's games over his last 3 seasons PLAYED (a missed season is skipped), each with that season's
rank of the opponent's defense for the stat and position group, plus one row for the next opponent.
Rank 1 = the toughest (fewest allowed), the opposite of `position_vs_opponent_*`, because it is printed
under the chart. The rank uses the card's role cut and stat list; the player's own games are not cut, so
they are the games the chart draws. App: `lib/defenseTier.ts` (tiers = thirds of that season's league),
a section in `usePlayerDetail`, the chip row in `PlayerStatsScreen`, the rank under each bar in
`HitRateChart`. The range chips still apply (L10 + Tough = last 10 games vs tough defenses). Hidden on H2H.

**Measured** (function body run read-only on production, 2026-11-07). Kyrie Irving, points, guards,
seasons 2024/2025/2027 (he has no 2026 rows): Tough 38 games, 37 at 15+, 24.8 avg; Average 36, 32,
25.9; Soft 41, 35, 24.3. Next opponent BKN #7 of 30 after 8 games. 116 rows, 385 ms. Ja'Marr Chase,
receiving yards, WRs, 2024-2026: Tough 12 games 67.6 avg, Average 9 86.0, Soft 19 106.7.

Before this, a split-half check on 2025-26 NBA guards' points (split 2026-01-18): a defense's first-half
rank correlates with its second-half home games at 0.25 and away games at 0.36; a home-only or away-only
first-half rank does worse (0.10 / 0.26). So the filter uses the full rank, not a venue split, and the
tiers are context rather than a strong signal.

**Verified.** `tests/test_player_vs_defense.py` (7) pins registration, grants, rank direction, and the
role and stat CASE against the sibling functions; `mobile/scripts/verify_defense_tier.ts` (30 checks).
Both were watched failing on a deliberate break (rank DESC, WR cut 2, tough cut `<`, opening tier).
tsc error set byte-identical to master (17 pre-existing, all in `lib/queries.ts`).

**Not done.** MLB (Matt has not decided whether a hitter's "defense" is the team or the opposing
starter), NCAAF, NHL and NCAAB (no rank function yet). Not applied to production: the worker's
migration pass applies it after merge, and until then the read fails and the chip row stays hidden.

## 2026-11-07 — NCAAB spread and moneyline re-graded without betPARX: a tie goes to the bettable book

**Why.** mike, "Re-grade it", after #1203 made betPARX fetch-only. The spread and moneyline models choose the
best quote over the eight books they were fitted on and write no bet when that book cannot be bet. Since 11-06
that meant every game betPARX priced best. It also meant the card failed its run and alerted ops each time
(`CardIncomplete: book_not_bettable`), from the first 15:05 UTC run after the merge, on 11-07.

**Measured** (the card's own code replayed on the research's copy of the 2021-2026 reads; units, stake_for, 95%
interval by date; with every book bettable both models reproduce their published records exactly):

| Model | Rule | 2021-2025 | 2025-26 |
|---|---|---|---|
| spread | as built | 409, +44.60u, +9.97% | 133, +30.34u, +20.96% |
| spread | 11-06: betPARX best, no bet | 342, +52.99u, +14.19% | 111, +27.45u, +22.81% |
| spread | next-best book | 406, +43.50u, +9.80% | 130, +29.46u, +20.81% |
| spread | **tie to the bettable book (shipped)** | **405, +44.70u, +10.09% [0.9, 18.5]** | **122, +25.73u, +19.40%** |
| moneyline | as built | 838, +53.11u, +4.22% | 76, +14.04u, +15.05% |
| moneyline | 11-06: betPARX best, no bet | 800, +39.44u, +3.30% | 76, +14.04u, +15.05% |
| moneyline | **tie to the bettable book (shipped)** | **836, +53.68u, +4.27% [-1.1, 9.3]** | **76, +14.04u, +15.05%** |

74 of the spread model's 89 betPARX bets, and 36 of the moneyline model's 38, were exact ties with a bettable book
(nearly all BetRivers), handed to betPARX by the alphabetical tie-break. On the spread model the betPARX+BetRivers
ties lost (70 bets, -11.91u), so the no-bet rule graded better there. On the moneyline model the same kind of
tie won (36 bets, +14.24u). A split that flips sign between two models of the same games is noise, so the
models' own stated rule ships: a tie on line and price is the same bet, and the bettable book takes it.

**What changed.** `best_per_game(z, bettable)` in both models puts a bettable book ahead of an unbettable one
inside an exact tie; with every book bettable the order is the research's, so the build replays are unchanged.
Both cards treat a strictly-best quote at a `config.STATE_LIMITED_BOOKMAKERS` book as an ordinary no-bet
(logged), not a failed run; any other unbettable best book still fails it. Docs: `docs/sports/ncaab.md`, both
models' sections.

**Verified.** Every replayed bet under the shipped rule is a research bet at the same line and price (527 of
527 spread, 912 of 912 moneyline). New tests watched failing first: the tie in both models' decide(), and both
cards' database-backed runs (the strict-betPARX game raised `CardIncomplete` without the card fix). The first-post
moneyline model already recorded an unbettable first poster as a no-bet without failing.

**Not done.** The 2023-24 to 2025-26 split by how many books share the best number (one book +21.44% on 258
bets, betPARX+BetRivers -15.43% on 70, spread model only) is a post-hoc finding and was not tested further.

## 2026-11-06 — NBA in-play model: a bet DraftKings did not price was lost on insert; fixed

**What happened.** The early spread model went through 11 games: 2 from 11-05 and 9 from 11-06.
- Two bets:
  - OKC -13.5 (live) at FanDuel -102, at 01:59:48Z. Stored, posted to #🔴-nba-live at 02:00:11Z (Discord returned
    a message id), and graded a WIN.
  - UTA +5.5 (live) at Hard Rock -105, EV 0.053, also at 01:59:48Z. Lost on insert: `null value in column
    "dk_implied_prob" of relation "picks" violates not-null constraint`. DraftKings had no price at +5.5, and
    `build_pick` left that column NULL. The same transaction held the decision log row, so neither was written, and
    nothing was posted. Dallas won 112-100, so the bet would have lost 1.05 units.
- Four games carried with no eligible quote (BOS @ CHA, PHI @ CLE, TOR @ MIL, POR @ LAL): no deciding-book price
  free of points scored since it and since Pinnacle's. That is the graded rule, not a fault.
- One game was skipped (WAS @ ATL): ESPN's header score ran ahead of its plays.
- Four were decided no bet: ORL @ PHX, MIA @ GSW, MIN @ NOP, SAS @ SAC.

**Fix.** A pick DraftKings does not price stores `dk_implied_prob = 0.0`, as the scorer's line-book picks do (`edge`
was already 0.0, `line_book` already names the book). The new test checks a line-book pick against production's NOT
NULL columns (read from `information_schema` on 2026-11-06), and it was watched failing on the old code. The test
fixture enforces no NOT NULL, which is why the existing test of this exact case passed. `data/supabase_schema.sql`
still marks `dk_odds` NOT NULL; production does not.

**Not done.** The lost bet was not written back: it was never posted, and mike decides whether it enters the record.

## 2026-11-06 — The app's book list cut to the server's ten; the checks now read config.py

**Why.** The server went from fourteen fetched books to ten on 2026-09-20 (mike: "yes drop the 4 books"). The app's copy in `mobile/src/lib/markets.ts` was never cut, so it still listed fourteen, and the member's sportsbook picker offered Bally Bet, ReBet and Fliff, which have no price in production since 2026-09-20, and ESPN BET, none since 2026-10-01 (`v_latest_odds_all_books`, measured 2026-11-06: the other ten all priced within the last hour). Found by the UX review on #1203. The two mobile checks that pinned the list pinned its LENGTH (`=== 13`), which is a second copy of the app's own number; the fetched-count check in `verify_book_lines.ts` was red on master.

**What changed.**
- `LINE_SHOP_BOOKS` is config's ten, in config's order. `BookKey` is the same ten. `BETTABLE_BOOKS` (the picker) is seven: config's `BEST_LINE_BOOKMAKERS` now that #1203 has made betPARX reference-only (it merged while this was in review and is merged in). `REFERENCE_ONLY_BOOKS` mirrors `BEST_LINE_EXCLUDE_BOOKMAKERS` as a set: Pinnacle, Bovada, ESPN BET (kept, as config keeps it) and betPARX. The display names and short labels of the four dropped books stay, so old picks and NFL card labels still render them.
- `mobile/scripts/config_books.ts` (new) reads the three lists out of config.py with Python, with the four env overrides removed so it compares against config's defaults. Neither Railway service sets any of them (variable listing, 2026-11-06). `verify_book_lines.ts` and `verify_preferred_book.ts` compare the app's lists with it, same books in the same order, and fail if config cannot be read.
- `tests/test_mobile_book_lists_match_config.py` (new) makes the same comparison from the `.ts` text, so it runs with no node.
- Fliff had a name and no short label, so the 62 stored picks that name it (in `decision_book`, `best_book` or `line_book`) showed the fallback badge FLIF; it is FLIFF now. Both checks pin the name and label of every dropped book. A comment in `BetslipBooksRow` no longer states a book count.

Two rows held all eleven old picker books and now read as all seven. `[ballybet, draftkings, fliff, hardrockbet, rebet]` reads as DraftKings and Hard Rock Bet. `[ballybet, betparx, draftkings, fliff, rebet, williamhill_us]` reads as DraftKings and Caesars. The dropped books had no price, so those members were already seeing only the books that remain. `usePreferredBooks` drops unknown keys on load, and the stored rows are not rewritten.

**Verified.** Before #1203 was merged in: the new pytest failed on master's `markets.ts` (3 of 5) and on config with betPARX excluded (2 of 5), and passed on the branch. `verify_book_lines.ts` failed the same two ways (6 and 2 checks) and once more when Python cannot be found. All 81 verify scripts ran on both trees: no script got worse; `verify_book_lines` and `verify_preferred_book` went from failing to passing, and `verify_book_links` lost its two Fliff failures (its remaining one fails on master too). tsc error set byte-identical to master (28 lines, 0 new); the one error outside the OTA gate's exemptions is a missing `@sentry/react-native` in the linked install, on master too. Full pytest against master at the same base: the same 14 failures on each, 5 more passes here. UX review: ship; `ux_scan` 0 findings. After merging master (with #1203): the four book verify scripts pass, `verify_book_links` and `verify_stats_odds` fail as on master, tsc is identical to master (28 lines), and the 50 app and config test files fail only `test_mobile_board_season::test_grade_legend_names_the_nba_season_the_way_bettors_do`, on master too. Master's own `markets.ts` (14 books) fails the new pytest 4 of 5 and `verify_book_lines` 6 checks.

**Not done.** `mobile/app.json` still lists the Bally Bet and ReBet URL schemes; that is native config and needs a store build to change, and it is harmless.

## 2026-11-06 — A pick's own stake (picks.stake_units); NCAAB agreement sizing at 2u

**Why.** mike: "I want a pick that has a higher confidence and higher long term win to be higher units, you can slice this from back testing".

**What was measured.** Seven sizing schemes, walk-forward on the six NCAAB replays (tiers from earlier seasons only), graded on 2024-25 and 2025-26 per unit risked. EV thirds, the model's win chance, price and each model's past record did not earn more per unit risked (record: -7.7 to +12.5 units at the same risk across six threshold settings). Agreement with an earlier NCAAB bet on the same game and side did: 233 bets +21.3% flat ([10.7, 31.8]; 67.8% won vs 55.5% break-even, Wilson [61.5, 73.5]), positive in every season and both halves; at 2u +32.2 units at the same risk, at 3u +50.4. Decision order checked on the replay (89 of 91) and live (every full-game total decided 7.9+ hours before tip).

**What changed.** `picks.stake_units` (1..3, NULL = 1u) and `stake_reason`, picks_log and the audit trigger too; three-argument `published_pick_stake` / `_unit_profit` / `_unit_risked`; `open_picks` passes it (`data/migrations/pick_stake_units_2026_11_06.sql`, after open_picks' owner and before the posted pair, every DDL behind a catalog read), and the two record views take it from `published_record_units_2026_10_16.sql`, which stays last and switches a view that still calls the two-argument pair. `stake_for(kelly, odds, stake_units)` and `pick_conviction`; Discord embeds, the free pick, the settled recap and the dashboard read it; a 2u pick's Discord line ends with its reason. `models/ncaab_stake.py` stamps it in all six NCAAB cards' write transaction (`config.NCAAB_AGREEMENT_STAKE_UNITS` 2.0). The app's `stakeFor` mirrors it (the parity fixture has 2u and 3u rows).

**Verified.** The migration on a scratch Postgres: views switched, NULL rows grade exactly as before, a 2u row grades at 2u, a second pass rewrites nothing, the audit trigger copies both columns. The card test: an earlier full-game Under makes the first-half Under 2u; the other side, a VOID, a NONE row and a side-model bet do not (watched failing with the rule off and with the VOID filter removed).

**Applied to production 2026-11-06, before merge** (mike: "Yes, apply it"; the one file, from the branch, with a 5 s lock timeout). Read back: both columns on picks and picks_log, the three three-argument functions, the audit trigger copying both, both record views and open_picks calling the three-argument pair (no two-argument call left). The record before and after: 47 rows, +31.784494 units on 1,255.578597 risked, and the daily view the same, to six decimals; the stake function with no stake equals the old one at all 1,241 distinct prices in picks; no pick carries a stake yet. Until this merges, master's migration list does not hold the change, so a view owner that recreates a record view puts the two-argument call back; with no stake set that grades the same, and the record-units file switches it again once merged. After that first apply the files were reordered (thirteen tests pin the record-units file last); the reordered files reach the same end state, so in production each one finds it already applied and skips.

## 2026-11-06 — NCAAB first-half total: cut 0.02 -> 0.025, EV floor 0.03 -> 0.037

**Why.** mike said yes to raising the first-half total model's bar to cut volume (it was 39% of NCAAB picks, the thinnest edge and the weakest closing value). The offer was 0.03. A finer grid of the same replay found 0.025 better than both 0.02 and 0.03.

**What changed.** `config.py`: `MODEL_EDGE_THRESHOLDS` and `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` 0.025; `MODEL_OWN_EV_FLOOR` 0.037 (edge 0.025 x the shortest allowed decimal 1.5 = 0.0375, so the floor never removes a bet the cut takes; 0.042, just under the replay's smallest EV, would have at -150 and shorter). `scripts/ncaab_first_half_total_build.py` reads its cut from config; the artifact was rebuilt so its replay is graded at the live cut. New test: the EV floor never sits above cut x the shortest decimal.

**Evidence** (the build's replay with the -200 limit, 2023-24..2025-26): 0.02 2,527 bets +123.78 units; 0.025 1,887 bets +127.31 (+5.98%, [1.51, 10.33]), every season and both halves positive; 0.03 1,369 bets +76.86. 0.0215-0.0265 is a plateau (+114 to +134). In-sample: the same seasons chose the model.

**Not done.** Nothing else in the model moved.

days. Team totals against Pinnacle's period lines: the books sit within half a point 95% of the time; 11 of 12 cells
under 200 bets, the twelfth +5.1% with an interval from −3.6%.

## 2026-11-06 — NCAAB: two bets on one game, and 1-3u sizing, measured; nothing changed

**Why.** mike: NCAAB volume high; game total, team totals and first-half total "duplicative", bet only the highest EV one unless exceptional; and "unit max on a 1-3 unit spread". Goal: units won and closing value only.

**What was measured.** The six NCAAB builds' `--bets-out` replays (the 2026-11-03 runs that reproduce the live cards; per-season units match docs/sports/ncaab.md to the cent), joined by game over 2023-24 to 2025-26. New `scripts/ncaab_stacking_check.py` reads those files and prints every table; it writes nothing. Result in docs/sports/ncaab.md, "Two bets on one game, and unit sizing".

**Result.** No NCAAB team-total model exists. Full-game and first-half totals overlap on 270 games, win correlation -0.01; one a game by highest EV costs 16.8 units (totals), 35.2 (sides), 73.4 (all six), and the dropped bets were profitable in every grouping. The only order the cards can run (full game first) costs 32.9 on totals. Sizing 1/2/3u by EV third (thirds cut walk-forward) returns 6.32% per unit risked against 6.07% flat; EV does not rank the bets.

**Not done.** No dedupe and no 1u/2u/3u by EV third (the first-half total at 0.03 would drop about 46% of its bets for about 16 units a season; that cut was not taken). Agreement sizing at 2u is the rule that did ship (the stake entry above) and this measurement does not change it. Found in passing: the NCAAB cards' NONE rows (#1147) were written and then removed from `picks` (535 in `picks_log`, 21 left in `picks`). Handed to a separate session, which found the scorer's end-of-pass sweep and fixed it in #1197 (entry below).

## 2026-11-06 — Sentry follow-ups from the post-merge review of #1199

**Why.** Chief Engineer reviews 5804446544 and 5804498015, after #1199 had already merged. Four gaps: the OTA source-map step reads `dist/` after `eas update`, which does not leave the bundle there; `device_id` was set in a promise after `Sentry.init`, so an early crash missed the join to `app_errors`; `sentry_events_sync` could stack a hung copy on the next 15-minute tick; nothing scrubbed a token out of a breadcrumb before it was stored in `sentry_events.raw`.

**What changed.** The OTA workflow exports with `expo export --source-maps` and publishes that directory (`eas update --skip-bundler --input-dir dist`). `mobile/scripts/upload-ota-sourcemaps.mjs` uploads those maps with sentry-cli at release `com.mja.bettingpicks@1.3.0+13` and dist `13` (from app.json — the native SDK's release; `Sentry.init` still does not set `release`, so the binary's dSYM keeps matching). `device_id` is mirrored to a file and read synchronously into `initialScope`; the first launch waits for AsyncStorage before init. The `release` tag is the app version. `beforeSend` / `beforeBreadcrumb` scrub emails, bearer and basic tokens, JWTs, API keys, passwords, query-string tokens, and user email and IP. The copy job sets `max_instances=1, coalesce=True`, a 5s/10s connect/read timeout, and a 45s budget per tick. Named in `_PIPELINE_JOBS`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_mobile_sentry.py`, `tests/test_sentry_events.py`, and `mobile/scripts/verify_sentry_scrub.ts`. The email scrub was removed once and that check failed, then put back.

**Not done.** No Sentry account, so nothing was uploaded or sent. No EAS update, build, or submit. `sentry_events` was not migrated (the table is already in production).

## 2026-11-06 — betPARX is fetched, and never the book a pick names

**Why.** mike: "global rule. remove betparx from sports books for recommeding betting lines in picks only niche
avaialble in a few states". betPARX is licensed in MD, PA and NJ only. 60 BETs over 14 models had been decided there
since 2026-09-11, 53 of them in the last 30 days (evidence: `docs/rules_evidence.md`, §6 entry).

**What changed.** `config.STATE_LIMITED_BOOKMAKERS = {"betparx"}`. It is removed from `BEST_LINE_BOOKMAKERS` and
`BETTABLE_BOOKS` after any environment override; the espnbet exclusion can still be reversed by a variable, this
one cannot. It stays in `LINE_SHOP_BOOKMAKERS`: the NCAAB consensus models take a median over every book and were
fitted with it. The copies of the list follow: the NFL cards' standalone fallback (`nfl/data_ingest/books.py`,
fallback literal and env path), the app (`REFERENCE_ONLY_BOOKS`, so the sportsbook picker, line chips, lines
board and parlay shop drop it, and All books shows it as a reference row), and the NBA in-play fetch, which built
its request from the deciding list and now names betPARX explicitly so its quotes keep arriving. Rule: one line in
CLAUDE.md §6, the full text in `.claude/rules/config-topology.md`. To fit the CLAUDE.md size cap, §8's repeat of
"every model is live real money" (already §2) was removed. From the UX review: the pick card's "Found at" caption
clipped the book to the pill's three letters ("PAR", and VoiceOver read it as the word "par"); it now shows the
four-letter label and speaks the full name. The All books spoken suffix reads "reference price only". Four tests
that pinned a model's fitted books to the deciding list now pin the fitted books minus betPARX, and that the card
never bets at it.

**Verified.** `tests/test_betparx_never_a_pick_book.py` (10 tests) watched failing first: before the change a
better betPARX price won both the game and the prop shop. Full suite, failure sets: master 14, branch the same
14 plus none (the 4 book-pin tests above were updated). App tests (`tests/test_mobile*.py`): identical on both.
App type check: no error outside `src/lib/queries.ts` and `scripts/`, which is the over-the-air job's gate.
`verify_book_lines.ts` on master fails 2 lines (stale 13-fetched and 10-bettable pins); on the branch 1 (the
13-fetched pin: the app still lists ballybet, rebet, fliff and espnbet, which config stopped fetching). Worker and
pollers carry no book-list variable.

**Not done.** The 5 open `ncaab_spread_market` BETs at betPARX on 11-06 stand (§1c). That model writes no bet when
its best quote is at a book it may not bet, so it loses the games betPARX prices best (7 of its 12 BETs so far);
it was not re-graded with betPARX out of the deciding set.

## 2026-11-06 — same-game parlays: the Betslip priced them as if every leg were independent

**Why.** Matt sent two screenshots of one 4-leg NBA slip (MIA@GSW: Lendeborg O24.5 pts, Klay O3.5 3PM;
ORL@PHX: Booker O29.5 pts, Carter O9.5 reb). The app showed +5993 at DraftKings and +6040 at FanDuel;
FanDuel's own slip priced it +4254, as two same-game parlays (+718 x +432). `priceBooksForParlay`,
`computeParlayMetrics` and `computeCorrelatedMetrics` multiplied every leg's price whatever its game.

**Measured.** FanDuel's two same-game prices multiply to +4252, so the whole gap sits inside the two
same-game pairs. Our correlation priors rate both pairs (opposing-team NBA props, rho 0.01) as almost
independent, so the gap is the book's same-game margin, not correlation. FanDuel leg prices from
`player_prop_odds` (snapshot 2026-11-06 00:40 UTC) give a 22% / 20% cut per pair; against the app's own
FanDuel tile (+6040, the number the formula is applied to) it is 15.8% per pair. Default set to 0.158.

**Options Matt weighed.** A (hide the price), B (estimate from correlation + a margin), C (buy a same-game
feed: OddsBlaze lists $299/mo; none of the vendors' own docs could be fetched from the sandbox). He chose
an estimate that improves from entered prices.

**What changed.**
- `mobile/src/lib/sgpPricing.ts`: a game with 2+ legs is a same-game group; a book's price is the legs
  multiplied x (1 - cut) per group. The cut is the book's own fit once it has 5 entries, else 0.158.
- Every surface applies it: headline + EV/grade (`computeCorrelatedMetrics`), betslip bar
  (`betslipSummary`), book tiles (`priceBooksForParlay`), saved slips (`computeParlayMetrics`). Labelled
  "est." / "Est. odds". The line-shop row is withheld on a same-game slip (one bet, one book).
- `SgpPriceEntry`: the member types the book's slip price; it replaces the estimate at that book for the
  slip and is stored through `log_sgp_price`.
- `data/migrations/add_sgp_price_observations_2026_11_06.sql` (hand-applied to production 2026-11-06, not in
  ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS): table `sgp_price_observations` (RLS on, no grants), insert-only `log_sgp_price`,
  `sgp_haircuts()` = per-book median cut over 120 days. Seeded with Matt's screenshot (fanduel, n=1).

**Verified.** `mobile/scripts/verify_sgp_pricing.ts` 30 checks pass; with the cut switched off 6 fail.
`verify_betslip.ts` and `verify_line_shop.ts` pass. tsc error set identical to origin/master.
Production: `sgp_haircuts()` returns fanduel 0.1579, n=1; security advisor lists the two new functions
alongside the existing `log_app_timings` pattern, nothing new of another kind.

**UX review** (frontend-ux-designer, ship with fixes): all six should-fix items done — the "DK +N" and hold
rows under the headline say "est."; the headline's "est." and odds are one VoiceOver stop; the panel's
VoiceOver label is its on-screen text; "Use" confirms with a toast and a typed price can be cleared; no
best-payout star when typed and estimated prices are mixed; book chips get hit slop. Also taken: the bar
shows a typed DraftKings price (shared store keyed to the slip, `useEnteredSgpPrices`), the tile coverage
line is one line. Not taken: the two hex colours ux_scan flags in BetslipBar are pre-existing lines.

**Not done.** The 0.158 default is one slip at one book; DraftKings and every other book use it until they
have 5 entries each. The default was fitted on the same slip the test reproduces, so that test pins the
arithmetic, not accuracy.
## 2026-11-06 — NBA projection props store and show a shrunk probability; the bets do not change

**Why.** Graded walk-forward, every part of `nba_prop_projection`'s EV range claims about three times what its bets return (the EV calibration grade, PR #1178). The open question was whether members should see that honest edge instead of the inflated one; this builds it, with no bet changed. mike, 2026-11-06: "Yes, ship it".

**What changed.**
- `config.MODEL_PROB_SHRINK = {"nba_prop_projection": {"k": 0.325, "from": "2026-11-07"}}`: k by maximum likelihood on all three graded seasons (66,588 bets; 0.324 on a finer grid; 90% likelihood range 0.270 to 0.377). Rows for games before "from" store the raw number, so a reader tells the two apart from the row's own date.
- `models/honest_ev.shrink_factor`, `shown_probability` (p' = implied + k (p − implied) at the decision price) and its inverse `decision_probability`.
- `scripts/nba_prop_projection_card.pick_rows`: `model_probability`, `model_probability_cal`, `edge`, `decision_edge`, `best_edge` come from p', stored unrounded. The door's key `_ev` stays the raw EV; `find_bets` is untouched.
- `tracking/discord_notifier._honest` (paid channels) and `_decided` (the free pick) take the decision price and game date and recover the decided number before solving the "good to" price. Found in review: without the date, a re-post of an old raw row was un-shrunk and posted "good to −200" (Myles Turner Under 1.5 Blocks: −136 today); now pinned by a test.

**Verified.** Every graded bet of both NBA prop models replayed through the live code (`tests/test_nba_prop_projection_shrink.py`): the door's choice is identical bet for bet, in order, with the same drop reasons, at four a night (1,867) and six (2,639); "good to" printed identically on all 66,588 (rounded to 6 decimals, 113 broke a half-point tie the other way, so the row stores p' unrounded); smallest stored decision edge +0.0015, so the action filter keeps every bet. Four mutations (no recovery in Discord, door ranked on the shrunk EV, raw number stored, 4-decimal rounding) each fail the new tests. At the door the projection model's bets claimed +28.1% as stored until now, +9.1% shrunk, and returned +9.9%.

**Not done.** The 78 rows written before this change keep the raw number (CLAUDE.md 1c). The calibration monitor's window mixes raw and shrunk claims until the old picks age out (flagged). The free pick's eligibility cuts on the DraftKings edge, not the decision edge as the app does, so a projection pick whose DraftKings price is worse than its book's drops out of the free-pick pool more often (8 of 55 live DraftKings-priced picks instead of 1; flagged, every model's pool would change if fixed). The code graph is not rebuilt here. Every reader and what changes for it: `docs/nba_models_2026.md`, section 5, "The probability a pick stores".

## 2026-11-06 — the game scorer's sweep deleted every other writer's NONE rows

**Why.** A session analysing NCAAB pick volume found the NCAAB cards' NONE rows (PR #1147, Matt's "the all tab should have all picks") in `picks_log` but not in `picks`.

**Cause, measured (read-only Supabase).** `picks_log` holds every NCAAB NONE delete with its transaction time: 257 deletes in 27 transactions from 2026-11-04 15:23Z. Each transaction landed 0.3 to 2.5 s before a full scoring pass (records_in about 400) logged its finish in `pipeline_log`, and the same transactions also deleted NFL opener/wind and NCAAF NONE rows. That is the end-of-pass sweep in `models/scorer.run_scorer`: on a full pass (refresh pass, daily pipeline) it had no model filter, so it deleted every writer's unsettled non-BET rows on every game not yet started across the whole look-ahead window. The per-game clear never reached NCAAB (no NCAAB model in `config.MODELS`, so the loop skips the game first). Since 2026-11-01 the same sweep also deleted 3,704 `nfl_opener_spread` and 2,478 `nfl_wind_totals` NONE rows in 213 passes; `publish_scored` re-inserted them at the next NFL poll.

**What changed.** `scorer._pass_rewritten_models`: the poller clears `config.MODELS`; a full pass clears `config.MODELS` plus the MLB pitcher/batter and WNBA prop models (their scorers run next in the same pass, and the clear is what releases a player the prop lock holds at NONE). The sweep, the per-game clear and the UFC left-out-id clear all filter on it. Checked against every model with a non-BET unsettled delete in `picks_log` since 2026-07-01: every one outside the set is a live model (cleared by its own loop, `is_live`), a retired model (mlb_prop_batter_hr/rbi, nba_moneyline), an nfl_prop model (already excluded) or a victim (the five NCAAB cards, NFL opener/wind). New: `tracking/restore_swept_ncaab_none_rows.py` and the worker job `restore_swept_ncaab_none_rows`, declared to run once (apply, after an 11-minute wait so the replaced container's 600 s drain, which can finish a scoring step, is over).

**Behaviour change, NFL.** NFL opener and wind NONE rows now stay in place between NFL polls and are updated by `publish_scored` (its UPDATE path and `touch_created` rule) instead of being deleted and re-inserted every scoring pass. Their `created_at` now means what that writer intends: when the number was last seen to move.

**Verified.** `tests/test_scorer_spares_other_writers_no_bet_rows.py` runs `run_scorer` on the sqlite board: all nine protected rows (five NCAAB cards, two NFL rules, a card row on an MLB game the loop re-scores and on one it does not) are deleted on the old code and kept on the new; the game model and prop clears behave as before. `tests/test_restore_swept_ncaab_none_rows.py` runs the restore SQL on a scratch Postgres; with the started-game and has-a-row guards removed it restores 2 rows it must not. Rebuild of the four columns picks_log lacks, checked against all 131 NCAAB NONE rows in picks at 15:06Z: every logged column and every rebuilt column matched. Count-only run on production at 15:18Z: 57 rows to restore (all `ncaab_total_blend`, games from 21:00Z). Predicted at 15:06Z and then observed: the full pass that finished at 15:24:34.23Z deleted the 110 NONE rows the 15:05Z moneyline and spread cards wrote (58 and 52), at 15:24:33.82Z; they join the restore.

**Not done.** NONE rows of NCAAB games that started before the restore runs are not restored (200 of the 257 at 15:00Z, plus whatever later passes delete before the deploy); the owner decides. The bet links are not in `picks_log` and stay empty on restored rows.

**After the merge (#1197, 16:14:48Z; mike: "Merge it").** The worker ran the new code from 16:19:25Z. The first full pass on it (16:22Z to 16:28:10Z) deleted no NCAAB or NFL opener/wind row: 72 NFL opener/wind NONE rows written before it were still in `picks` after it, while it still cleared its own UFC, NHL and NCAAF rows. The restore job was queued at 16:20:12Z, wrote at 16:31:13Z and restored all 167 rows it found (57 `ncaab_total_blend`, 52 `ncaab_spread_market`, 58 `ncaab_moneyline_own_spread`, every game untipped). So the 110 rows the 15:24Z pass deleted all came back, and the rows still missing are the 200 of games that started before the fix. Full suite, branch against origin/master: the 14 failures on master are pre-existing and are also on the branch; the branch added 3, all guards this change tripped. Two are the NHL goalie scans, which require every picks writer to call the goalie gate or be listed with a reason (the restore is now listed). One is a UFC test that pinned the left-out-id clear's exact parameters. Fixed in the follow-up PR.

## 2026-11-06 — Freeze tracking: a trail on every app report, restart / js_stall / modal_stuck

**Why.** Matt: the Settings Back button did nothing, then "Cannot move from that screen need to restart. This keeps happening in other spots check the whole app". The code shows nothing on Settings that overrides the native Back. `app_errors` (last 3 days) held only `early_exit` rows on iOS 27.x, and `app_timings` records only screens that read data, so Settings left no trace. Cause not established. Matt: "put in data and analytics tracking so that we can find these errors in the future".

**What changed.** `mobile/src/lib/freezeLog.ts` (import-free, like crashLog): a 40-event trail (screens, coalesced taps, app state, pop-up open/shown/close/dismissed, stalls) attached to every `app_errors` report as `extra.trail` + `extra.session`; a session record saved every 5 s and on every app-state change; three kinds: `restart` (previous session ≥15 s ended active/inactive, or relaunch within 2 min of background), `js_stall` (2 s watchdog, gap ≥5 s while active, max 3 per launch), `modal_stuck` (iOS gave no onShow / onDismiss within 4 s). All 22 pop-ups now use `components/TrackedModal.tsx` (required `trackName`). The root counts taps with `onTouchStart` (never claims them). Migration `app_errors_freeze_kinds_2026_11_06.sql` widened the kind CHECK and the RPC and raised the `extra` cap to 16,000; **applied to production 2026-11-06 via MCP before merge** (CHECK read back with all eight kinds), because the client queue stops at the first rejected send.

**Verified.** `verify_freeze_log.ts` 26 checks; five guards watched failing by breaking their code (onShow clear, background window, watchdog clock reset, trail hook, stall cap). One redundant guard (last-tick state) was removed after its break was not caught. `tsc` error set byte-identical to origin/master, 0 in touched files. Full pytest: failure set identical to origin/master (34 failed + 127 errors, all pre-existing, none in touched files). UX review: ux_scan 0 findings; RN 0.81.5 source confirms always passing onShow/onDismiss changes nothing and the root onTouchStart cannot steal a touch; its battery note moved the tick 1 s → 2 s and the stall bar 3 s → 5 s.

**Not done / unverified.** Not run on a phone: whether iOS fires onDismiss on every close here (if every close shows as `modal_stuck dismiss`, it is the callback), whether native-header taps reach the root tap counter, and the battery cost. Queries: `docs/app_errors.md` § Freeze tracking.
## 2026-11-06 — Stats tab NBA matchup column graded on last season

**Why.** Matt: "Match up looks the same for everyone" (NBA Rebounds board, every row 3 of 5 bars).

**Cause.** `StatsScreen` read the opponent-defence grade with `fetchTeamStats(sport, yearET())`. NBA labels a
season by its ENDING year, so in November that asked for 2026 = 2025-26 while the Teams board
(`teamBoardSeason`) read 2026-27. Tonight's opponents on the screenshot (GSW 112.51, MIA 111.72, PHX 110.52 in
2025-26) all sat within half a sigma of the 112.395 anchor, so every row graded C = 3 bars.

**What changed.** The matchup read uses `teamBoardSeason(sport)`. The season note in the matchup fact
(`gradeOpponentDefence`) and the column tooltip use `teamSeasonLabel`, so NBA reads "(2026-27)", not "(2027)"
(UX review finding). Guards: `tests/test_stats_matchup_season.py` and a new check in
`mobile/scripts/verify_toughness.ts`; each fails without its fix.

**Verified.** Production `team_stats_board('NBA', 2027)`: 30 teams, 7-9 games each; GSW 114.17, MIA 115.81,
PHX 123.41, which grade 4, 4 and 5 bars. tsc error set identical to origin/master. `verify_toughness` has one

## 2026-11-05 — ten-minute Railway drain, and the daily gate rechecked from master

**Why.** #1186 shipped the SIGTERM handler and the merge check while the review was still open. Railway's drain default is 0 seconds, so SIGKILL follows SIGTERM and the handler does not run. The check ran on pull_request events only, so a green 5:30am head could merge at 6:20am, and a head pushed during the daily stayed red after the row closed. The pull-request job also checked out the PR and ran it with the write `DATABASE_URL`.

**What changed.** `railway.json` `deploy.drainingSeconds` is 600 (the schema type is a number; config-as-code overrides the dashboard for that deployment). The gate workflow is `pull_request_target` plus a schedule. The checkout is the base sha. The pull-request job reads `DATABASE_URL_READONLY` only. The schedule, default branch only, re-posts check name `daily pipeline not running` on each open PR head every 10 minutes from 09:00 UTC through 03:59 UTC, using `DATABASE_URL`. Merge queue was not enabled: nothing else in the repo listens for `merge_group`, and that event runs the workflow from the merge commit. A session advisory lock (`tracking/scoring_lease.py`) is held by the daily child and by `refresh_pass.sh` until the ledger row is closed. The boot sweep skips a kind whose key is held. The daily catch-up waits for the daily key on its own thread, not before `sched.start()`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_scoring_lease.py` and the new cases in `tests/test_deploy_drain.py`. The sweep test aborts the evening and leaves the daily open while the daily lease is held, and aborts the daily when the lease is free. A lock-timeout wait is not treated as free. `railway.json` and the lease constant are both 600.

**Not done.** No Railway dashboard edit. No GitHub ruleset edit. The readonly role SQL is in `docs/cloud_worker.md` and was not run. The first deploy of the lease overlaps a process that is not holding it. A new refresh does not wait on the daily key, and live loops are not under the lease. #1160 is still an open draft; its in-process wait is not on master. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. `graphify` is not installed here, so the graph was not rebuilt.

## 2026-11-05 — odds_dk_pregame_current leaves out a weeks-old early line the provider is not listing

**Why.** The 15:26Z row was STALE: "2 of 66 games ... (NBA 2 of 4, NCAAB 0 of 44, NCAAF 0 of 7, NHL 0 of 11)", `latest_seen` 2026-10-23T02:11:27Z. PHI@CLE (tip 2026-11-07 00:40Z) and POR@LAL (tip 03:10Z) have no h2h, spread or total from any book newer than 2026-10-23 02:10Z. The other seven Nov 6 NBA games have no price from any book, so they are not in the share. MIA@GSW and ORL@PHX have a DraftKings price at 15:17:01Z. The 15:17Z `/v4/sports/basketball_nba/events` call returned 200 and 434 bytes, and `/v4/sports/basketball_nba/odds` returned 200 and 4,822 bytes: the provider was listing today's two games. Nov 5's games first got a DraftKings price at 05:17:22Z, so the 04:58Z run does not reach the next evening's slate. The 2026-10-29 exemption only drops a far game when the same sport still has another game more than 24 hours out that a book is pricing. No NBA game that far out was priced.

**What changed.** A game is left out of the share when no book has a main-market price inside the 180-minute bound, it starts more than 24 hours out, that price is more than 7 days old, the same sport has a game inside 24 hours with a fresh DraftKings price, and a successful bulk odds or events-list call for that sport landed inside the bound. Seven days sits between the 09-30 outage (newest price 2026-09-26 22:59Z, under four days, still 11 of 11) and this 13-day early line. A per-event prop or alternate call is not the list. A price only hours old stays in, a game inside 24 hours stays in, and a sport with no fresh nearer game stays in. Pick logic, the price-age bound and stakes are unchanged. No production write.

**Verified.** The new fixture (two NBA games more than 24 hours out with only the Oct 23 DraftKings snapshot, today's NBA games fresh, NHL, NCAAB and NCAAF fresh, and the 15:17Z list calls) was STALE, "NBA 2 of 4", "the board is emptying", before the change. After: OK, and the detail says the call did not list them. `python3 -m pytest -q --noconftest tests/test_health_pregame_prices_current.py`: 42 passed. The `LIKE` in that read is written `'%%'` so psycopg2 does not treat the percent as a format spec (`tests/test_sql_percent_escaping.py`). A far price from this morning stays STALE with the same call. A prop call, another sport's call, a call older than the bound, and a 401 do not drop the games. A game at 10 hours and a game at exactly 24 hours stay in. A sport whose every game is 33 hours out stays STALE while the call succeeded. The 09-30 replay still asserts CRIT and "11 of 11". The 2026-10-29 pulled-slate replay stays OK.

**Not done.** The 15:26Z row was not rewritten. The next health run writes the new verdict. No threshold, stake, or pause change. `graphify` is not installed here, so the graph was not rebuilt.

## 2026-11-05 — SIGTERM drain, and a merge check while the daily is open

**Why.** Every merge to master redeploys the Railway worker. The process had no SIGTERM handler, so the container died mid-step and left `pipeline_runs.finished_at` null until a later boot stamped `aborted`. That cut off the 6am daily and the evening and hourly passes.

**What changed.** The scheduler catches SIGTERM, forwards it to the direct child, and waits. The daily (`run_pipeline.py`) and `scripts/refresh_pass.sh` finish the step in progress, skip the rest, and close the row with `failed_steps` containing `deploy-cutoff`. That token is a deploy-kill, like `aborted`: it does not use the one-retry cap, and a drained hourly is still replaced at boot. `refresh_pass_steps` ignores the token. SIGKILL is unchanged. The check `Daily pipeline gate / daily pipeline not running` reads an open `run_kind = daily` row (`started_at::timestamptz` within 75 minutes) and fails while one is open. Without `DATABASE_URL` it uses 06:00–07:15 ET. Bypass: label `emergency-merge`, or `bypass-daily-gate` in the PR body. The check blocks merges only once it is required on master.

**Not done.** No Railway config, env, or secret change. No resumable daily. No separate pipeline service. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. `graphify update` was not run.

## 2026-11-05 — BYOM staking functions inlined (NBA 2025-26 points record timed out)

**Why.** Post-deploy check of #1177, as anon at 3s: NBA points over took 593 ms for 2026-27 (was 6,032 ms), but 2025-26 still timed out (official 1,966 ms + close 1,276 ms). Both carry `SET search_path`, which stops the planner inlining a SQL function, and `byom_win_units` called `byom_risk_units` three times: four full calls per row. Both checks fail without the file (proconfig set; the plan prints `byom_win_units(...)`). A second pass leaves both functions untouched. 341 BYOM, migration, grant and adapter tests pass.

**Not done.** The Supabase advisor will report the two functions as `function_search_path_mutable`; accepted, since the bodies use only qualified names and no table.
