## 2026-11-05 — NFL pick labels carry no unit figure; one unit number per row

**Why.** mike: "everything must be units", on one basis, the posted stake (1u to win at `COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds)`, risk capped at 3u). On production the open picks view and Discord showed NFL rows with two unit numbers that disagreed: `DAL @ IND — IND +7.5 · 0.79u · NEW 0m` beside `1.17u to win 1u`, `LA @ WAS Under 47.5 · 0.87u` beside `1.1u to win 1u`, `CLE @ NO — CLE +3 · 0.98u · NEW 0m` beside `1.15u to win 1u`. The label's figure came from `scripts/nfl_wind_publisher.py`: `build_rows` wrote `· {stake_pct:.2f}u` and `build_opener_rows` wrote `· {kelly_fraction / OPENER_UNIT_PCT:.2f}u`, the models' own Kelly sizing (production: pick 4197557 `kelly_fraction` 0.00787 and label `0.79u`; pick 4197610 0.0087 and `0.87u`). Read-only on production: 61 labels in `picks` carry a `· N.NNu` figure, all `nfl_wind_totals` (37) and `nfl_opener_spread` (24) BETs, first 2026-09-06, last 2026-11-04. No other model's label has one.

**What changed.** New wind and opener labels are the bet only, with the opener's age tag kept: `LA @ WAS Under 47.5`, `DAL @ IND — IND +7.5 · NEW 0m`. The sizing stays on the row as `kelly_fraction`. That matches every other sport's labels. Discord, the app and the view already show the posted stake beside the label; writing it into the label as well would repeat it, and could disagree with a better price Discord publishes. Stored labels are not rewritten (CLAUDE.md §1c). Both publishers are insert-once, so no existing row is touched. One pattern drops the old figure on display, `\s*·\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)?u(?!\s*[^\s·])`: `tracking/pick_integrity.label_without_stake`, the `open_picks` view's `pick` column (`regexp_replace`, marker re-hashed), and the app's `labelWithoutStake` in `mobile/src/lib/format.ts`; tests pin the three copies to one string. It is used by Discord's pick and free-pick fields, the X post, push summaries and line-change alerts, and the app's pick rows, results modal, model pages, tracked list, stake editor, pick detail title, betslip legs, saved slips and the VoiceOver labels at those sites. The Signals card, which already dropped the figure with its own copy of the pattern, now calls the same helper. The bet, the age tag and an old `(Opener …, BOOK)` annotation stay word for word. The Discord void notice keeps the stored label: it quotes the message it voids and shows no stake. Readers that parse old labels are unchanged and still pass on all three label generations: the line check (`pick_problems`), the CLV book parse, Discord's book parse and `mark_placeable`'s age match. `docs/sports/nfl.md` and `docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md` describe the label.

**Verified.** The new tests failed before the fix (16 at first, and 3 more for the betslip and the Signals card after the UX review) and pass after. The 131 test files that touch the changed code ran on clean origin/master and on the branch. Master fails 2 of them (`test_market_coverage_probe::test_an_all_line_leg_slip_does_not_claim_a_model`, `test_nhl_totals_card::test_insert_once_under_the_model_lock`). The branch fails the same 2. Twelve existing tests in six files pinned the old label text or the old view column and were updated to the new shape. The pattern was run read-only in Postgres on all 61 stored labels: 61 changed, 0 still carry a unit figure, and 0 labels without a `·` changed anywhere in `picks`. The view's `WHERE` and `pick` expression, run read-only, return the 5 open NFL rows as `DAL @ IND — IND +7.5 · NEW 0m` / `1.17u to win 1u` and so on. The app type check (`npm ci`, `tsc --noEmit`) is byte-identical to master: 27 diagnostics, the same set, and the repo's tsc gate passes on both. The frontend-ux-designer review found no blockers; its should-fix (the betslip) is in.

**Not done.** The view change reaches production only when `python -m data.view_migrations` runs after the merge. Stored labels keep their text in `picks`, `opening_signals` and Discord's already-posted messages.
## 2026-11-05 — NBA prop market model at the door: its top 2% returns nothing; no re-weighting clears

**Why.** Section 15 flagged it: at the NBA prop door (one bet a game across both NBA prop models,
six a night, first by tip), `nba_prop_market` claims +7.9% and returned +3.0% walk-forward. The door
picks each game's bet by the bet's percentile within its own model, so a market bet high in its own
range takes the game from a projection bet. mike wants the highest-EV picks and the most units.
Task: does the market model's EV rank its own bets at the top, and should the door count a market
bet for less when it competes with a projection bet in the same game?

**What was measured** (both NBA prop models' graded bets, 2023-24..2025-26, the section 15 loader;
every door rule compared with today's door night by night; walk-forward on 2024-25 and 2025-26):
- The market model's EV ranks its bets up to its 98th percentile. Its top 2% claims +10.5% and
  returned -1.6% (492 bets, -9.1..+5.9), against +6.4% for the 90th-98th. The gap is negative in
  every season and both halves, and every 90% range includes zero.
- That is all of the door's over-claim: below the 98th percentile its door bets claim +6.3% and
  returned +8.2%; at the 98th and up they claim +10.9% and returned -1.8% (277 bets).
- The projection bets those market bets displaced were no better: in 778 contested door games,
  -2.6 units (-61.8..+59.2), -1.1 points per unit risked (-9.7..+7.7).
- The door: lowering the market percentile (0.01-0.50) moves -7.3 to +9.6 units against today's
  +183.7. Capping it loses at every cap (-2.5 to -27.0). Handing its top bets to the projection bet
  moves -14.4 to +7.1. Ranking its top 3% last in its game gains +28.4. That is a peak (top 4% +4.5, top 1% +8.3)
  that falls all in the early half, and half of it is swaps to its own next-best bet; walk-forward
  +14.0 (-23.5..+52.0). Every rule's walk-forward 90% range includes zero, apart from two caps whose
  range sits below it. No rule tilts to favourites: those that hand games to the projection model
  risk up to 4.6% less.

**What changed.** Docs only: `docs/nba_models_2026.md` section 16 (and a pointer from section 15's
flag), `docs/thresholds.md` (the `nba_prop_market` section).

**Verified.** Today's door reproduced to the unit (+183.7, +8.17%, 2,639 bets; the market model 791
bets, +7.79% claimed, +4.95% returned) before any rule was graded. The rule that ranks the market
model's top bets last in their game mixes three kinds of swap. It was taken apart, and the clean version graded on its
own (+6.8, walk-forward -4.9).

**Deliberately not done.** No ceiling on the market model's EV: its bets above EV 0.08 made +5.3
units over three seasons, so a ceiling moves a few units either way, and it would be a model change.
No `Updated-By`: nothing cleared.

## 2026-11-04 — daily and refresh do not score together; manual daily reclaim is bounded

**Why.** Follow-up on #1158 (squash `60dac3a5`). Chief Engineer review 5769412674, two Mediums. A relaunched daily starts about 5s after boot and the refresh catch-up about 60s after, and the :17 hourly still fires while that daily is running. Both passes delete unlocked pick rows across the look-ahead window and insert. The unique index drops a second insert of the same pick. It does not stop one pass deleting a row the other just wrote. A deploy-killed manual `daily_pipeline` row was reclaimed on every boot with the attempt given back, so it ignored the 12-kill cap, the 22:00 ET cutoff, and `RUN_DAILY_RETRY`.

**What changed.** `run_refresh_pass` waits until `_DAILY_LOCK` is free, without holding `_REFRESH_LOCK` during the wait. `run_daily_pipeline` holds `_DAILY_LOCK` and waits until `_REFRESH_LOCK` is free. A second refresh still skips. Past the wait (daily max plus a minute, or 30 minutes the other way) the waiting pass scores anyway. `_reclaim_stale` fails a running `daily_pipeline` row when `decide_daily_retry` would skip, including the kill switch, the deploy-kill cap, and the cutoff. A fresh pending row is still the manual kick. `classify_relaunch` uses the latest `started_at`. A skipped manual job result includes `start_number`.

**Not done.** No production row was written. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. The pre-game line poller can still publish beside these passes; it already takes the Discord and push advisory locks.

## 2026-11-04 — NBA prop projection model: its EV over-claims by about three times; no ranking fix clears

**Why.** Grading the six-a-night prop door (next entry), ranking props by EV lost units because the
projection model's highest-EV props looked the most overconfident. mike wants the highest-EV picks
and the most units. Task: a calibration fix for the top of `nba_prop_projection`'s EV range, graded
walk-forward, then the door re-graded with corrected EVs.

**What was measured** (both NBA prop models' graded bets, 2023-24..2025-26; walk-forward on 2024-25
and 2025-26, 2023-24 mapped leave one out only for the door's three-season total):
- Every eighth of the projection model's EV claims about three times what it returns (top eighth
  +23.2% claimed, +6.9% returned; bottom +2.5% and +1.4%); 7 of 8 outside their 90% range.
- EV x k with k 0.325 / 0.300 (fit on the earlier seasons) puts all 8 inside, largest gap 2.1
  points, 8 of 8 quartiles inside early and late. The top 2% still claims +11.3% against +4.4%
  (-1.9..+10.8).
- Above about the 80th percentile of its EV the return is flat (+5% to +8%). Five reordering maps
  (blend with the price, per market and side, a squared-gap term, isotonic, banded): none ranked the
  top better than the model's own EV (top eighth +4.1% to +6.3% against +6.3% to +6.9%).
- The door (one a game, six a night): today +183.7 units. Best six of the night by the shrunk EV
  -29.4 against it (walk-forward -21.8). The rules that won more units (the blend percentile
  +68.1, expected units +46.3) risked 29-36% more and changed the return per unit risked by
  +0.5 and -0.7 points, with every 90% range including zero.
- Sizing (section 14's 1-3 unit grade, same 2,639 bets, at flat's risk): the shrink keeps every
  within-model third the same, so the reversed scale's lead cannot go away (+165.7 within each
  model, raw or shrunk). On one scale across both models it halves (+195.9 against +208.1, flat
  +183.7). No corrected EV makes sizing up beat flat; every 90% range includes zero.

**What changed.** Docs only: `docs/nba_models_2026.md` section 15, `docs/thresholds.md` (the
`nba_prop_projection` section).

**Verified.** Today's door reproduced to the unit (+183.7, +8.17%, +62.7 / +61.4 / +59.6), and the
best four by percentile to +102.5, before any corrected EV was graded.

**Deliberately not done.** The shrink was not shipped: one factor on every EV changes no bet and no
ranking. The stored probability (`model_probability`, `edge`) is still the uncorrected one. The
market model's door over-claim (+7.9% claimed, +3.0% returned walk-forward) is flagged, not worked.

## 2026-11-04 — NBA pick volume: one game-total bet a game, six props a night

**Why.** mike: the NBA card sends too many picks. The full-game over, the home team total under and
the first-half over looked like the same bet; props came only from the early games because of the
four-a-night limit. Goal: the highest-EV picks, maximising units won and closing-line value.

**What was measured** (2023-24..2025-26, the lab bet files the replays read; live picks 10-20..11-04):
- Every game the full-game over bet, the first-half over bet too (323 of 323), because the first
  half's cut (gap >= 2) sits under the full game's (>= 3). The twins: +3.7% (90% range -5.0 to
  +12.7), correlated 0.5 with the full-game bet. The team total under met an over on 2 of 942 games.
- One a game by the highest model EV: +124.40 units; full game first: +139.08; every bet: +152.79.
  Each model's EV is market-fair plus one fixed uplift, so it does not compare across models.
- No gap band where both bets paid: the twins at gap 5+ returned -12.9%.
- On the live population (no 10-game wait): 450 of 450 full-game bets had a first-half twin, the
  twins +3.7%; every bet +156.27 units on 1,624, the live rule +136.74 on 1,162.
- Props: live, every night with more than four games took its four props from the first five or
  six tips. In the grade the early games were the better ones (+8.0% first four tips against +2.0%
  for the rest, nights of six or more). Own-model EV percentile is anti-predictive at the top
  (0.80-0.90 +10.1%, 0.98-1.00 +4.7%); the best four of a night by percentile +102.5 units against
  +153.6 in tip order. Six a night in tip order +183.7, eight +182.8.

**What changed.**
- `models/nba_first_half_total_luck.py`: a ceiling at the full-game model's cut
  (`FULL_GAME_GAP_CUT`); a game at 3.0 or more is decided and recorded `full_game_model_bets`, no bet.
  The replay passes `gap_ceiling=None` so it still reproduces the graded cell.
- `scripts/nba_game_totals_door.py` (new): one bet a game across the three NBA game-total models.
  Each card's publish takes the shared lock before its own, re-reads the other two models' BETs, and
  writes a dropped game's decision row with n_bets 0 and `one_total_a_game:<model>_already_bet`.
- `scripts/nba_props_card.py`: `MAX_NBA_PROP_BETS_PER_NIGHT` 4 -> 6, still in tip order.
- Docs: `docs/thresholds.md` (both sections), `docs/nba_models_2026.md` section 14,
  `docs/sports/nba.md` (also corrects "both teams need 10 games", stale since 11-02), config comments.

**Verified.** New tests watched failing with the rule off (9 for the totals, 1 for the prop limit).
Full suite on the branch: the same 10 failures as master by name (none in a touched file), plus 3
tests that pinned four a night (two in `test_nba_prop_market.py`, one in `test_nba_s_postseason.py`,
whose control reuses the live door, so a re-run of that search now uses six). They now read the limit;
their three files re-run: 189 passed.

**Deliberately not done.** The first-half uplift was not refitted on the kept 272 bets (in-sample).
Skipping each game's top-2% prop for its next one graded better at 6-8 a night but is a peak, not a
plateau. No void or rewrite of any written pick (the 11-02/11-04 pairs stand).
## 2026-11-05 — iOS Sign in with Apple is the system sheet

**Why.** TestFlight Apple sign-in opened `ASWebAuthenticationSession` against `*.supabase.co`. The supported iOS path is the native sheet and `signInWithIdToken`.

**What changed.** `signInWithProvider('apple')` on iOS calls `expo-apple-authentication` (`signInAsync` with full name and email) and `supabase.auth.signInWithIdToken({ provider: 'apple', token: identityToken })`. The sign-in screen uses the system Continue with Apple button when that module is present. A missing module keeps a white button with black text and does not open the browser. Any other Apple failure is one sentence. Google stays on the in-app browser PKCE flow. Android still does not show the Apple button (`showAppleOn` is `['ios']`). An Apple identity re-authenticating on Android still uses the browser. The native module is a dynamic `require` after an iOS check, not a static import. `app.json` version is `1.2.0` (`runtimeVersion` follows `appVersion`), `ios.usesAppleSignIn` is true, and the `expo-apple-authentication` plugin is listed. Bundle id stays `com.mja.bettingpicks`. Email OTP and the App Review demo path are unchanged. The Supabase dashboard, EAS, and App Store Connect were not touched.

**Verified.** `npx tsx scripts/verify_auth.ts` (70) and `npx tsx scripts/verify_app_review_sign_in.ts` (49). `tsc` reports no errors in the touched app files. The pre-existing `queries.ts` and `scripts/` errors remain.

**Not done.** No new EAS binary. Until a 1.2.0 iOS production build is on TestFlight, installed 1.1.0 binaries keep the browser Apple flow. Supabase Apple Client IDs must include `com.mja.bettingpicks`, with the Services ID still first, or the native token's audience is rejected.

## 2026-11-05 — byom_legs forces a custom plan

**Why.** #1172 Medium, Matt approved via CE. Production `byom_legs` (read 2026-11-05, after #1172 had applied) has `byom_prop_lookup_v1` in the body and `proconfig` of only `search_path=public, pg_temp`. PostgREST reuses a pooled connection. After a few calls, plpgsql can cache a generic plan for `RETURN QUERY`. A generic plan of `(v_markets IS NULL OR p.market = ANY(v_markets))` does not use `idx_byom_prop_facts_market`, so the closing branch goes back to reading the whole season. The file's skip matched the marker and would have left that setting off.

**What changed.** `byom_prop_lookup_2026_11_04.sql` sets `plan_cache_mode = force_custom_plan` on `byom_legs`, next to `SET search_path`. The skip now also requires `plan_cache_mode=force_custom_plan` in `proconfig`, so the already-applied body is replaced once. Same answers. No index, threshold, stake, or pause change. The CREATE INDEX write-block note and the anon EXPLAIN of NBA player_points were left out.

**Verified.** The migration text carries the SET on the function and the proconfig predicate in the skip. A scratch cluster that resets the setting, reapplies the file, and reapplies it again keeps `search_path` and `plan_cache_mode`, and the second pass does not change `xmin`.

## 2026-11-04 — NBA: the in-play game-state model goes live, the quarters 1-2 spread and its loop

**Why.** mike: "everything goes live now, that's why we do back testing ... and you continually improve models as you
get more data." The third NBA search's game-state model (IG-2, record 7.3) passed the house standard in six cells,
which are three rules, and was never built because no NBA in-play loop existed.

**What changed.** One live model, `nba_live_spread_q12` (the live spread in quarters 1-2, EV >= 0.05 at the best of
the eight deciding books, -200 floor). The quarter-3 moneyline and spread (`nba_live_ml_q3`, `nba_live_spread_q3`)
were built with it and are held out (below). `models/nba_live_ig2.py` decides by calling the graded code for every step; the artifact
(`models/saved/nba_live_ig2_2027.json`) is IG-2's state model fit on every held season 2020-21 to 2025-26 and the
prior's distribution. `models/nba_live_loop.py` (job `nba_live`, pollers) fetches at each hour minus 264 seconds
(the archive's hourly purchase), reads ESPN's free play-by-play twice, 170 and 230 seconds after the fetch, decides
once per game and rule into `nba_live_decisions`, locks a bet, and posts it to the NBA live channel. The live id is
in every config registry and in `LIVE_MODELS` for settlement. NBA rows the refresh pass receives after the tip are now
stored `in_play` (on 2026-11-02, 5,735 of 16,508 `open` full-game NBA rows were stamped after the tip). The quarter-4
spread is not built: it failed the placebo bar.

**Two independent reviews, and what changed for each.**
- *Fetch times.* The first build also fetched at every listed tip, as the archive's :30 gap-fill purchases did. The
  loop cannot rebuild those (2026-27's `commence_time` is the actual start), so it now fetches only at each hour minus
  264 seconds, retries a failed attempt every 60 seconds up to 240 seconds after it, and writes each failed attempt as
  a `skipped` row with its reason; a fetch with no fresh quote is a `carried` row. A restart seeds the quotes it already
  fetched from its own `odds` rows.
- *ESPN's publication delay.* Measured free on the 2026-11-04 slate (8 games, 629 plays first seen live): median 34
  seconds from a play's wallclock to the summary serving it, 99th percentile 600; scoring plays median 39, 99th
  percentile 168, longest 219. The loop reads at 170 seconds, again at 230, and fails closed when the second read
  carries a play logged before the fetch that the first did not, when ESPN's header score is ahead of its plays on a
  re-read, or when ESPN's clock runs while its newest play is over 230 seconds old. Replayed on the measured plays
  (153 fetch moments): no scoring play missed by both reads; 5.2% of moments skipped; 18.3% still miss a non-scoring
  play ESPN served more than 230 seconds late (the score never wrong; 2 moments miss a technical foul). The old single
  45-second read missed a scoring play at 5.2% of moments.
- *Operations.* NBA live picks never fall back to the shared live channel; with no NBA live channel set the loop decides
  nothing and its job fails. A pick is announced only while its game is in progress. Each game is evaluated in its own
  try (a failure is that game's `declined` row). The loop beats `job_heartbeats`, and `system_health` reads its outputs
  the next morning (`nba_live_outputs`). `--dry-run` makes no paid call; the loop stops fetching under 1,000 credits or
  15% of the plan; missing offer values are stored as null; a spread of 0 is labelled PK. EV is rounded before the book
  choice so a tie always goes to the same book. Variables: `docs/cloud_worker.md`.

**The re-grade on the loop's own fetch times** (hourly purchases only, the shipped rule, section 9's house standard
with 200 placebo runs, `scripts/nba_live_ig2_regrade.py`). Quarters 1-2 spread: 1,348 bets, +96.6 units, +6.4%,
interval +1.4 to +11.3, 5 of 5 seasons, passes every bar. Quarter-3 moneyline: 503 bets, +26.2 units, +8.3%, -2.7 to
+19.3, 4 of 5, fails the interval and the bands. Quarter-3 spread: 813 bets, +41.2 units, +4.5%, -2.0 to +11.0, 4 of 5,
fails the interval, the bands and the placebo. The same pipeline on every purchase (the comparator) reads 1,417 / 634
/ 991 bets; it differs from record 7.3 (1,475 / 651 / 1,055) by the 2026-27 search's stable join and its
far-from-Pinnacle and clock-jump rules, with 93 to 96% of record 7.3's bets the same bet. Record 7.3 itself would fail
section 9's bands bar in all three.

**The decision** (mike's standing rule that the backtest is the gate, applied by the coordinator, 2026-11-04): only
the quarters 1-2 spread ships. The quarter-3 moneyline and spread are held out the way the quarter-4 spread is: not in
the loop's rule list (`models/nba_live_ig2.HELD_OUT_RULES`), not in config's live models, thresholds or EV floors, not
in the artifact's registered rules (it lists them under `held_out_rules`; the state model, prior and golden grid are
unchanged by the rebuild). Their code path and the re-grade script stay, so each is re-graded as this season's rows
accumulate and added the day it passes. EV 0.08 is no fallback (the moneyline fails the bands there, the spread the
interval). Three new checks were broken once and watched failing: a quarter-3 rule back in the live list, a quarter-3
id back in a config registry, and the output health check counting three rules.

**What was verified.** On record 7.3's own table the serving decision reproduces every graded bet (1,753 / 768 / 1,210
over 2020-21 to 2025-26), over the whole table and one game at a time through the loop's own path. On 2026-27's live
rows at the loop's fetch times (95 moments), each fetch seeing the plays logged by its served time plus 230 seconds
makes every decision, at the same fetch, that a replay knowing every play makes (78 / 30 / 34), plus 2 quarters 1-2
decisions on the two clock-jump games, one of them a bet (`NBA_2026-10-20_PHI_NYK`). The fetch times move the record:
the search's every-10-minute fetches bet 36 / 29 / 51 times, the loop's fetch times 35 / 11 / 16, only 7 / 3 / 6 the
same bet. Every rule of the first build was broken once and its test watched failing (20 of 20), and every review fix
likewise (20 of 20). Full suite on the branch (2:24, one run): 26 failures. Run alone on the master this branch contains (87ef8b70; master is 10 commits further on), 9 of them fail there too. 2 more pass alone on the branch (the social-post schedule tests, timing under load). 14 were this change: the live-publish test's `games` table lacked `home_score`, which production has and the new in-progress filter reads; the fixture now carries it and all 14 pass. The last, the app read-surface test, fails because the front-end commit stopped reading `v_latest_inplay_odds_all_books`; it is flagged, not fixed here.

**Named differences from the grade.** No BookMoveClock (one quote per book per fetch; the grade's freshness rule
instead); the clock-jump rule applies to plays known so far; ESPN's late non-scoring plays above.

**Not done here.** No production write, no paid call. The app side (labels, the Live board counting NBA, the
threshold mirror, the deciding book on in-play picks) was done on this branch by a separate front-end change. Before
the merge (it needs nothing from this branch): the NBA live Discord channel (`discord_create_channel`, a copy of
#🔴-ncaaf-live, then `DISCORD_CHANNEL_LIVE_NBA` on pollers); without it the loop decides nothing. After the merge: the
migration pass, the threshold sync after both deploys, the artifact's `--store` then `--register` with an absolute
path.

**App, after the second design review (2026-11-05).** The two held-out models left the app's labels, timing copy
and the price-slot list, and the thresholds mirror was regenerated. The review found one blocker: the betslip
called every in-play leg's price "DraftKings', ~45s old", beside a leg priced at BetMGM's lock. The note now names
no book. Its should-fix items were also done:
- the NBA deciding book's hourly row no longer becomes an "Open with" price in the slip (`liveBookRowsForPick`);
- an in-play card off the member's books reads "Found at MGM -108 · not one of your books";
- the chip is "LIVE Spread", like every other in-play chip;
- the Live board's copy is singular, and drops the "~45s" strip and the price banner when only NBA cards are on it.

TypeScript errors are identical to master: the same 27, none in the touched files. The slip test was watched
failing. `v_latest_inplay_odds_all_books` left the app's read list because the app stopped reading it; the grant
sweep revokes write verbs only, so older app bundles keep their SELECT. Pollers' variable names, read 2026-11-05,
include `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` and both NCAAB live channel ids. So a `DISCORD_CHANNEL_LIVE_NBA` set there resolves, and
the docs that said pollers had no token were corrected.

**Full suite on the branch with master merged (2026-11-05):** 12 failed, 10,435 passed, 78 skipped, in 1 h 45 min.
- One failure was this branch's: master's new `open_picks` view must name every model the app names, and the NBA
  early live spread was missing. It was added, with the view's hash marker moved, so the migration pass re-applies it.
- Nine fail the same way on `origin/master` (ad18b731):
  - the `config-topology` rule's `.env` scope (no `.env` in a worktree);
  - the coverage probe's slip check;
  - the NHL skater sort claim;
  - the closing-line book names;
  - the NHL totals card lock;
  - the unique-row card insert;
  - two price pre-filter rollbacks;
  - the web record math.
- Two scheduler tests passed when run alone; they failed on timing under load.
## 2026-11-04 — BYOM NBA prop records over the 3s anon limit: two indexes and a market filter on the closing branch

**Why.** The post-merge check of #1169 (measured as anon) found the NBA backfill done. `byom-nba-game-facts` took 17m14s and `byom-nba-prop-facts` took 34m06s. There were 408,866 NBA prop facts and 0 unscored 2026-27 games, and `byom_official_legs` was 925 MB. But `byom_record(2026, NBA player_points over)` took 3,687 ms, past the 3s anon limit. The closing half took 1,682 ms because it read the whole season's prop facts before the market filter; one market reads in 41 ms. The official half took 1,978 ms because the join to `byom_prop_facts` on (canonical_key, market, player_key) had no index: 1,949 ms and 895,998 rows removed by the join filter. NFL pass-yards over fell from 831 ms to 348 ms after #1169.

**What changed.** `byom_prop_lookup_2026_11_04.sql` (ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS) indexes `byom_prop_facts` on (canonical_key, market, player_key) and on (sport, season, market). It replaces `byom_legs`: the closing prop branch filters on market and over/under, and the official prop join filters on sport and season. The same answers come back; the scratch equivalence test compares against the lock-bands body on 14 filters, including props.

**To verify after deploy.** Time the same NBA call as anon.

## 2026-11-04 — open picks are one view any Claude chat reads; nothing is pasted

**Why.** `docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md` told a person to paste a prompt and a query into a claude.ai "Betting" project.
Matt set one up once; mike has none, and was wrongly told to paste into it. mike: no pasting anywhere, ever.

**What changed.** `public.open_picks` (`data/migrations/open_picks_view_2026_11_04.sql`, registered before the
posted-stake pair): one row per open pre-game BET, ordered by start time. Only bets the app shows: Discord has posted
it (CLAUDE.md 1b, the app shows a bet once the channel has it), and its model is not paused. Discord posts only what
clears the model's cut in `model_action_thresholds` at the decided price, and a posted bet keeps the cuts it was
written under, so the view re-applies no cut. Out: graded, in-play, started, VOID, DUPLICATE, written while paused.
Columns: sport, Eastern date and start, matchup, the label as stored, decided price and book, the published stake as
text and as two numbers, the app's model name, decided time in Eastern, pick id and model id. A first version also
showed a bet that cleared its cut before Discord posted it; the main session reversed that the same day, so the view
matches the app exactly.
security_invoker, SELECT for anon and authenticated, declared in `data/anon_readable.py`. The skip compares the view's
comment to a hash of its body. The prompt doc now says to ask for open picks and names the view. Three tests that
pinned the pasted prompt now read the view; the live-odds market mapping that only the prompt carried is gone with it.

**Verified on production (read-only).** At 5:18 PM ET the first version returned 16 rows: NFL 5, NHL 5, NCAAF 4,
NCAAB 2, all posted to Discord. At 6:06 PM ET the posted-only version returned 23: the same 16, plus 7 picks decided
between 5:54 and 6:05 PM ET (NBA 6, NCAAB 1). The app's filter written out separately (posted, model not paused or
retired, not a paused row or duplicate) selects the same 23, none on one side only, and no bet that cleared its cut
was waiting to be posted. Left out: 10 NCAAF VOIDs Discord never
posted, and 2 NCAAF moneyline picks written while that model was paused. The stake text matched Discord's
`fmt_stake` for 21 prices. 11 ms, on the picks signal index. Every dependency is readable by anon and authenticated.

## 2026-11-04 — a 0% same-book close is a CLV push

**Why.** Mike: when the same book's close equals the price taken on the same line, that is a push. It does not count against the beat rate. Headline beat rate is beats / (beats + losses). Nov 3 NCAAB was printing 1/8 with seven of those closes at 0%.

**What changed.** `clv_outcome` in `tracking/clv_math.py` uses half a hundredth of a percent, the grain `clv_pct` is stored at. Discord, X, Eggz, and the period recaps share `clv_line`. A day with pushes reads `50% beat close (1/2, 7 push)`. Zero pushes omit that clause. An all-push day is `0 measured, N push` and does not divide. The model-quality beat rate uses the same split. The mean still includes the zero. `v_public_track_record` gains `clv_push`, `clv_game_push`, and `clv_prop_push`. `v_opening_vs_live` gains `clv_push` when that view exists. The app's Record beat rate, Sharp Score pedigree, model-detail tile, and opening comparison use beats / (settled − push). `avg_clv_pct` is unchanged. Market CLV stays the secondary column. No threshold, stake, grade, unit, record, or pause change. The migration does not alter `picks` or rewrite `results_snapshots`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_clv_push_beat_rate.py` and the recap tally tests. The migration text is unchanged by the SQL adapter, skips when `AS clv_push` is already in the view, and sits before the posted-stake pair.

## 2026-11-04 — NCAAB first-half CLV uses a later pre-tip row

**Why.** NCAAB first-half picks (`ncaab_first_half_total`, `ncaab_first_half_spread`, markets `totals_h1` / `spreads_h1`) were stamped `standard_v2` with every closing field empty. The 2026-11-03 NCAAB beat-close line was 1/1 with 13 picks. `tracking/paper_tracker._closing_odds` accepted only `snapshot_type = 'close'` for those markets, the rule copied from the NBA first-half and team-total feeds. `data/ingestors/ncaab_first_half_odds.py` never writes a close row; every pull is `open`.

**What changed.** For NCAAB `totals_h1` / `spreads_h1`, the close is the bet book's newest pre-tip row on the same line pulled strictly after the pick was created. The decision pull is not the close. No such row leaves the pick unmeasured. NBA first half and team totals still read `close` rows only. The measurement write goes through `_real_close`: a null close does not set `standard_v2`. The migration's empty stamp skips these two models, and a started game with no post-creation row is left out of the recompute and the Python backfill scan so it does not pin the queue. `scripts/recompute_empty_clv.py` lists picks already stamped `standard_v2` with an empty close. It is a dry run until `--apply`, and it takes `--date` and `--sport`. It was not run.

**Verified.** `tests/test_ncaab_first_half_clv.py`: a same-book pull 10 minutes before tip, after the pick was created, is the total's close; a spread with no pull after its creation is not updated; an NBA first half ignores a later `open` row and reads the `close` row; a null close, including an older method on an NCAAB first half, is not stamped. Related CLV tests passed (137).

**Not done.** The script was not run against production. The six-hour empty stamp remains for other markets, and an NBA bet with no league tip is still stamped empty after two days, so those queues still drain. No threshold, stake, pause, record, or recap-format change.

## 2026-11-04 — a damaged byom_legs body is rebuilt with jsonb_exists

**Why.** #1161 (`56307150`) already skips `byom_official_lock_bands_2026_11_04.sql` on `to_regprocedure('public.byom_legs(integer, jsonb, text)')` when `position('p_filters %s' in p.prosrc) = 0`. The CREATE OR REPLACE body on that commit still spells the five key tests as a bare jsonb key operator. Rebuilding a damaged body would write that operator again.

**What changed.** The skip stays that one `to_regprocedure` match plus the position predicate, once. The five key tests in the replacement call `jsonb_exists`. The file has no `p_filters ?`. No prop-filter UI from #1161. No production write.

**Verified.** `tests/test_byom_official_legs.py`: a healthy body keeps its `xmin` through an adapted re-apply; a body rewritten from `jsonb_exists` to `p_filters %s` is replaced once, then skipped, and `byom_record` with primetime still runs. The static guard asserts one `to_regprocedure` and one position predicate, and that the body after RETURN calls `jsonb_exists` and does not contain `p_filters ?`.

## 2026-11-04 — BYOM preview 400s: the DB adapter rewrote `?` inside function bodies; props lose favorite/underdog, team spread, game total

**Why.** Matt reported "Couldn't load this preview" on the NFL builder. Edge logs showed `POST /rpc/byom_backtest` returning 400 from 14:03 to 14:04 UTC. The postgres log said `type "s" does not exist`, in `byom_legs` line 83: `p_filters %s 'primetime'`. The worker applies `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` through `data.db`'s SQLite-dialect adapter. That adapter turns every `?` outside a single-quoted literal into `%s`, and it did not treat a `$$ ... $$` body as quoted. So the jsonb key operator in both 11-04 BYOM migrations was stored as `%s`. Measured: `_adapt_sql` changes 5 `?` in each file. Matt's account replaced the body at 14:17:44 (`byom_legs_jsonb_exists_fix_2026_11_04`, via the management API). The anon `byom_record` call returns rows again: home underdog primetime, close, 7-1, +6.46u.

**What changed.**
- `data/db.py`: `_SQL_SKIP_RE` also skips dollar-quoted bodies (`$$...$$`, `$tag$...$tag$`). A `?` or `:name` inside a function body is never a placeholder. `tests/test_db_adapt_sql.py` covers this, including every ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS file passing the adapter unchanged. The three new tests failed with the old `data/db.py`.
- Props (Matt): "favorites and underdog don't fit and should only be relevant for teams", and "game total and game spread don't fit player props". The prop step no longer has Favorite/Underdog. The Odds sheet no longer has Game spread or Game total. `filtersForDraft` drops `role` and the four band keys for a prop, including keys already stored on a model. `draftFromFilters` reads a prop's role as none. Measured: `byom_models` holds no rows, so no saved model changed. The server still accepts those keys, so an older app build keeps working.

**Why it came back (15:00).** `byom_official_lock_bands_2026_11_04.sql` guarded on `pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) = 'integer, jsonb, text'`. That function returns `'p_season integer, p_filters jsonb, p_timing text'`, so the guard never matched, and the file re-created `byom_legs` on every worker pass, through the adapter each time. The guard now uses `to_regprocedure`. `position_defense_ranks_nfl_2026_10_18.sql` had the same guard and dropped and recreated `player_season_stat_values_nfl` and `player_h2h_stat_values_nfl` on every pass. It is fixed the same way. A test scans every ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS file for the pattern, and a scratch test applies the BYOM files a second time through the adapter and checks `byom_legs` is untouched. Both failed on master's files.

**Not done.** The two migration files still spell the operator `?`. Their guards skip in production, and the adapter no longer rewrites them. The official-line record is incomplete until `byom-nfl-game-facts-official-*` finishes. That job has been reclaimed twice, because each merge redeploys the worker.

## 2026-11-04 — an applied BYOM lock-bands migration is a catalog read

**Why.** `dispatch:apply-view-migrations` failed on the 14:17 and 15:17 UTC hourly passes (pipeline_log 229040, 94/95, 29.5s; 229108, 95/96, 29.9s). Both errors name `byom_official_lock_bands_2026_11_04.sql` and `ALTER TABLE public.byom_official_legs ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, cancelled by lock timeout. Production PostgreSQL 17.6, read the same afternoon: `pg_get_function_identity_arguments` for `public.byom_legs` is `p_season integer, p_filters jsonb, p_timing text`. Comparing that to `integer, jsonb, text` is false, and `prosrc` already contains `byom_official_lock_bands_v1` and `byom_official_legs_v1`. The guard never returned, so every pass ran the ALTER (AccessExclusiveLock even with IF NOT EXISTS) and the CREATE OR REPLACE, and lost the 5s lock_timeout whenever a reader held the table. The same nameless comparison is in `position_defense_ranks_nfl_2026_10_18.sql`. Production identity arguments there are `p_season integer, p_stat text` and `p_seasons integer[], p_stat text, p_teams text[], p_opponents text[]`, and both results already contain `pos`, so those two drops were running on every pass too. `byom_official_legs_2026_11_04.sql` does not compare identity arguments. The lock-read index returns on `to_regclass`.

**What changed.** The three guards compare the named identity form. An already-applied file returns on that catalog read, before ALTER, DROP, or CREATE OR REPLACE. A fresh database still runs the DDL: the bands marker is absent until this file replaces `byom_legs`, and the stat functions skip only once `pos` is on the result.

**Verified.** Local PostgreSQL 16.15 returned the same named identity and the old file hit lock timeout while another session held ACCESS SHARE on `byom_official_legs`. After the change, that re-apply commits inside a 200ms `lock_timeout`, and the columns and primary key are present. Tests cover the named literal, that locked re-apply, and a scan of every migration that rejects a types-only identity comparison. No production write.

**Not done.** The worker applies the corrected file on the next refresh. No threshold, stake, pause, or record change.

## 2026-11-04 — CFBD 429 backoff, and BYOM odds reads no longer sit in the official-leg write

**Why.** The 2026-11-04 daily (`pipeline_runs`, started 14:05:51Z) failed `ncaaf_stats`: CFBD returned 429 on `/stats/season/advanced` for 2026 week 2, and `_get` slept 5s, 10s, then 15s and returned None. The weekly loop then aborted the season so it would not overwrite good rows with NULL. Hourly passes at 14:17Z and 15:17Z failed `apply-view-migrations` with `canceling statement due to lock timeout`. `worker_jobs` 977490 (`byom_game_facts`) ran 14:10:11Z–14:44:41Z and wrote official legs. 977491 (`byom_prop_facts`) was claimed at 15:00:13Z, died, was reclaimed, and claimed again at 15:35:12Z. Read at 15:43Z: pid 615178, `xact_start` 15:35:25Z, state `active`, granted `RowExclusiveLock` on `byom_official_legs`, query the DraftKings odds read (`SELECT o.game_id, o.bookmaker, o.market`). The builders write `byom_official_legs` and then read odds on the same connection, and commit only after every season. Autocommit is off, so the DELETE/INSERT lock stays held for the rest of the build, including while Python runs the lock search (idle in transaction) and while the next season's odds query is running.

**What changed.**
- `data/ingestors/cfbd_ingestor.py` `_get` tries six times. A positive `Retry-After` (delta-seconds or HTTP-date) is the wait. Otherwise the wait is 5, 10, 20, 40, then 60 seconds, capped at 60. One call stops if the next sleep would pass 150s, and one season's snapshot shares a 600s clock. A None payload still aborts the snapshot instead of writing NULL efficiency. `CFBD_REQUEST_PAUSE` default is 1.0s (was 0.6). The env still overrides. No Railway variable was set.
- `byom/load.py` and `byom/prop_load.py` commit when `apply` is true, at the end of one season, before the next season's odds read. `_fetch` rolls back a clean read before the caller uses the rows. `byom/refresh.py` uses that: the prop odds read follows the game-leg commit.
- The three BYOM jobs open their connection with `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` of 60s. It is a libpq option on that connection, replayed on reconnect. It is not a role or server setting. Other connections in the process are unchanged.

**Verified.** pytest on the CFBD ingestor, the open-transaction tests, db reconnect, the idle-read tests, the transaction-pooler tests, and the BYOM job tests that patch `get_connection`. The weekly abort test still raises on a None advanced payload and does not write.

**Not done.** The view-migration runner and the BYOM migration SQL were not edited. No production write, no backend terminate, no threshold, stake, or pause change.

## 2026-11-04 — covering index for the official lock read

**Why.** Follow-up to #1159 (squash `93cd9810`). After the lock-bands rebuild, one NFL prop season is about 0.5–0.7M candidate rows. `byom_legs` filters `sport`, `season` and `match_role`, then `DISTINCT ON (canonical_key, market, leg_side, player_key) ORDER BY` those columns plus `locked_at`. `idx_byom_official_legs_season` is `(sport, season, match_role, market)`. CE asked for the covering index as a migration. A live `CREATE INDEX` timed out twice.

**What changed.** `data/migrations/byom_official_legs_lock_read_idx_2026_11_04.sql` creates `idx_byom_official_legs_lock_read` on `(sport, season, match_role, canonical_key, market, leg_side, player_key, locked_at)`. Registered in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` immediately after the lock-bands file, so the worker applies it on the first pass after merge. The file returns on a catalog read once the index exists.

## 2026-11-04 — BYOM official lock includes the bands; empty official headline falls back to the close

**Why.** Follow-up to merged PR #1157 (`f384be77`). Matt: the official pick is the first DraftKings quote where the bet fit the rules, and a line, price, spread or total band is one of those rules. The lock was searched on role only, then the bands filtered that one quote. A missing `byom_official_legs` table failed inside the facts transaction and rolled the facts back. An empty official record headlined 0-0 while the close had the bets.

**Choice on the missing table.** Loud skip. `write_official_legs` checks `to_regclass` and the `context_at_lock` column. When either is absent it logs an error and writes no official rows. The facts upsert in the same transaction still commits. Failing the job would put the facts back to the stale record #1157 was fixing. Official rows are a second store; the card falls back to the close while they are absent.

**What changed.**
- `byom/official.py` stores one candidate per pre-kickoff snapshot (`game_lock_candidates`, `prop_lock_candidates`) with the role, team spread and game total in force then. `first_fit` is the earliest candidate that fits the role and the four bands. Example A: underdogs +3 to +7, open +2.5 then +3.5, locks +3.5. Example B: ML dogs +150 to +250, open +260 then +200, locks +200.
- `data/migrations/byom_official_lock_bands_2026_11_04.sql` adds `team_spread`, `game_total`, `context_at_lock`, extends the primary key with `locked_at`, and replaces `byom_legs` so the official row is that search. The function keeps `byom_official_legs_v1`, so the previous migration's guard still skips. Rows with `context_at_lock` false still read the closing spread and total, so a band does not drop bets until the rebuild writes candidates. The closing timing is unchanged.
- Declared jobs `byom-nfl-game-facts-official-lock-bands-2026-11-04` and `byom-nfl-prop-facts-official-lock-bands-2026-11-04` rewrite the candidates. Both enabled, apply true.
- The app's headline is the close when the official rows sum to 0 bets and the close has bets (`headlineFor`). The bet list opens on that headline.

**Not done.** Stale official rows when a fact stops assembling. Official `skipped` stays 0. `apply_view_migrations` already rolls back a failed file (`_apply_one`), so the aborted-transaction hunch was not changed. Moneyline role still comes from the spread clock only.

## 2026-11-04 — deploy-kills do not use the daily start cap

**Why.** `pipeline_runs` for 2026-11-04, read the same afternoon: four `run_kind=daily` rows, every one `failed_steps=aborted` — `3d3685c194b949d69243f13a1d3c52e7` (11:00:00Z–11:13:34Z), `8a44ef65d4364828af08e025aa292a51` (11:13:34Z–13:01:36Z), `c1869c1532714e769631f99e8b10225d` (13:01:40Z–13:19:13Z), `d5a270750a284c2e89a684051d73fe2a` (13:19:17Z–13:29:24Z). The cap of 4 ledgered starts then skipped the day. The 13:17Z hourly is absent; the last hourly is `ad42c98b05d7403dae57308177c792bb` at 12:17:00Z, finished ok. An open daily row does not block a refresh: that 12:17Z hourly finished while `8a44ef65` was still unfinished. The 13:01Z and 13:19Z boots called `catch_up_daily_pipeline` on the main thread before `sched.start()`, so the :17 cron was not registered while the daily ran.

**What changed.** A deploy-kill (aborted stamp, or `finished_at` still NULL) does not count toward the one-retry failure cap (`MAX_NON_ABORTED_FAILURES` = 2). Deploy-kills stop at 12 (`MAX_DEPLOY_KILLED_STARTS_PER_ET_DAY`) or at 22:00 ET. The ops note says which start this is and why (`deploy-kill`, `real-failure`, `missing`, or `manual`). The daily catch-up is a one-off job five seconds after the scheduler starts, so hourly and evening are not held behind it. `worker_jobs` job type `daily_pipeline` runs the full daily once under `_DAILY_LOCK` and is not declared.

**Not done.** No production row was written. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. The 22:00 ET cutoff also skips a daily that was never ledgered; the manual job is the kick after that.

## 2026-11-04 — BYOM NFL: official pick beside the close, main-model staking, the bet list, daily refresh

**Why.** Matt: the builder said home underdogs in primetime this season were 7-0, +7.0u. Measured in production: `byom_game_facts` was last built 2026-10-24 21:45 UTC, and 27 played 2026 games had no score in it. One of them matched (2026_07_DAL_PHI, PHI +185, lost 21-38), so the record was 7-1. `byom_backtest` staked 1u to win with no risk cap, and the app showed no list of bets. Matt asked for the bets to be shown, graded "on the locked betting line for that model", and for units to follow the main models.

**Decisions (Matt, in chat).** Staking: "Do what we do in the app on the main models", i.e. `stake_for`, 1u to win, risk capped at 3u. Line: "use both, it's all about when a line becomes an official pick", so the official line is the first DraftKings quote where the bet fit the model's rules (first match), and the close stays beside it. Refresh: "Yes, turn it on".

**What changed.**
- `byom/official.py`. For each leg and each role a model can ask for (any, favorite, underdog, pick'em), the first pre-kickoff DraftKings quote with an American price where the leg fits that role. The role comes from the DraftKings home spread in force at that snapshot. Graded at that quote's point. Props are the same at the player line, with the role of the player's team. Written to the new `byom_official_legs` by the game and prop builders, in the same transaction as the facts. NFL only.
- `data/migrations/byom_official_legs_2026_11_04.sql`, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`. Adds the table, `byom_leg_label` (the only place a bet's label is built from stored fields), `byom_risk_units` and `byom_win_units` (stake_for), `byom_legs` (one filter parser for both timings, with the Phase A keys), `byom_record` (per timing and market, with `risked`), and `byom_bets` (newest first, 500 per season, `total_bets`). It replaces `byom_backtest` with the closing rows of `byom_record`: same signature and columns, so the saved-model snapshot uses the cap too. The guard uses `to_regclass`, never `::regclass`.
- The BYOM jobs (`byom_game_facts`, `byom_prop_facts`, `byom_refresh`) run `apply_view_migrations` first, the same as the CLV backfill. `scheduler.run_byom_nfl_refresh` queues `byom_refresh` with apply true daily at 10:41 ET (dedupe key `byom-nfl-refresh-<date>`, pipeline role). Measured: `nfl_player_game_log` rows for Mon 10-26 and Mon 11-02 landed 06:02 and 06:24 ET the next morning. Declared jobs: `byom-nfl-refresh` on with apply true (catch-up), and one-time rebuilds `byom-nfl-game-facts-official-2026-11-04` and `byom-nfl-prop-facts-official-2026-11-04`.
- App. The card leads with the official-line record ("At the official line") and shows "At the closing line: …" under it. ROI now shows, because `byom_record` returns `risked`. "See the N bets" opens `ByomBetsScreen`: an Official/Closing toggle, one row per bet with the server label, market, matchup, date, price, result, and units at two decimals. The ranking on My models uses the same official number. The link is hidden while the server has no `byom_record`. NBA is graded at the close only.

**Verified.**
- Production `byom_backtest` (live body, read via `pg_get_functiondef`) was reproduced in a scratch Postgres on all 285 real 2025 NFL facts. Seven filters returned identical results: -33.72u h2h, -22.31u home h2h, and so on. The new function returns the same wins, losses, pushes, ties, and skipped on every filter. Units differ only on moneylines with a favorite shorter than -300: whole-season h2h -33.72 to -36.30, home h2h -22.31 to -26.20. Underdog-only filters are unchanged.
- The Phase A, prop-side, total-side, and NBA scratch tests are now parametrised to also run on the new body. They return the same answers. 114 BYOM tests pass, plus the new `tests/test_byom_official_legs.py` (first-match logic, the cap, labels and the away-spread sign, anon read-only).
- The mobile `tsc` error set is byte-identical to master (53, none in touched files). `ux_scan`: 0 findings. The UX review was run. Its blocker was fixed: rows of the old timing were briefly shown under the new toggle.

**Not done / caveats.**
- The migration and the backfills had not run at the time of writing. They apply on the worker's first pass after merge.
- An official lock is searched on role only. A line, price, spread, or total band filter is checked against the locked quote. It does not move the lock.
- The scratch-test runner refuses to start as root. The suite was run as a non-root user.

**Session summary (2026-11-04 — daily relaunch ceiling and the stale cutoff):**

Follow-up on the killed-daily catch-up, from the Chief Engineer review of that change. Two Mediums, before merge.

**What changed.** The 75-minute stale cutoff in `abort_prior_refresh_orphans` now closes `run_kind = 'daily'` only. An hourly or evening this process opened stays unfinished when the daily lock is free, so `refresh_pass_completion` can still see a hang past 2 hours. Aborted dailies relaunch until 4 ledgered starts (`MAX_DAILY_STARTS_PER_ET_DAY`), not 8. A daily that finishes and fails before the MLB freshness steps for any other reason gets one retry (`MAX_NON_ABORTED_FAILURES` = 2), then stops. The ops note names which start this attempt is (`Start N of 4`).

**Not done.** The review's Lows (start-order of the in-progress health detail, the date on an ancient open row, the health-check note, a failing UPDATE still deciding) were left. No production row was updated. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. `graphify update .` refused to overwrite `graphify-out/graph.json` (42377 nodes vs 42872 on disk) and was not forced.

**Session summary (2026-11-04 — a killed daily is aborted and started again):**

The 6:00am ET daily on 2026-11-04 never finished. `pipeline_runs` `3d3685c194b949d69243f13a1d3c52e7` started 11:00:00Z and was stamped `failed_steps=aborted` at 11:13:34Z, the same second `8a44ef65d4364828af08e025aa292a51` started. That second row was still unfinished at 12:34Z. `pipeline_log` has the first run's steps through 11:09Z and no daily steps after that until the 12:17Z hourly, which finished. `job_heartbeats.daily_pipeline` last ok is 2026-11-03 11:39Z. Finished dailies from 2026-10-15 through 2026-11-03 ran 23.8–47.5 minutes (longest `16c6612cbcf54ca8b922c6959b7825b6`).

There is no SIGTERM handler. A killed run stays `finished_at` NULL until the next same-kind `start_run` or the boot sweep. The boot sweep closed hourly and evening only, so the 11:13Z daily stayed open. `decide_daily_retry` counted that open row toward a cap of 2 and returned skip before the unfinished branch. The in-process lock was not held in the new container. Nothing in this path is a database lease.

**What changed.** Boot and the daily catch-up stamp unfinished `daily` rows from a previous process, and a daily older than 75 minutes when the daily lock is free, `ok=false`, `failed_steps=aborted`. The catch-up then starts today's daily when none completed the MLB freshness steps and this process is not already in one. This commit's ceiling was 8 ledgered starts; the follow-up the same day lowered it to 4 and limited the 75-minute cutoff to `daily` (the entry above). `daily_pipeline_completion` is CRIT when an unfinished daily is past 75 minutes, or when 75 minutes have passed since 6:00am ET with no successful daily and none in progress. `refresh_pass_completion` still waits 2 hours.

**Not done.** No production row was updated from this session. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change. `graphify update .` refused to overwrite `graphify-out/graph.json` (42301 nodes vs 42872 on disk) and was not forced.

## 2026-11-04 — App Review rate limit ignores a faked forwarded hop

**Why.** The per-caller bucket used the first `x-forwarded-for` hop. That hop is chosen by the caller, so a new value on each request was a new bucket and the six-digit guesses were not capped.

**What changed.** The bucket is `cf-connecting-ip` when the edge sets it, otherwise the last forwarded hop. More than 200 failures in an hour, from every caller together, is refused before the code is compared, on top of the per-caller cap. The migration was not changed and was not applied.

**Verified.** `npx tsx scripts/verify_app_review_sign_in.ts`. A faked first hop does not change the bucket hash. Not run against production Supabase.

**Not done.** Secrets were not set. The function was not deployed. No model, threshold, stake, or pause change.

**Why.** The repo is public. The first draft of the review sign-in committed a six-digit code in the metadata, the session log, and the tests. Once the secret is set, that code would sign in as the review account.

Tests fail if a six-digit token appears in the review metadata or the review sources. They do not assert a particular code. Wrong codes are counted per caller (SHA-256 of the address, not the address). A full bucket is refused before the code is compared. Master was merged so CI can run. The migration is still not in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` and was not applied.

**Verified.** `npx tsx scripts/verify_app_review_sign_in.ts` and `pytest --noconftest tests/test_app_review_sign_in.py`. Not run against production Supabase.

**Session summary (2026-11-04 — the All board shows every scored NCAAB game, and the day's read is paged):**

The brief (Matt, 2026-11-03, with a screenshot of NCAAB All (7) = Signals (7)): *"We're only showing signal picks. The all tab should have all picks."* Measured first (Supabase, read only): on 2026-11-03 NCAAB had 7 rows in `picks`, all BET, and every NCAAB pick ever written was a BET. The app was not filtering: all six `scripts/ncaab_*_card.py` wrote only BETs. Matt approved writing the rest ("Yes").

**What changed.**
- Five NCAAB cards (`ncaab_total_blend`, `ncaab_spread_market`, `ncaab_moneyline_own_spread`, `ncaab_first_half_spread`, `ncaab_first_half_total`) write a NONE row for each game they priced and did not bet (`below_cut`; spreads also `too_few_books`; any bet the price or EV floor refused), at the decision's book, side and number, `kelly_fraction` / `recommended_bet` 0, in the same transaction as the decision row. No row for unpriced decisions, `edge_cap`, unbettable books or a refused label. Never AVOID. Ledger `n_bets` and the returned bet count count BETs only. `docs/sports/ncaab.md` "Every scored game is a row".
- `ncaab_moneyline_first_post` stays BET-only (`pick_rows(..., scored=False)`): it decides a game once per book's first post, and the picks unique key (game, model, side) means an early NONE row would swallow a later post's BET under `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.
- App, `fetchPicksForDate`: the day's picks are two paged reads (was one `.limit(5000)` request, silently capped at 1,000). Signals (`signal_type <> 'NONE'`) must load, as before; NONE rows are enrichment, reported to the partial banner on failure, and awaited after the line shop. Busiest days measured: 1,432 pre-game rows on 2026-09-19 (1,355 NONE); BET+AVOID at most 764 (2026-05-14). The `authenticator` role carries no `pgrst.db_max_rows` override, so the platform's 1,000 applies.
- App, `fetchUpcomingNcaabPicks`: lines and the line shop are read only for BET/AVOID games (signal cards are the only ones that draw them), and a failure reaches the partial banner instead of an empty catch.

**Why it is safe to write NONE rows.** Discord, push (BET; its AVOID path needs an `opening_signals` row), X, settlement (`paper_tracker`, all BET) and the record select BETs. `games_with_a_bet` / `decided_games` read BETs and the ledger, so a NONE row never re-opens or closes a game.

**UX review (frontend-ux-designer).** Ship with fixes. Applied: (1) a late NONE page that timed out would have blanked Signals: split read; (2) the NCAAB look-ahead fetched all-books lines for every priced game and swallowed failures: narrowed and reported. Not applied, decision for Matt: (3) NONE cards (NHL, NCAAF already, now NCAAB) show the betslip button and VoiceOver calls them "this bet". Not applied: show a floor-refusal reason on a NONE card (needs a stored reason column read by PickCard). Scan: 2 files, 0 findings.

**Verified.**
- Each new or changed check watched failing on origin/master code first: 7 refused-bet unit tests, 3 new below-cut tests, 2 database assertions (spread, moneyline cards), the 2 paging pins.
- Database tests on a scratch Postgres 16 (one fresh database per file): spread, moneyline and first-post cards pass. The first-half spread / first-half total / totals card database tests fail identically on master (missing `ncaab_first_half_fetches` in their fixture; totals 2 of 4): pre-existing, not touched.
- Full suite, branch vs origin/master: identical failure set except 4 `test_ncaab_quote_bounds.py` tests that asserted "no row" for a game decided below the cut after a bad quote was dropped; they now assert no BET and any NONE row is never at the dropped quote's book. After the last commit, the 77 test files touching NCAAB, `queries.ts`, `useTodayPicks` or session docs: same failure set as master (1 failed, 22 errors, all environment), 2,028 passed vs 2,022.
- `tsc --noEmit`: 29 errors vs master's 30; the one gone is the old picks cast. No new error.

**Not done.** No backfill of NONE rows for games decided before this ships (the ledgers hold the decisions). `graphify update .` not run: the CLI is not installed in this sandbox. The other card-script models (NFL, NBA first half and friends) were not checked for the same BET-only pattern.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — the Claude-mobile picks prompt shows every open pick, at the line it was decided at; one session-log hash; the code graph rebuilt with every group named):**

The day's NCAAB review found two wrong lines in the Claude-mobile picks prompt (`docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md`, flagged in the NCAAB model-pages entry below). Docs and graph only: no code file changes, so the merge skips the worker redeploy.

**What changed (before -> after).**
- Which picks the prompt reads. Before: `WHERE p.game_date = '{today_et}'`, so a pick posted the day before for a later game (an NCAAB total decided at DraftKings' first post, an NCAAF total written days ahead) did not show until game day, and a started game's pick still showed. After: every open pick, decided at any time: `p.result IS NULL`, the game not started (`g.commence_time::timestamptz > NOW()`), a game with no start time bounded to today, pre-game only, the same Discord-producer bounds. The cut is unchanged (the `model_action_thresholds` join, VOID excluded), plus the two app filter clauses the prompt lacked: a row written while its model was paused, and the second copy of one bet. The game's date is now selected and shown in the Game Time column ("YYYY-MM-DD h:mm AM/PM ET"). The DraftKings quote read is limited to the games that carry an open pick, so it stays on the odds index.
- The closing reminder. Before: "Picks may flip to AVOID on later refreshes — re-query before placing bets. Lines refresh hourly 6am–6pm ET, then every 10 minutes until 11pm." After: "Each pick stands at the line and price it was decided at. It is never re-priced or withdrawn when the line moves. The live DK columns show the line now: if it has moved, check your book's price before you bet." (CLAUDE.md §1c.)
- The same false claim in two more places: the F5 note "⚠ Borderline (probability may shift on next hourly refresh)" -> "⚠ Borderline probability"; the rule "Never bet a pick that's flipped to AVOID" -> a BET is never withdrawn or turned into AVOID when the line moves. The zero-row line no longer names a date.
- `scripts/emit_threshold_sql.py` is not part of this prompt (the prompt joins the table), so there is no generated SQL to change.
- `docs/sessions/2026-11.md`, the `ncaab_first_half_spread` entry (#1128): its post-rebase test run was against a22cfee1, not 7db37973 (the branch's parent chain: a0998574's parent is a22cfee1).

**SQL validated read-only against production** (Supabase MCP, the block verbatim with today = 2026-11-03, run at about 03:30 UTC on 2026-11-04): 4 rows, all NCAAF (`ncaaf_over_under`, games on 2026-11-07, written 2026-11-01 and 11-02), 360 ms, the odds read by index (6 games). All four are on Discord (`push_sent`, `discord_signal`). The other two open BETs for games not yet started are `ncaaf_moneyline` rows written while that model was paused; they are not on Discord, and the prompt leaves them out. The old `game_date = today` filter returned none of the four.

**Code graph.** Master moved twice while this was open. #1148 (the API call log) merged at 03:36 UTC on 11-04, and the branch was rebuilt on it. Then about 60 commits landed up to #1168 (2633bfe5), and then #1150 (d39793a3); each time the pull request showed a conflict, so the branch merged that master too: the session logs keep both sides, and graphify-out kept this branch's named graph as the starting point. The graph in this pull request is built on d39793a3's code (graphify 0.9.91, SQL extra, AST only, no LLM call). Each rebuild re-clusters, and graphify keeps a name only while its group keeps most of its members: the first update left 337 groups with a fallback name, the one after #1148 left 285, the one after #1168 left 272, and the one after #1150 left 235. Each time, those groups (and, the first time, the 181 that master named after a file) were named by hand from a per-group digest (size, files, busiest members, the names their members carried before, so a group that carried over kept its name), checked for clashes, symbol shapes and the banned word, and written with a re-signed membership file. One dropped edge made the next update regenerate graph.json, the report and graph.html with the names, and a further update printed "No code-graph topology changes detected; outputs left untouched."

| | master (d39793a3; its graph was built at 1918a2cf) | this branch |
|---|---|---|
| Nodes / edges | 43,088 / 104,478 | 43,571 / 105,701 |
| Groups | 1,249 | 1,139 |
| Named in prose | 1,071 | 1,139 |
| Named after a file path, folder or symbol | 178 | 0 |
| Waiting for a name (fallback file) | 0 | 0 |

Master's 178 are names like "scheduler.py", "tests/test_sbr_loader.py" (several groups), "CLAUDE.md" and "docs: best_line + config_topology". The count is a rule, not a reading: a name is a symbol if it has no space, ends in a file extension, contains "()", starts with a folder and a colon, or equals one of its own members.

**Verified.** The prompt tests (`test_prompt_reads_thresholds`, `test_units_in_prompt_and_views`, `test_rule_model_markets_everywhere`, `test_units_one_basis`, `test_context_budget`): 85 passed after the merge. Targeted failure-set diff on the 60 test files that read the prompt, the session log, the graph or `docs/`, against a clean worktree of master at d39793a3 with no `.env`: the same failure set on both sides, 2 failed, 1,549 passed, 17 skipped each. The two failures are this machine's setup, identical on master: `test_claude_rules_split.py::test_every_rule_file_is_path_scoped[config-topology.md]` (no `.env` in a worktree) and `test_web_site.py::test_record_math_matches_the_node_suite` (no node). No test runs graphify; the five test files that name `graphify-out/` (context budget, everything-in-Supabase, live money, NHL goalie guards, the API call log) only skip it in a repo scan, and all five are in the targeted set. `git diff origin/master --name-only` lists only `docs/` and `graphify-out/` files.

**Deliberately not done.** No test pins the open-pick window: a new test file is a `.py` file, which would redeploy the worker. The app keeps a bet Discord has posted even when its model's cut has since moved; the prompt still applies today's cut (as it did before), which needs the Discord ledger to match. No production write, no Odds API call, no merge.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — every Odds API call is written to `api_call_log`, however the process started):**

Found today: between 18:06 and 22:30 UTC about 112,813 credits went out with almost nothing in `api_call_log`. Another session ran the NBA first-quarter history purchase on mike's PC through a launcher (`run_with_env.py`) that calls `runpy.run_module("data.ingestors.nba_odds_history")`. The purchase's own logs (`%TEMP%/q1trend/buy_*.log` and `.err`) add to exactly 112,813: props 160 + 26,671 + 30,671, team totals 160 + 24,763 + 21,193, and 5,774 and 3,421 in the first runs, which were stopped. Read-only query of `api_call_log` for that window: the Odds API's remaining count on logged rows fell from 1,221,768 to 1,103,846 (117,922), while the rows logged 5,911 credits, all from the worker's usual sources and none from the purchase. The cause: only entrypoints installed the call logger (`monitoring/probe.py`): the scheduler, the pipeline and a list of scripts. A module run that no entrypoint started logged nothing. Plumbing, not a model update.

**What changed.**
- `config.py` installs the logger as its last statement. Every Odds API caller in the repo imports config, so every process that can make a paid call has it. The seven callers that read the key from the environment instead install it themselves: `nfl/data_ingest/odds_api.py`, `nfl/live_model/feeds/odds_live.py`, `nfl/live_model/backtest/pull_prop_snaps.py`, `nfl_live_leader/odds.py`, `ncaaf_live/feeds/odds_live.py`, `scripts/live_feed_probe.py`, `scripts/probe_ufc_markets.py`. Where a run from inside `nfl/` cannot see the repo root, the root is added at the end of the path.
- `install()` takes `source=None`. Left None, rows are named for the module the process ran (`data.ingestors.nba_odds_history`, from `__main__.__spec__`, which `-m` and `runpy` both set) or its script name, and a later call with None never renames them. An entrypoint's own name (`scheduler`, `pipeline`, ...) still wins. `install()` and the timeout floor read the live `Session.request` for the probe's mark, so a second install never wraps twice, even after a test resets the flag.
- The writer starts on the first recorded call, not at install, so the processes that import config and make no call start no thread. The exit hook is still registered at install, so it keeps its place in the exit order.
- A failed write is kept and retried on a later tick, with a pause that doubles up to `API_LOG_RETRY_MAX_SEC` (30 s); it used to be dropped. At most `API_LOG_QUEUE` rows are held, and the Odds API's go last. At exit the writer tries once more; what is still unwritten is reported on the process's own log with the credits it carried. A prune that fails can no longer send a committed batch round again.
- A pytest process never starts the writer: `tests/conftest.py` installs the logger with no writer before anything imports config, and config's own install does the same whenever pytest is loaded (the `ncaaf_live` and `nfl/live_model` suites have no such conftest). So a test run on a machine whose `.env` names production writes nothing. The main suite's conftest also refuses the Odds API host at the transport, so a test that forgets to mock fails instead of billing credits.
- Also closed by the same change: the NFL schedule refresh (nflverse, every 15 minutes, no rows on 2026-10-31) and the college prop pass the scheduler starts when `RUN_NCAAF_PROP_ODDS=1` now log under their module names.

**Verified.** `tests/test_odds_call_log_everywhere.py` (14 tests) runs the purchase's own request code (`nba_odds_history.Meter.get`) in a child process, with the transport answering like the Odds API (`x-requests-last: 3`) and the writer writing to a sqlite file: a `-m` run and a `runpy` launcher run each write exactly one row with the right credits, remaining, status, path, sport and module name; the worker's order (install before config, and after) writes exactly one, named `scheduler`; a database that never answers costs the row and not the call, and says so on stderr; two failed connects then a good one write the row once. It also imports each of the 23 modules that build an Odds API request in a fresh process (nfl/ ones from cwd `nfl/` with no PYTHONPATH) and checks the logger is on. **Session summary (2026-11-03 — the NCAAB model pages say when each model now decides):**

The brief: several NCAAB models changed today, and the app's model pages must say what each one now does, in plain words. Copy only, no decision logic. The NCAAB Totals page still said "Decided the evening before", the 22:00 UTC rule that #1139 replaced (that entry, below, left the page for its own design review).

**What changed (before -> after).**
- NCAAB Totals (`ncaab_total_blend`), model page. Title "Decided the evening before" -> "Decided when the total opens". Tooltip: "decides each game once, the evening before, at DraftKings' total; a game with no total by then is decided on game day" -> decides each game once at DraftKings' opening total, usually within minutes of it posting; DraftKings opens most totals the day before the game, some on game day; it bets at DraftKings, only if DraftKings still offers that opening total at the same prices, and skips the game if the total or a price has moved; an opening total often changes within half an hour, so check DraftKings' total and price before betting; never re-priced. Empty state "posts the evening before each game day" -> "bets only some games, usually the day before each one, as DraftKings opens its totals". Note under the picks "Posted the evening before and never re-priced." -> "Posted at DraftKings' opening total and never re-priced." No clock time: DraftKings posts at no fixed hour.
- NCAAB Totals, Pick Detail: adds "An opening total often changes within half an hour: check DraftKings' total and price before you bet.", the opening moneyline's pattern.
- NCAAB Spreads (`ncaab_spread_market`): "on the morning of game day, from the spread at each book we price" -> "at 10am ET on game day (11am ET from mid-March), from the spread at each sportsbook we check". The empty state keeps one clock time, "by noon ET".
- NCAAB Moneyline (`ncaab_moneyline_own_spread`): the same hour, "at each sportsbook we check", and "It never lays more than -200."
- NCAAB Opening Moneyline (`ncaab_moneyline_first_post`): "checks a game as each book first posts" -> "minutes after each book first posts"; "sometimes the evening before" -> "sometimes the afternoon or evening before" (its evidence: 69% of bets midnight to noon ET on game day, 26% the afternoon or evening before); adds "It never lays more than -200."; the empty state is split into two sentences.
- First-half spread and first-half total pages: unchanged. "Five to ten minutes before tip-off" and "about an hour before tip-off" already match `docs/sports/ncaab.md`.
- Code comments that still said the totals model decides the evening before (`useTodayPicks.ts`, `queries.ts`, `BuiltInModelDetailScreen.tsx`, `markets.ts`, `modelMeta.ts`), and `docs/sports/ncaab.md` (the "In the app" bullet, the Today-board bullet, and the totals card's row in the "What runs" table, which still said hourly at 22:00 / 15:00 UTC).

**Facts behind the copy.** 15:00 UTC is 10am ET in winter and 11am ET from the second Sunday of March (14 March 2027). The -200 cap is `MIN_PRICE = -200` in both moneyline models. The totals model bets at DraftKings (`models/ncaab_total_blend.py` `BOOK`). The 76% / 21% day-before / game-day split is #1139's measurement (the entry below), not re-measured here. A live read-only check (Supabase, `ncaab_total_decisions`): the 6 decisions the five-minute card wrote at 23:48-23:53 UTC on 2026-11-03 were all quoted before game day and made 3.7 minutes after the quote; none was a bet.

**UX review (frontend-ux-designer).** No must-fix. Five should-fix, all applied: (1) the totals tooltip said "minutes after", which a game held for a missing box score is not, said "check DraftKings' total" without the price the card also requires, and stacked "still DraftKings' number"; (2) the totals note repeated "minutes after" and broke the siblings' pattern; (3) Pick Detail now says "total and price"; (4) no count of books: "the eight sportsbooks we price" matches the server's default, but the list is set by an environment variable and the app's sportsbook picker lists 11; (5) the spread empty state carried three clock references, and now carries only "by noon ET". Nits taken: the two long empty states split. Nits not taken: "Decided when the moneyline opens" for the opening moneyline's title (not in this diff), a "-250 is skipped" example after the cap, printing the hour from code (a logic change).

**Verified.**
- Pins watched failing first. On the old copy, 4 of 17 tests in `tests/test_mobile_ncaab_in_app.py` and 7 checks in `mobile/scripts/verify_ncaab_ui.ts` failed; after the review's wording was pinned, 3 tests and 5 checks failed on the pre-review copy. All pass on the branch (17 tests; 52 checks, exit 0).
- `verify_ncaab_ui.ts` also had two Stats checks that failed on master since the NCAAB Players board landed (#1134: "NCAAB has no leaderboard stats", "no hit-rate view"). They now pin what the app does: stats with Points first, and a hit-rate view.
- Targeted failure-set diff against a clean origin/master worktree (78372f2f), 121 test files that read the edited files or the session docs: branch 2,575 passed, 1 failed; master 2,573 passed, 1 failed. Same one failure on both (`test_claude_rules_split.py::test_every_rule_file_is_path_scoped[config-topology.md]`). The two extra passes are the two new tests. After the review edits, the 66 of those files that read the edited sources: 1,458 passed, 0 failed.
- The 26 verify scripts that import an edited file, run on both trees: master 36 failing checks, branch 34. The only difference is the two Stats checks above; the other 34 fail identically on master (missing packages and older pins in this setup). Every exit code is the same except `verify_ncaab_ui.ts`, which now exits 0.
- Full `tsc --noEmit`: 30 errors on the branch, 30 on master, identical sets; 0 outside `src/lib/queries.ts` and `scripts/` on both, so the over-the-air gate passes. `ux_scan`: 5 files, 0 findings.

**Deliberately not done.** No production write, no Odds API call, no graphify rebuild, no merge. The Claude-mobile prompt (`docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md`) describes no NCAAB model's timing, so it is unchanged. Flagged, not fixed: the prompt's closing reminder ("Picks may flip to AVOID on later refreshes ... lines refresh hourly") is wrong for every posted-once model; the prompt reads `game_date = today`, so a totals bet posted the day before does not show until game day; the app's sportsbook picker lists 11 bettable books against the server's 8; the opening moneyline's and totals' "check before you bet" sentence also shows on a settled pick.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — men's college basketball on the Stats tab, Players board):**

Matt (screenshot of the Stats sport sheet): *"We need to add NCAA basketball men's and women to this stats tab with all the same features as the others."* Answers to the scoping questions: men's first, then women's; women's is Stats-only (no picks or models); no player-prop lines for college ("Don't include those. Include things like margin etc, bet types on teams I've seen on fan duel and draft kings"), which became a per-bet team Hit Rates view as a later change, covering spread, total, ML, first half, team totals and alternates; history "This season + 2025-26".

NCAAB `games` 2026: 6,300 finals. `latest_odds` NCAAB markets: totals, spreads, h2h (10 books), *_h1 (8), team_totals_home/away and alternate spreads/totals (7); no winning-margin market. NCAAB is already in `SPORTS` on master (since 2026-10-30), so the screenshot is a build without that update. ESPN is not reachable from the sandbox (proxy refused site.api.espn.com; WebFetch: ENOTFOUND), so the college summary's `boxscore.players` shape is ESPN's NBA one as stored in `espn_nba_summary_401811026.json`, not measured on a college game.
