- `ncaab_player_game_log` + `ncaab_player_box_misses`, `v_player_season_totals_ncaab` and the four Stats RPCs (`player_window_totals_ncaab`, `player_recent_games_ncaab`, `player_season_stat_values_ncaab`, `player_h2h_stat_values_ncaab`), the WNBA set with the table swapped: `data/migrations/add_ncaab_player_game_log_2026_11_03.sql`, on `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`. Log is anon-SELECT through a policy; the miss table is worker-only.
- `data/ingestors/ncaab_player_box.py`: parses `boxscore.players` by label with the NBA parser's played rule; refuses a team whose players' points do not sum to the stored final, or a box naming other teams. `ncaab_espn_box._one_game` hands it the summary it already fetched (no second ESPN call); a refusal or write error there never undoes the team box. `step_ncaab_box` also runs the player catch-up for the last 3 ET dates (no call when nothing is due).
- Job `ncaab_box_backfill` kind `players` (2026..2027); declared `ncaab-player-box-2026-2027`, enabled, requested_by matt. Free (ESPN), about 6,300 + this season's finals at one call a second.
- App: `statCatalog` NCAAB stats (Points, Rebounds, Assists, 3PM, PRA, Steals, Blocks, Minutes), Hit Rates on, no prop market; `queries` routes the five reads; `playerLog` reads the log on the player page; `boardSeasonET('NCAAB')` is the ending year.

**Verified.** 13 new tests (`tests/test_ncaab_player_box.py`), including the migration applied twice, the grants, and every RPC's answer on a scratch Postgres 16; each guard's test watched failing with the guard removed (points sum, team pair, the team-feed hook). tsc error set identical to origin/master's. The Postgres tests in `tests/test_ncaab_espn_box_pg.py` fail here on master too: on Postgres 16 `schema_is_current`'s first catalog query names the 17-only MAINTAIN privilege and its rollback discards the CREATE it guards (production is 17.6).

**Not done.** Women's college basketball; the NCAAB Teams board and per-bet team Hit Rates; the same-position card on the player page (no college position source); 3-letter team abbreviations (the app cannot read `ncaab_teams`). Nothing written to production from here.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — NCAAB opening moneyline model, LIVE: `ncaab_moneyline_first_post`):**

mike approved a new model when a new search beats what is live, and capped NCAAB moneylines at *"-200 max"*. The round-5 search found one: a bettable book's FIRST moneyline post for a game, priced off the same book's spread at that moment, with 3+ other bettable moneylines up, edge above 0.015. `Updated-By: mike`.

**What changed.** New: `models/ncaab_moneyline_first_post.py` (the rule, on the 15:00 UTC model's own pricing, flags and -200 floor), `scripts/ncaab_moneyline_first_post_card.py` (the card and its ledger `ncaab_moneyline_first_post_decisions`, one row per (game, book) first post), `scripts/ncaab_first_post_fill.py` (the fill check), `scripts/ncaab_moneyline_first_post_build.py` (the replay and the frozen numbers), `models/saved/ncaab_moneyline_first_post_2027.json`. Additive: `models/ncaab_book_quotes.standing_quotes` and `models/ncaab_moneyline_own_spread.read` take `window_min=None` (each book's newest row however old); `scripts/ncaab_moneyline_card.pick_rows` takes `model_id`; the 15:00 UTC card writes `other_model_bet` for a game the new model bet and takes both models' locks; the capture writes the fill check after its write, in its own commit, and never fails on it. Registered beside the 15:00 UTC model: config (cut 0.015, EV floor 0.022, price floor -200, own probability, rule), settlement (h2h), the closing-line market function (a new stamp, every earlier one kept), heartbeat (20 minutes), the every-minute job, the worker-only table, the app's name and timing copy ("NCAAB Opening Moneyline"), the generated thresholds, docs.

**The trigger.** An every-minute job, not a hook in the capture: it also sees the first posts the refresh passes store, keeps its own kill switch, heartbeat and failure, and needs no watermark (the ledger is the memory). The scheduler's planning read (latest_odds keys for games tipping within seven days, minus the ledger's) starts the card only for a new post, so a bet goes out within the minute after the capture stores the post. The fill check is the one thing written inside the capture, because only the capture sees the unchanged and the pulled quotes.

**Verified.** The build's replay of the card's own functions on the round-5 history (2,268,688 moneyline and 1,790,416 spread rows; 183,212 first posts at 138,563 moments in 31,969 games; margin tables equal to the research's to the last digit) matched all 353 research bets: game, side, price, book and moment, edge difference 0.0. 2021-2025 297 bets +67.11 units +15.15% [7.34, 23.37]; 2025-26 56 bets +9.33 units +13.87%; neighbours 0.01 587 +7.27% (3 of 5 seasons) and 0.02 150 +14.78% [3.74]. With the 15:00 UTC model, one bet a game, the earlier first: 1,053 bets +102.42 units +6.52% [1.76, 11.01]; 2025-26 123 bets +17.46 units +11.82%, the research's numbers exactly. Unit tests and a real-Postgres card test (the production latest_odds trigger installed): -201 never bet, the 3-other rule, the tie-break, no freshness window, one bet a game across both models in both directions, a game closed by a BET and not by a decision, missed and pre_model, the fill check. Each rule broken on purpose made its test fail (ten mutations). Targeted test files (45, every one that walks a registry this touches) against clean master `16e651e7`: branch 1,321 passed, 3 skipped; master 1,274 passed, 3 skipped (the two new files are the difference); no failure on either. App: the frontend-ux-designer review (ship with fixes) was applied: its own badge "Open ML", the page copy says an opening price often changes within half an hour and that the two moneyline models never bet one game twice, and both NCAAB moneyline Pick Detail notes name no line. `verify_ncaab_ui`, `verify_a11y`, `verify_pick_timing`, `verify_pick_categories`, `verify_paused_catalog` and `verify_results_in_units` all pass on branch and master; the tsc error set on the touched files is identical to master's (environmental only, none in them).

**The feed-typo guard (#1140), wired in.** Merged onto `claude/ncaab-line-bounds` (and origin/master `8ee822b7`): the card drops a moneyline or spread priced beyond +/-100000 and a spread beyond +/-60 before any flag, count or price reads it, logs and counts each, lists it in the ledger row as `out_of_range`, and writes a moment whose largest edge is above the data-error cap as `edge_cap` (no bet, the run fails), as the 15:00 UTC card does. The cap is 0.40, not 0.25: the replay's largest priced edge was 0.2416 (BetMGM's +340 first post on central-connecticut_umass-lowell, 2021-11-17, one of the 353 bets, and the largest of all 97,429 posts priced with 3+ other books up). Rerun with the guard: no quote dropped at any first-post moment, the 353 bets identical row for row (equal frames, same hash), the combined 1,053 / +6.52% unchanged. The `window_min=None` read lives in the guard's `_newest` now. Each guard rule broken on purpose made its test fail (three mutations).

**Deliberately not done.** No production write, no worker job, no Odds API call, no merge. The 15:00 UTC card's in-lock re-read of the other model's BETs is covered by the lock-order test, not by a race test. The live fill rate is unknown until bets post: the history only bounds it (at least 69% of 2021-2025 bet quotes and 34% of 2025-26 ones stood five minutes).

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — NCAAB cards: a feed typo in a quoted line can never become a bet; the first-half spread card never bets a stale call):**

Found today: the odds feed holds a DraftKings full-game total of 1465.0 for `NCAAB_2023-03-17_providence_kentucky` (stamped 2023-03-12 23:16Z, stored by the 2026-11-03 backfill at 02:07Z; that book's next row is 146.5). The totals card skipped only a missing line or price, no NCAAB card capped the edge, and the EV floor is only a floor, so a live 1465.0 would have priced the under near 100% and been written as a bet. None was (`ncaab_total_decisions`: 140 rows, every total 131.5-171.5). A data-safety guard, not a model update: no rule, cut, floor or artifact changed.

**What changed.** One shared guard, `models/ncaab_quote_bounds.py`, read by all five live NCAAB cards (the first-half total card merged during this work, PR #1137, and is wired the same way).
- A quote is a data error when its line is outside its market's range (full-game total 90-220, spread +/-60, first-half total 40-120, first-half spread +/-35) or a price is beyond +/-100000. It is dropped BEFORE any consensus, flag or price reads it, so it cannot move a median, and the book's older row is not used in its place (its real number at that moment is unknown). A missing value and a price inside -100..+100 are left to each model's own checks, as before (the moneyline model keeps such a spread's line in its sign-error median on purpose, pinned by its tests).
- Where: `models/ncaab_book_quotes.standing_quotes` (the 15:00 UTC spread and moneyline reads; `dropped_quotes` returns what it dropped), `models/ncaab_total_blend.decide` (DraftKings' total, status `out_of_range`), `models/ncaab_first_half_spread.quote_offers` / `asof_consensus(market=...)` and `models/ncaab_first_half_total.quote_offers` (the first-half quotes and the full-game consensus behind them, including the team term's closing lines).
- What happens to the game follows each card's own rule. The totals card makes the game wait (`out_of_range`, not final; looked at again on the next run). The spread, moneyline and first-half cards decide on the books left; the dropped quote is listed in the decision row (`out_of_range` in the quotes JSON, or named in the detail). Each drop is logged with game, book and value and counted as `quotes_dropped` (totals: `out_of_range`) on the run's line.
- The edge cap: a best edge above the model's cap is a data error. No bet; logged at error; the run raises `CardIncomplete` after writing the rest, so the failure alerter reports it. The totals card holds the game (status `edge_cap`, not final); the one-moment cards record it as `edge_cap` with no bet. A model with no cap raises `KeyError`, so a new NCAAB card cannot skip choosing one.
- The first-half spread card refuses a decision fetch more than five minutes old (`MAX_DECISION_DELAY_MIN = 5.0`, the first-half total card's rule): the game is missed and the run raises, never a bet on a price that may be gone. Its decision rule is otherwise unchanged.
- The totals build drops a data-error first total before the blend fit and the replay, and prints the count, so a rebuild on a fresh copy (which would hold the 1465.0 row as DraftKings' first total for that game) cannot fit on it.

**The ranges, measured** (eight bettable books, pre-game rows). Research copy of `odds` 2021-2026 (3,456,036 rows): full-game totals 109.5 at the 0.001% quantile, max 194.0, one BetMGM 42.0 at -10000/+3300 (2020-12-20) the only one below; spreads -47.5 to +34.5; first-half spreads -27.5 to +20.5 and totals 43.5-94.5 (the 2023-26 purchase); prices: spreads -375..+285, moneylines down to exactly -100000 (975 prices, kept), nothing beyond +/-100000 at a bettable book (one Pinnacle -304573). Live `odds`, 2026-27 games, rows stamped at or before tip (read-only MCP query): totals 125.5-172.5, spreads -44.5 to +11.5, first-half spreads -18.5 to +5.5, first-half totals 64.5-82.5; one Hard Rock moneyline of -200000/+4000 (stetson_clemson, 2026-11-02 14:34Z), which the guard now drops. A -78.5 spread and a 247.5 total are stored, but only after a tip, which every card already excludes.

**The caps, measured** (largest edge in each model's own replay on the research copy): totals 0.1257 (1,513 bets 2022-2026), spread 0.1500 (613), moneyline 0.0594 (914), first-half total 0.3381 (17,142 decided games; FanDuel under 79.5 when every other book had 68-69, graded a win), first-half spread 0.4633 (17,206 decided games). Caps: 0.25, and 0.60 / 0.50 for the first-half spread / total models, each above its replay's largest. **Its three largest replay edges (0.4633, 0.4108, 0.2583) are all quotes no other book had**: BetMGM home +12.5 when every other book had -12.5 (vmi_western-carolina 2024-01-27), DraftKings away -14.5 when the others had -9.5 or +1 (lafayette_mercyhurst 2024-12-07), FanDuel home +10.5 against +5 (hofstra_campbell 2024-01-13). All three are graded wins in that model's evidence. A 0.25 cap would have removed them from the replay, so the cap sits above them and this is reported, not changed.

**After #1139 merged (the totals card now decides at DraftKings' first posted total).** Merged into this branch with four fixes its author listed. (1) The totals card's diagnostic statuses include `out_of_range`, `edge_cap` and `first_post_gone`. (2) The 0.25 cap made the `line=100.5` test quotes (an edge of about 0.36) data errors, so the card-run tests in `tests/test_ncaab_total_blend.py` and `tests/test_ncaab_total_card_pg.py` use 125.5 (126.5 and 129.5 where they move it). (3) The build keeps this branch's typo filter, which drops and counts a quote, in place of #1139's raise; the reduction itself still drops a typo before taking the first row. (4) The totals cap comment now quotes the first-post replay's largest edge, 0.1606, measured here; still under 0.25. `models.ncaab_total_blend.TOTAL_RANGE` now reads the shared bound, so there is one number. #1139 already skips a typo'd first post in SQL and waits on a typo'd standing total; this branch adds the price bound, the edge cap and a check inside `decide`, so a typo that slips past the loader is still never priced. Replay on research round 5's first-post copy (`q5/truefix`), #1139's head against this branch: 2,436 bets and 2,431 card-path bets (26,874 quoted games through `nb.decide`), both files byte-identical, the rebuilt artifact identical apart from its build time.

**Verified.** `tests/test_ncaab_quote_bounds.py` (26), watched failing first: before #1139, 23 of its first 24 failed on master (the 24th, a five-minute-old fetch still deciding, is a control); against #1139's code all 26 ran and 23 failed, the 3 that pass being the two controls and the 1465.0 standing total, which #1139 guards itself: on master a 1465.0 total, a -335 spread, a -45.5 first-half spread, a 7.5 first-half total and a -300000/+250000 Hard Rock moneyline each WROTE a bet; an in-range quote at an absurd price bet on every card; a stale first-half fetch bet; a -335 BetMGM spread moved the first-half spread card's consensus from -4.5 to -5.0, and a 1465.0 BetMGM total the first-half total card's from 146.0 to 146.5. The totals fixtures in `tests/test_ncaab_total_blend.py` moved from a 100.5 line (a 0.36 edge, now a data error) to 125.5. Replays, each card's build on the research copy, origin/master against the branch: totals 1,513 bets, spread 613 rows (542 at 3+ other books), moneyline 914, first-half spread 17,206 decided games (1,253 bets), first-half total 17,142 decided games (2,527 bets); every bets file byte-identical (same SHA-256) and every rebuilt artifact identical apart from its build time, so the bounds removed nothing real. The 1465.0 row is not in that copy (taken 2026-10-26..30, before the backfill stored it), so no replay read it; the totals build reports 0 dropped. Targeted tests (every test file naming a touched module, plus test_config; the Postgres card tests ran on a scratch cluster), 23 files against an origin/master worktree. Before #1139 (16e651e7): master 546 passed, the branch 570. After #1139 and #1142 (8ee822b7): master 559 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed; the branch 585 passed (the 26 new), the same 1 skipped (mobile node modules not installed), 0 failed. The failure sets are identical both times: empty.

**Not done.** The 1465.0 row is still in `odds` (no production write here); the guard makes it harmless to every card. The spread build's closing consensus for the margin table and the moneyline build read no bounds (training reads; the measured history has no out-of-range spread to drop). No sign-error rule was added to the first-half models: the three off-market first-half replay wins above are a question about that model's evidence, raised, not decided here.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — Tracked picks book the posted stake; CLAUDE.md §4 agrees with §2):**

mike: "everything must be units", and the two decisions #1138 left open. Display, copy and one CLAUDE.md line: no model, threshold, pause or stored row changes.

**Performance tab, tracked picks (app, over the air).** They used to score every tracked pick at 1u risked, the "1u flat" pill, so a −200 win read +0.50u there and +1.00u on the Record tab.
- The default is now **As posted**: the stake the Record tab books (`postedPickUnits`). Each pick is staked to win 1u at `COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds)`, risk is capped at 3u, and a pick with no price counts as 1u to win 1u. A push risks nothing, as on the Record tab, so the return figure has the same denominator.
- **My stakes** is the member's own override. The amount typed is what the bet risks, at the same decided price. A bet they leave alone stays as posted, and its chip says so ("1.5u as posted"). With no price, a custom stake pays even money, the way the posted stake counts an unpriced pick.
- The old 1u-risked mode is gone. A device that stored it lands on As posted; its saved custom stakes are kept for My stakes.
- The graders (`lib/trackedPerformance.ts`, `lib/liveTracked.ts`) no longer read `profit_flat`, which is 1u risked and invents −110 for a pick with no price, and they have no hidden 1u default. The stake comes from the new `lib/trackedStake.ts`, which uses `thresholds.stakeFor`, the same price math as `postedPickUnits`. The graders keep no value imports, because a node test loads `trackedPerformance.ts` with type stripping.
- Before → after, one tracked pick: a −200 win +0.50u → +1.00u; a +150 loss −1.00u → −0.67u; a win with no price +0.91u → +1.00u.

**CLAUDE.md §4.** The units rule said `profit_flat` "is stored as dollars-per-$100 stake, so divide by 100 to report it". That is the 1u-risked figure and contradicted §2. It now says to report units on the posted stake (`published_pick_unit_profit` / `_risked`, which is `stake_for` at `COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds)`), and that `profit_flat` / 100 is the 1u-risked figure no surface shows. The heading and section number are unchanged. CLAUDE.md is 41,683 bytes, and with the session-start hook 43,820 of the 44,000 budget.

**Verified.**
- **Watched failing first.** The two new pins in `tests/test_mobile_units_one_basis.py` were red on the unchanged code (origin/master 16e651e7). The 18 new checks in `mobile/scripts/verify_units_one_basis.ts` could not load there (no `lib/trackedStake.ts`). After the change, putting the posted stake back at 1u risked turned 14 of the 18 red; restored, all pass.
- **Tests:** 236 targeted test files (every test file that names the app, CLAUDE.md, `profit_flat` or units), against a clean origin/master 16e651e7 worktree. Branch 3 failed / 5,038 passed / 45 skipped; master 3 failed / 5,036 passed / 45 skipped. The failure sets are identical: `test_claude_rules_split` (a worktree has no `.env` by design), `test_mobile_nhl_skater_stats::test_the_behavioural_checks_pass` and `test_web_site::test_record_math_matches_the_node_suite`.
- **Type check:** the error set is identical, 30 vs 30, and the over-the-air gate has 0 errors outside `src/lib/queries.ts` and `scripts/`.
- **Verify scripts:** all 77 have the same failure sets on both trees. The four touched ones pass (`verify_units_one_basis`, `verify_tracked_performance`, `verify_live_tracked`, `verify_results_in_units`).
- **`ux_scan`:** 0 findings.

A posted bet's stake editor opens empty, with the posted stake as the placeholder: Save without typing used to store the rounded figure as the member's own stake, so a +300 win read +0.99u. And each row says what it risked under As posted. All seven nits are in: both captions, the editor hint, "your stake" on the chip, the chip's VoiceOver label, a clear button in the stake field, and the Explainer paragraph.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — Discord routing for #ncaabw, #ncaab-live and #ncaabw-live):**

The three channel ids were already set on the Railway `worker`. No webhook URL was added and no Discord message was sent.

- `NCAABW` is in `config.DISCORD_SPORTS`. `DISCORD_CHANNEL_NCAABW` is read with the other channel ids, and the first pick creates the bot's webhook in that channel, the same way NCAAB does.
- `config.DISCORD_CHANNELS_LIVE` reads `DISCORD_CHANNEL_LIVE_<SPORT>`. `_live_webhook_for_sport` still uses `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_<SPORT>` when that is set. Otherwise it opens the channel id through `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` and names the created webhook `<sport>-live`, cached apart from the pre-game webhook. A live channel id this process cannot open is skipped: one error line, no Discord call, the pick is not marked sent, and it is not redirected to the shared or pre-game channel.
- `discord_create_channel` accepts one leading emoji (`🔴-ncaab-live`). The slug stays lowercase, 2–32 characters, with a letter in it.
- Where live posts run, measured 2026-11-03 from production logs and variable names (values not readable from this session). `worker` is `SERVICE_ROLE=pipeline` and skips `live_loop`, `ncaaf_live_loop` and `nfl_live_leader`. `pollers` is `SERVICE_ROLE=poller` and runs those loops. `pollers` lists `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_MLB`, `_NCAAF` and `_NFL`, and does not list `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` or any `DISCORD_CHANNEL_*`. The bot token and the three new channel ids are on `worker`. NCAAB / NCAABW live posts that originate on `pollers` stay unsent until that service has `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (Manage Webhooks) plus the channel ids, or `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_NCAAB` and `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_LIVE_NCAABW`. Neither was added.
- Tests, no Discord calls: `tests/test_discord_notifier.py`, `tests/test_discord_channel_probe.py` and `tests/test_config.py`, 152 passed. The channel tests in `tests/test_ncaab_total_blend.py`, 10 passed.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — `ncaab_total_blend` decides at DraftKings' first posted total):**

mike approved replacing a live rule when a new search beats it (*"do the rest of outstanding tasks"*). Model update, `Updated-By: mike`. Research round 5 (2026-11-03) found the card deciding later than its own evidence: it decided at 22:00 UTC the evening before (else 15:00 UTC), while the evidence's "first stored" total included the purchase's late-evening hours (00:00-03:00 UTC) the card never used. At the card's real instant, every game, cut 0.04, the shipped model made 1,563 bets, +66.46 units, +3.90%, interval [-0.50, 8.00] on 2022-2026. At DraftKings' true first posted total (measurable from the half-hourly opener history stored 2026-11-03), with the one feed typo removed (1465.0 for 146.5 on NCAAB_2023-03-17_providence_kentucky) and the blend refit on those quotes: 2021-2025 1,787 bets, +122.66 units, +6.26% [1.72, 10.66]; 2025-26 649 bets, +58.05 units, +8.17% [0.42, 15.19].

**What changed.**
- `scripts/ncaab_total_card.py`: a game is due once DraftKings has a first post (`load_first_posts`: the earliest pre-game DraftKings totals row by its own time, total 90-220 and both prices in the WHERE before the first row is chosen, a stamp tie keeps the row stored last). It is bet at that quote, as of its hour, only while the first post is still DraftKings' standing number (same total and both prices; `same_quote`); otherwise it is decided `first_post_gone` with no bet. A standing total that is itself a typo makes the game wait (`out_of_range`, not final). The slate reads today through 7 Eastern dates ahead (2.6% of first posts land 2+ dates early, at most 7). The run returns before the season read when no undecided game has a first post.
- `models/ncaab_total_blend.py`: `decision_hour` and its constants removed; `TOTAL_RANGE` / `valid_total`; `decide(..., now=)` ages a missing box to the run's instant, so a held first post keeps its cutoff and a box that never lands still stops holding after 36 hours.
- `scheduler.py`: the card every five minutes at minute 3, 8, .. 58 UTC (three after each capture tick; the capture has 120 seconds), grace 240 s. `tracking/job_heartbeat.py`: silence bound 20 minutes.
- `scripts/ncaab_total_blend_build.py`: the reduction spec is the first posted total, typos dropped first (`load` refuses a reduced set holding one); per-season replay grades; `card_path` replays the card's own decision (`nb.decide` with each season's walk-forward numbers, then the price and EV floors) and matches it to the replay game by game.
- `models/saved/ncaab_total_blend_2027.json` rebuilt from research round 5's corrected first-post set: November c 0.2103, beta 0.2368, sigma 17.2224 (5,735); December on c 0.6143, beta 0.4585, sigma 16.5124 (24,540), equal to the research's fit. End state and league rates unchanged; stage 2 moves slightly.
- Config: no value changed (cut 0.04, EV floor 0.07 against a replay minimum of +0.0734, price floor -200); the EV floor comment re-measured.
- Docs: `docs/sports/ncaab.md` (when a game is decided, the hold, the new evidence table beside the old rule's at its own instant, the risks), `docs/thresholds.md`.

**Verified.**
- The build's replay equals the research's bets game by game: 2,436 of 2,436, same side and price, edge difference 0.0. By season 2022 307 bets +32.05 units (+9.55%), 2023 274 +39.05 (+12.98%), 2024 496 +19.35 (+3.55%), 2025 710 +32.21 (+4.14%), 2026 649 +58.05 (+8.17%).
- The card's own decision code, walk-forward over 2022-2026 (`card_path` in the build: the hold, the refit at one cutoff, the inputs, the price, the price and EV floors, each season's numbers built only from earlier seasons): 2,431 bets, every one the same game and side as the replay's (largest edge difference over every scored game 1.5e-8), no bet refused by a floor. The replay's other 5 bets are all 2021-22 IU Indianapolis games the card holds as `box_missing` (14 games of that season whose teams have two or more old games with no stored box); live, such a hold fails the run so the alerter reports it. By season: 2022 302 bets +33.25 units, 2023 274 +39.05, 2024 496 +19.35, 2025 710 +32.21, 2026 649 +58.05.
- Tests: the 18 new or changed tests in `tests/test_ncaab_total_blend.py` and `tests/test_ncaab_total_card_pg.py` (real Postgres: the earliest usable row wins, a 1465.0 first row is skipped, a later-stored row is not read, run_card bets the standing first post once and never bets a first post that moved) all fail on master's code and pass here. Targeted failure-set diff (27 files): this branch 876 passed, 0 failed; clean master d69bd6da 864 passed, 0 failed.

**Deliberately not done.** No Odds API call, no worker job, no production write, no merge. The app's model page copy ("Decided the evening before") is not changed: DraftKings posts 76% of first totals the day before and 21% the same day, so it is close, and an app change needs its own design review. Measured for the PR, read-only: at 2026-11-03 19:55 UTC 1 of 405 upcoming games had an undecided first post.
## 2026-11-03 — Local carbon copy of Supabase and GitHub on mike's PC

**Why.** mike, 2026-11-03: *"supabase is the SOR, but everything should be a carbon copy on my local as a backup -
everything"*, and *"can you also mirror github on my machine? the full setup"*.

**What changed.** Three scripts and a section of `docs/local_ops.md` ("Local carbon copy of Supabase and GitHub").
- `scripts/local_mirror.py` copies every table in every schema that holds app data, saves the schema as `.sql` files,
  downloads every storage object and checks the counts against Supabase.
- `scripts/local_mirror_github.py` mirrors each repo and exports its issues, pull requests, reviews and settings.
- `scripts/local_mirror_run.ps1` is what a scheduled run would start.

The copy lives at `C:\Users\micha\Documents\betting_local_copies\`, outside the repo. One line under the Supabase rule
in CLAUDE.md §1b points at it. No model, threshold or pipeline code changed.

**How a table converges.** It is stored as chunks, each covering a range of primary keys, each with its row count and
a fingerprint of its rows computed by Postgres.
- Tables under 2M rows, the 14 tables with no primary key, `latest_odds` and `latest_prop_odds` are copied whole every
  run. The two `latest_*` tables were measured that day at 16.8M updates and 82M deletes against 2.8M and 8.8M live
  rows.
- The seven bigger tables are caught up: new keys are appended, every range's count is compared (deletes), and the
  newest 40 ranges plus the oldest-checked seventh have their fingerprints compared (edits).
- None of those seven has an `updated_at` column (measured from the catalog), so the fingerprint is the only way an
  edit is found.

**Load rules, as built.**
- Every read is one transaction on the transaction pooler: `BEGIN ... READ ONLY` with `SET LOCAL statement_timeout =
  60s`, UTC, and pinned date and float output, so a fingerprint means the same thing on every connection.
- `SET SESSION` was not used. On the transaction pooler it would stay on a shared server connection, and a live scorer
  could inherit read-only.
- At most 50k rows or about 32 MB a read, then 0.5 s of rest.
- It waits while more than 6 other client queries are active, checked at most every 15 s.
- No reads at 10:00-10:50 UTC or at :15-:27 of hours 11-22 UTC. A read does not start within 75 s of either window.

**What was verified.**
- 39 new tests (`tests/test_local_mirror.py`, `tests/test_local_mirror_github.py`) run the sync engine against an
  in-memory server that each test changes, then compare local rows with the server's.
- 16 deliberate breaks of the module, one at a time, each failed at least one test (the harness rewrote the module and
  restored it byte-identical). One break first hung instead of failing. The count loop now always advances.
- Bug found and fixed: the restore export (`export-csv`) crashed on every table with a timezone-aware timestamp,
  because Arrow's strftime rejects `%f`. Found by running it on the real copy of `social_post_queue`. The test for it
  failed first (exit 3) and passes on the fix. 143 rows then exported.
- Gap found in review and fixed: a range check that hit the 60 s limit failed the whole table pass. The next run would
  check the same cold range and fail again, so that table would never converge. A timed-out check now means "copy
  that range again", and the copy halves its batch until it fits. Two tests failed first: the engine crashed on
  the timed-out range, and PgSource re-raised the timeout. Both pass on the fix. The first copy started before
  this fix. Its copy in `bin\` was updated, so the next run uses it.
- `tests/test_ddl_guard.py` flagged the module because its schema dump writes `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` lines into
  the backup files. It runs none of that text, and every statement it sends is read-only, so it has an exemption entry
  that says so.
- Full suite on this branch (on master 71b152d0): 8 failed, 9,909 passed, 72 skipped, in 1 h 49 min. Run alone, the
  same 6 of the 8 fail on clean master and on this branch: the rules-split `.env` check, the NHL totals card lock,
  the picks unique index, two price pre-filter rollbacks, and the web record-math check against node.
- The other 2 are social-post scheduler tests, which pass alone on both trees and after this branch's tests (124
  passed). This branch imports nothing they use.
- Live, read-only. Inside a read transaction: statement_timeout `1min`, transaction_read_only `on`, TimeZone `UTC`,
  isolation `repeatable read`.
- `nfl_kickoff_wind_reads` (key: text plus timestamptz) copied 16,282 rows exactly. On a new connection all 5 of its
  ranges hashed the same, so 0 were copied again.
- A no-key table (`nba_injury_asof`, 91,354 rows) copied by heap pages and matched. auth.users holds 0 rows on the
  server.
- Storage: 18 of 18 objects downloaded and matched their stored md5. A second pass downloaded none.
- GitHub: the private repo mirrored through gh's credential helper. Its remote URL holds no token, and no token string
  is in any exported file.

**The first full copy.** Started 2026-11-03 18:48:09Z as a detached one-off (`Start-Process`, the wrapper, from
`betting_local_copies\bin\`).
- Wrapper PID 10028; the database copy is PID 28088, the GitHub mirror PID 28424.
- Logs: `betting_local_copies\logs\supabase_20261103T184810Z.log` and `github_20261103T184810Z.log`.
- Tables are copied smallest first, so the biggest table comes last.
- First 612 s of table copying (18:48:57 to 18:59:09, from `supabase_mirror\progress.json`): 5,495,380 rows, 8,974
  rows/s on average. About 150 tiny tables dominate that average. The last 44 s, on 100k-300k row tables, ran
  at about 16,900 rows/s.
- Estimate: 173.9M rows remain by the planner's count. At 10,000-15,000 rows/s that is 3.2 to 4.8 hours of reading.
  The four refresh windows still ahead (19:15 to 22:15 UTC) add about 53 minutes, and the count and fingerprint checks
  about 20 more. So the expected finish is between about 23:15 and 01:00 UTC. The big tables are wider than the
  ones measured, and their old rows are read from disk.
- GitHub: `MJACode/betting-model` mirrored in about 2 minutes: 1,406 refs (264 branches, 1,142 pull-request heads),
  291.6 MiB. The small private repo mirrored too. Its export refused rulesets (403, "Upgrade to GitHub Pro") and has
  no Pages site (404).

**Not done here.** No scheduled task, service or startup entry. mike has not approved one. The proposed `schtasks`
commands are in `docs/local_ops.md`. No write to the database and no Odds API call.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — One unit basis everywhere, and the older faults the units sweep found):**

mike: one unit basis on every surface. CLAUDE.md §2 already defines units as the published stake: each pick to win 1u at `COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds)`, risk capped at 3u, a pick with no price at the bare 1u. The Record tab, Yesterday and Discord #results book that (`published_pick_unit_profit` / `_risked`). After #1135 the model pages, the Models list, the custom-model backtest, the player and team pages and the Streamlit dashboard showed `profit_flat / 100` (1u risked per pick) instead, labelled "1u risked per bet". So a −200 loss read −1u on a model page and −2u on the Record tab. Plumbing and display: no model, threshold, pause or stored row changes.

**App (mobile/src, over the air).**
- Built-in model page and Models list: they read `v_public_track_record`'s own `units` / `risked` columns. When those are absent, they compute from the settled picks with `postedPickUnits`. The tiles are now "Return on bets" ("each pick to win 1u") and "Units". History rows show each pick's published-stake units. A pick with no price counts at 1u, as on the Record tab, and is named "N unpriced, staked at 1u".
- Custom models: the backtest still grades every scored pick its rules match; only the unit basis changed. The new read-only function `custom_model_backtest_posted` (migration below) sums those picks on the published stake. Until it answers, the summary keeps its 1u-risked units and the tile caption says "1u risked, updating"; a failure of that second call no longer takes the screen down. Rows graded on the phone no longer read `profit_flat`, which settlement fills in at an invented −110 when a pick has no price.
- Player and team pick records: on the published stake, printed at two decimals like the Record tab (a −115 loss is −1.15u, not −1.2u).
- The Yesterday sheet and the Return on bets sheet no longer say the Models tab's percentages differ, because they don't now.

**Migration.** `data/migrations/custom_model_backtest_posted_2026_11_03.sql`:
- It is one idempotent DO block. It creates a STABLE plpgsql function over `custom_model_picks` with no row cap, sums `published_pick_unit_profit` / `_risked` at the decided price, and grants it to anon and authenticated.
- It changes no view, pick or existing function. It is registered in `data/view_migrations.py` before the posted-stake pair, and the app's RPC is declared in `data/anon_readable.py`.
- The body's aggregate was run read-only against production over `mv_scored_pick_outcomes`: `mlb_moneyline`, 2,629 graded picks, −104.23 units on 3,049.11 risked (−107.30 at 1u risked). The MCP role cannot execute `custom_model_picks` itself (`permission denied for function _jsonb_text_array`).

**Dashboard.**
- The sidebar's last 7 days, the running units chart and the per-model table read `v_public_track_record` / `_daily`: the same rows and units as the Record tab. The per-model table is all-time since the record start, not windowed.
- The pick log's units are the published stake (`tracking.published_units.unit_profit`).
- Record queries no longer re-cut settled picks with today's action filter (CLAUDE.md §1c). They filter on what the pick was, plus `RECORD_EXCLUSIONS` and duplicate copies. Only Today's Picks keeps the live action cut.

**The older faults.**
- Settle log (`tracking/paper_tracker.py`): the total line is read back from the rows this pass stamped, on the published stake. It used to print the settlers' `profit_flat` sum, which counts an unpriced pick at an invented −110. The return dict keeps its stored figures.
- Opening-signal shadow track (`tracking/opening_signals.py`): going forward, a pick with no price grades but writes no profit (`_priced_profit`). Stored rows are not rewritten. Measured read-only on 2026-11-03, twelve settled rows carry the invented −110: `nhl_over_under` 5, `nba_team_total_luck` 3, `nba_total_luck` 2, `nba_first_half_total_luck` 2, +1.37 units between them. 136 unpriced rows are still pending.
- NFL wind and opener cards: the dollar `stake_amt` column and both `--bankroll` flags are gone. `wind_totals.select_bets` takes no bankroll, and nothing read the column.
- `docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md` and `scripts/emit_threshold_sql.py` no longer tell a reader to re-paste a threshold block. The prompt joins `model_action_thresholds`.

**Left on the 1u-risked basis, on purpose.**
- The Performance tab's tracked picks: a member's own chosen stake per bet, 1u by default.
- The Opening comparison screen: a shadow experiment that shows ROI only.
- The model card's "Holdout ROI · backtest, flat bets": a training metric.

**Phone entries typed as dollars** before #1135 read ÷100 ($50 shows 0.5u). That follows the repo's convention that 1u is a $100 flat bet, and they are left as they are.

**Verified.** Each new guard was watched failing first, on the #1135 head (588cbeac): `tests/test_units_one_basis.py` (dashboard, settle log, shadow track, NFL cards, prompt doc: 18 of 19 red), `tests/test_mobile_units_one_basis.py` (10 of 11 red) with `mobile/scripts/verify_units_one_basis.ts` (built-in stats `undefined`), and `tests/test_custom_model_backtest_posted_migration.py` (2 of 3 red before registration). The dashboard rendered under Streamlit AppTest with canned rows: no exception, no "$", only Today's Picks applies the action cut, and the pick log reads +1.00u / −2.00u for a −200 win / loss. The `frontend-ux-designer` review's should-fix items are in: two-decimal units on the player and team pages, the honest custom caption, a posted-units failure no longer an error screen, captions spoken as units, and an ROI sheet that points at a figure that exists. Its nits are in too, except the model card's "Holdout ROI · backtest, flat bets", which is a training metric and keeps its wording. Against clean origin/master 71b152d0:
- **Tests:** 240 targeted test files (every test naming a touched module, all mobile tests, test_config, test_context_budget). **Session summary (2026-11-03 — Eggz mentions and the Oct 29 under):**

mike: Discord mentions and `CAR Panthers/GB Packers U47.5 (-123) 1U` were stuck in needs_review, and those rows were holding the recaps. Also approved a Corrected recap for the Eggz 2026-11-02 daily, whose all-time line was off by 1 win and 1u. The restatement was not run.

**Measured, before the change.** `capper_messages` / `capper_picks` (Supabase, 2026-11-03):

- `1566495943430242375` (Eggz, posted 2026-11-01 16:55:37Z). Body is `Sunday Nov. 1`, `NFL`, `BAL Ravens +3 (-105) 1U`, then `<@1408438245594763375>`. Pick 1664 is the Ravens spread, graded LOSS -1.05u on `NFL_2026_08_BAL_BUF`. Pick 3251 is the mention, needs_review, no game_date.
- `1565385033441742909` (Eggz, posted 2026-10-29 15:21:15Z). Body is `Thursday Oct. 29`, `NFL`, `CAR Panthers/GB Packers U47.5 (-123) 1U`. Pick 3252 is that line, needs_review. The game is `NFL_2026_08_CAR_GB`, final 27-19 (total 46). Under 47.5 at -123 for 1u wins 1u.
- ParlayMatt `1566116597980995586` is `<@…> moonshot parlay`. `1566504414771023912` is `TD Mega parlay <@…>`. `1565454245032103989` is the mention alone. Picks 3253, 3255, 3254.
- Eggz daily ledger has 2026-11-02 and not 2026-10-29 or 2026-11-01. The week lock is `discord_eggz_results_week:2026-10-19` (no week of 2026-10-26). No Eggz month row.

**What changed.** A mention (`<@id>`, `<@!id>`, `<@&id>`, `<#id>`, `@everyone`, `@here`) is removed before the line is read. A line that is only a mention creates no row, and the other lines keep their numbers, so a graded leg on the same message is not rewritten. A total accepts `U47.5` with no space after the side. `/` splits two teams; `@`, `vs`, and `at` still need spaces. `grade_pending` already calls `reparse_stored` on needs_review messages and then grades a final, so the next Eggz poll deletes the mention rows and grades the under at the posted price. No second ledger. `config.RECORD_RESTATEMENT_APPROVALS["2026-11-02"]` quotes the approval. The register test pins that one entry.

**Not changed.** ParlayMatt's moonshot and TD Mega posts stay needs_review after the mention is removed. `moonshot parlay` is a parlay header with no legs, which is unparsed. `TD Mega parlay` is two words before `parlay`, and the header takes one tag word, so it is not a header. Neither has a price or a leg, so neither is graded. The 2026-11-02 daily already went out; it is not re-posted by the grade pass.

**Verified.** `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_eggz_picks.py tests/test_record_never_restated.py`: 98 passed. The grade test uses the stored bodies, leaves pick 1664 graded, deletes the mention row, and grades the under WIN +1u risked 1.23u on `NFL_2026_08_CAR_GB`. A second `grade_pending` returns 0.

**After deploy.** The 15-minute Eggz poll grades. Morning recaps then post the held days still inside the 7-day catch-up, and the week and October while those windows are still due. The Corrected 2026-11-02 recap is a separate command, after that grade:

```
python -m scripts.eggz_record grade --apply
python -m scripts.eggz_record restate 2026-11-02 --apply
```

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — Everything in units: every result, stake and payout on every surface, and the record-price line made true):**

mike: *"everything must be units"*. Results are always units, never dollars (CLAUDE.md §4). The 2026-11-02 UX review found the app's model page history rows printing "+$90.91". Display and copy only: no stored column, no stored meaning of `profit_flat`, no threshold, no pause, no production write.

**The rule used everywhere: change the unit, never the basis.** A surface that showed `profit_flat` (stored per 100 staked) now shows `profit_flat / 100` as units at 1u risked, captioned "1u risked per bet". A surface already on the posted stake (1u to win, `published_pick_unit_profit`, the Record tab, Discord) stays on it. The Models tab and the Record tab therefore still differ for the same model, as they did before; the model page tile is now "Flat units" and the Record copy says "Models risk a flat 1u on every pick, so their percentages won't match."

**App (mobile/src, over the air, no native build).**
- `lib/format.ts`: `formatCurrency` / `formatCurrencySigned` deleted. New `formatFlatUnits` (stored per-100 to "+0.91u", two decimals like Discord's `{units:+.2f}u`), `formatFlatStake` ("1u", "0.5u"), `FLAT_UNIT` (100) and `FLAT_UNIT_TONE_SCALE`, so the colour follows the units as printed (`pnlColor(v, 2, 0.01)`: a +0.40 stored result prints "0.00u" and is grey).
- Built-in model page: history rows "+$90.91" to "+0.91u" (VoiceOver "+0.91 units"); the P&L tile becomes "Flat units"; the ROI caption "vs $100 flat per bet" becomes "1u risked per bet". Each tile speaks as one VoiceOver stop, units as "units". Custom model page and the Models list row: the same.
- Performance tab: synced sportsbook bets (not live yet), logged bets and tracked picks all print units. "$100 flat" pill to "1u flat"; stake chips, the stake editor ("Stake in units", "Reset to 1u") and the Add a bet form ("Stake (units)") take units. Stakes are still STORED at the per-100 scale (100 = 1u), so a custom or logged stake saved earlier as $50 now reads 0.5u.
- Betslip bar: "$10 pays $26.45" to "1u pays 2.65u". Parlay screen: "Potential payout" printed a UNITS figure with a "$" (2.65u shown as "$2.65"); now "2.65u".
- Copy: "EV is expected profit per $1" to "per unit staked"; "$100/bet" to "1u/bet"; "Flat $100 bets" to "Flat 1u bets"; "Models use a flat $100 bet" to "Models risk a flat 1u on every pick" (Yesterday sheet and the Return on bets sheet); the Explainer's "$50" example and "$100 flat or a custom amount".
- **The record-price line.** "Model records and stats use the best price across all books" was false for `ncaab_moneyline_own_spread`, which bets the book with the largest edge (269 of its 914 replayed bets had a better price elsewhere). `lib/myBooks` now returns one statement for every model: "Model records and stats use the price the model found, not your book's" / "record kept at that price". The Explainer's sportsbook section and the Settings "Your sportsbooks" hint say the same: the record is graded at the price the model found.

**Left on purpose.** Subscription prices on the paywall (real money, not a result). The optional Settings bankroll ("1 unit = $20", Matt-approved, #831): it converts a unit into the member's dollars and shows no result; removing it is a product call.

**Dashboard, email, logs, the picks prompt.**
- Streamlit dashboard: the paper-Kelly "Bankroll $" tile and chart removed (they are dollars on a paper roll no published stake uses); "Last 7 days" and the pick log show `profit_flat / 100` as units (priced picks only); "Total Exposure $" becomes "Units at Risk" and "Bet $48" becomes the published stake ("1.1u to win 1u", `stake_for`). In passing: six dashboard queries sent a literal `{_ACTION_FILTER}` to Postgres (plain strings, not f-strings), so every tab but Today's Picks errored; and `Styler.applymap` is gone in pandas 3, which crashed the Pick Log.
- `email/render_picks_email.py` (wired to nothing): stakes in units, no bankroll or exposure in dollars; `email/preview.html` regenerated.
- Settle and summary logs (`tracking/paper_tracker.py`, `opening_signals.py`, `parlay_track_record.py`), the MLB record audit and the NFL opener / wind card printouts: units, no Kelly or bankroll dollars. `tracking/model_quality.py`'s volume check labelled `recommended_bet / 100` (paper-Kelly dollars) as units; it now counts the published stake's units laid.
- Claude-mobile picks prompt (`docs/mobile_picks_prompt.md`): it asked for a bankroll and printed `Bet ($)` from `kelly_fraction x bankroll`. It now selects the published stake in units (`published_pick_stake(COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds))`, the function the record books) and tells Claude never to quote `profit_flat` as money. The new block has to be pasted into the Claude project's instructions once; nothing in the repo writes that field.
- Docs: three dollar figures in `docs/followups.md` and `docs/discord.md` restated in units; `mobile/docs/UX_REVIEW.md` now says results go through `formatFlatUnits` / `formatUnits`, never a dollar formatter.

**Verified.** New guards, each watched failing on the unedited code first: `tests/test_mobile_results_in_units.py` (no dollar formatter, no "$" on a result outside billing and the Settings bankroll, no false best-price line) with `mobile/scripts/verify_results_in_units.ts` (crashed on master: `formatFlatUnits` missing); `tests/test_results_in_units.py` (17: dashboard, email, logs, volume check, audit, NFL cards); `tests/test_units_in_prompt_and_views.py` (5: the prompt block). Pins that asserted the old dollar strings were updated (`test_mobile_a11y`, `test_mobile_contrast_tokens`, `test_record_units_agree`, `test_yesterday_discord_units`, and the a11y, contrast, betslip and NCAAB verify scripts). Measured on the final tree against an origin/master (7b45c44f) worktree: the app's tsc error set is identical to master's (none in a touched file; the OTA gate passes); all 75 shared mobile verify scripts have the same failure set as master and the new one passes; `ux_scan` finds the same 54 findings in the 26 touched files on both trees, none new; 160 targeted test files (every test that names a touched module, all mobile tests, test_config, test_context_budget) fail on exactly one test on both trees, `test_nhl_totals_card::test_insert_once_under_the_model_lock`, which this change does not touch. The dashboard's per-model ROI is now over priced picks only (an unpriced pick's -110 is invented), so that one figure moves as well as its unit. The `frontend-ux-designer` review's must-fix (say which unit a model page uses: "Flat units", "1u risked per bet", "Models risk a flat 1u on every pick") and its VoiceOver, wording and duplicate-rounding fixes are in.

**Not done.** Logged bets and custom stakes saved before this change were typed as dollars; they now read as that number / 100 in units (no migration of on-device data). Synced sportsbook bets (not live) read at the same scale; which unit they should use once sync ships is open, and the Settings bankroll stays a Settings-only display (#831's pinned scope). The model pages stay on 1u risked while the Record tab and Discord use 1u to win: they now say so, and switching them is a separate call.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — `ncaab_first_half_total` LIVE: NCAAB first-half totals an hour before tip):**

mike: *"we need to build 1H ... no matter what"* and *"I want it all done and go live now"*; he approved adding models when a new search beats what is live. Model update, `Updated-By: mike`.

**What it is.** The research's round 5 cell (family A_to, first-half totals, the purchase an hour before tip, best book, cut 0.02, every game): the bettable books' full-game consensus total mapped to a first-half mean (a + b x T, frozen per season), plus the two teams' shrunk first-half scoring tendencies (k 236), priced through the round-4 table of first-half totals; a side shorter than -200 set aside before the pick; the single best quote bet when its edge is above 0.02. Evidence: 2,529 bets, +125.78 units, +4.40%, [0.57, 8.12]; with the -200 limit 2,527 bets, +123.78 units, [0.45, 8.05]; by season +67.27 / +41.03 / +15.48 units with the limit. 78% unders, no closing value (-1.95%), and 14 of 1,036 versions cleared the interval test where about 26 would by chance. `docs/sports/ncaab.md` has the rest.

**What changed.** `models/ncaab_first_half_total.py` (reuses the first-half spread module's table, consensus, halves and shrunk-sum code; that module's table function gained an optional grid and support, its own defaults unchanged). `scripts/ncaab_first_half_total_build.py` and `models/saved/ncaab_first_half_total_2027.json` (a -1.928, b 0.4850 on 2021-2026). `scripts/ncaab_first_half_total_card.py`, with its own ledger `ncaab_first_half_total_decisions` (worker-only). The capture now retries the tip-minus-60 call every minute and names a fetch time shared by two marks after the decision it serves; the window job plans and runs the total card as its own step; the alerter names a game either first-half model lost. Settlement on period 1 of `ncaab_period_scores` (`totals_h1`), the closing-value stamp bumped, config's seven entries, the app's market, chip, note and timing. The first-half spread model's evidence was re-measured in the docs on fresher full-game lines: 1,143 bets, +74.40 units, +5.90%, [0.71, 11.69], so it now clears the bar it missed; no earlier fetch time beats tip minus 10. No code change to that model.

**Verified.** The build replays 2023-24..2025-26 through the card's own functions from the research's cached quotes: its maps equal the research's on all three seasons, and its bets equal the research's game by game, 2,529 of 2,529 without the limit and 2,527 of 2,527 with it (side, book, line, price; largest edge difference 4e-16). Ten rules broken one at a time, each failing its own test (team terms subtracted, the limit after the pick, the table key negated, the away residual flipped, the over threshold flipped, the T-60 retry, the shared-instant name, the window's plan, the settlement map, a stale decision fetch), and the real-Postgres card test fails with the limit left out of the decision. Targeted tests, 172 files, against a clean `origin/master` (71b152d0) worktree: failure sets identical (the same 2 failures in `test_social_posts` and `test_social_profile` on both), 3,895 passed on the branch. App: `verify_preferred_book.ts` 86 passed (master 84, the same 1 failure on both), `verify_pick_timing` and `verify_paused_catalog` all pass, a type check of the touched files identical to master; the UX review approved (one copy change, taken).

**Not done.** No production step (after merge: view migrations, threshold sync, artifact `--store`). `graphify update .` not run.

**Flagged, not fixed.** The first-half spread card decides on a tip-minus-10 call however late its run comes before tip; the total card refuses a call more than 5 minutes old. Changing the spread is a model change and was not asked for. Before this change a 10:10am ET tip's 15:00 UTC call was named "15z" and could never decide the spread; it is now named for the decision.

**Session summary (2026-11-02 — nba_prop_market bets the play-in, playoffs and Cup final):**

mike's rule passed the pre-registered search on the 272 such games of 2023-24..2025-26 (`docs/nba_search_postseason.md`, branch `nba-searches-graded`), so it now bets them, with its rule, cut, floor and caps unchanged (`Updated-By: mike`). None of the six `nba_prop_projection` cells passed, and nothing else was graded there, so no other NBA model reaches them.

**What changed.** `models/nba_total_luck.py` gains `graded_postseason` beside `regular_season`, which is untouched. `scripts/nba_total_luck_card.py` splits its read so the old gate and the new one share it. `scripts/nba_prop_market_card.py` gains `rule_slate`, and `look` reads it; `slate` stays regular-season, so the projection card needed no edit. `scripts/nba_prop_window.py` plans from `rule_slate` and treats one of these games as done when the rule alone has decided it. `data/ingestors/nba_schedule_refresh.py` gives a postseason game a `games` row on its own day. `tracking/failure_alerter.py` and `scripts/nba_pregame_window.py` warn on a lost game and on an open bet with no final.

**The live numbers, stated before it ships.** The search's pass is every qualifying proposition: 1,953 bets, +138.87 units, +6.8%, 95% interval +2.1% to +11.3%, -192 to +210. The live cap writes one a game: **251 bets, +23.36 units, +9.71%, interval -3.82% to +22.81%, which crosses zero; -155 to +188, median +106, 5th to 95th -125 to +145, 64.5% plus money, 2 worse than -150, 0 worse than -180.** By season +1.08 / +19.99 / +2.28 units over 84 / 81 / 86 bets, so one season carries most of it. Recomputed here from the independent checker's list (`%TEMP%/pm_postseason_build/one_a_game_prices.py`, keyed on `real_game`). The first possible bet is the 2026-27 Cup final on 2026-12-11 (`nba_schedule`, ESPN agrees on the date, no teams or valid tip yet); the 8pm stored for it belongs to the placeholder row, which never joins the real game. After that the play-in, if the two-days-after-the-last-regular-day pattern of 2024, 2025 and 2026 repeats, 2027-04-13. No stored row holds that date.

**Findings from the two reviews, built in.** A second row for one real game must not hold a bet: an unlisted row beside a listed game with no final is refused and named (`listed_twin`), the game-day insert stands aside for a feed row the other way round, and the results step scores that row with each team's own score. The reviewer's rule as written (any listed game of the two teams within two days) was narrowed, with evidence: 143 of the 251 playoff games of 2023-24..2025-26 fall within two days of the previous game of their series, so it would refuse Game 4 beside a played Game 3 on the failed-refresh day the unlisted branch exists for; a listed game that already has its final does not count. The Cup final's type no longer rests on one note string, because a listed `regular` row reaches four other models: any note saying "championship" (ESPN's 2023 final read "NBA In-Season Tournament Championship") or the date the league's own placeholder gives the final. A game with no type is not read as postseason, since an unlisted Cup quarterfinal is regular-season. The alert confirms a listed row on its own date, so an unplayed Game 6 is not named; it keys per game, so a night's second lost game still posts. When ESPN's tip and the feed's sit over 30 minutes apart the card warns and writes both to the ledger, and a game with only the feed's tip is marked `tip_source=feed`.

**Watching the rule on regular-season games too: yes.** It decided all 92 of 2026-10-20..11-01 (`nba_prop_market_decisions` joined to `nba_schedule`), so it stays quiet, and the alert runs live before the first postseason game needs it. Not extended to `nba_prop_projection`, which writes a decision only on its own projection path and does not bet these games. The stranded-bet warning covers both NBA prop models. Other sports would want it too (WNBA held four wrong-date rows in its 2026 playoffs, none with a bet on the 12 rows examined); not built here, recorded in `docs/followups.md`.

**Verified.** 50 rules broken one at a time on the worktree, each with the tests written for it, and restored with `git checkout`. 48 failed as they should the first time. Two passed with their rule broken, so their tests were wrong: the moved-line test never reached the ledger read because the game's bet stopped it first (a game decided with no bet now pins that), and the stub ignored the model id, so the door's shared one-a-game read was never reached (fixed). Both then failed as they should. `tests/test_nba_*.py`, the settle, settlement-timing, pick-integrity, publish-key, publisher-lock and settled-record suites, failure alerter, heartbeat watchdog, `test_config`, `test_ddl_guard`, `test_anon_readable`, `test_everything_in_supabase`, `test_context_budget`, `test_void_picks` and the window-job scheduler tests: 1,961 passed, 23 skipped, and 2 failed on a docstring that named the new gate from another file, which the source scan is there to catch (reworded; `tests/test_nba_postseason_gate.py` 66 passed). Real Postgres: `tests/test_nba_postseason_pg.py`, 8 passed. Saved ESPN boxes (2025-26 Cup final, a 2026 play-in, 2026 Finals Game 5) run through the live parser and the real settler. Reads against production: schedule by season and type, the 92 of 92 decided, 143 of 251 playoff games within two days of the last in their series, and the reporting query in `docs/thresholds.md` (0 rows, as there are no such bets yet).

**Not done.** No production step: nothing here can run before a postseason game exists. The reads to make before and after the Cup final are in `docs/followups.md`. Three things flagged and left alone, also there: a wrong-date feed row on a regular-season day stopping the three total models, an unlisted All-Star row, and a bet with no final in the other sports. Season-long numbers staying regular-season is a separate pull request, designed and not built: it carries a migration, which this one must not, and it is display only on those screens (`docs/followups.md` holds what it needs; the Cup final's rest input is the exception, and is in the review fixes above). No mobile change. `graphify update .` not run.

**Flagged, not fixed.** The alerter posts "Resolved" when a key leaves its list, so a per-game warning that ages out of its 24-hour or 30-day window reads as cleared when it only expired; the shape exists already, there are just more keys now. If the three total models' undecided read fails, the rule's watch is skipped that pass. A feed row dated after the real game and after its final passes the second-row test; the feed would hold no prices for it, so no bet follows, but the alert may name it. Measured, so not flags: the wider "championship" string matched exactly the three finals among ESPN's 598 type-2 games of December 2023, 2024 and 2025, and each of the four stored Cup final dates holds one schedule row, so the date rule retypes no other game.

**Review fixes on the same work (2026-11-02):**

Thirteen findings from two further reviews, all fixed in the code or the docs, one in part. **The gate:** a postseason game dated onto a regular-season day reached all four other NBA models, on master too. `_regular_games` now drops an unlisted row when the schedule lists the same two teams under a postseason type within two days (`models.nba_total_luck.postseason_twin`, `Updated-By: mike`). It cannot catch a wrong date before the schedule lists the real game, and `docs/followups.md` says a postseason game can be bet that way until then. **The tip:** a postseason start over 30 minutes before the feed's is read as ESPN's error and the window and the closing snapshot are measured from the feed's (`postseason_tip`), with the disagreement sent to the ops channel. This replaces what the entry above says: it decided on the earlier. **Closing-line value:** a bet whose only start is the feed's records none, because that start ran 2.3 to 20.2 minutes behind the league's on all 107 games of 2026-10-20..11-02 and refresh passes store in-play rows typed `open`. A row the other way round now reads ESPN's tip.

**What the review did not have.** `data/migrations/clv_standard_v2_2026_10_14.sql` runs on every pass and recomputes any bet not yet stamped, on the same two starts, so a test in the Python capture alone would have been overwritten on the next pass. The test is now in both, and `tests/test_nba_clv_feed_start.py` runs the real capture and the real file on a scratch Postgres. None of this touches a stored row: seasons 2024-2027 hold 0 unlisted NBA rows, and 0 NBA bets since 10-01 are unstamped.

**Rejected in part, with evidence.** The stranded-bet finding wanted a postponed game's bet settled NO_ACTION automatically, ideally, keyed on `nba_schedule.status_text`. That column has never held a postponed value (0 rows, all seasons), so no real row can test the trigger, and it would sit on the settlement path. The alert's text is split into the two cases, and the automatic path is in `docs/followups.md`.

**Verified.** 32 rules broken one at a time on the worktree, and the docs put back to their old text once, each with the tests written for it. One passed with its rule broken, so its test was wrong: the test that ESPN's false start bounds nothing held no quote for the false bound to read. It has one now, and fails as it should. One more, any listed type counting as a same-teams copy, passed in the gate's file and failed in the test beside it (the PHX_MIN pair in `tests/test_nba_prop_market.py`). Reads against production: the feed's lag above, the Cup finals with no `games` row (0 of 3), `status_text` above, and the report's query (0 rows). `graphify update .` not run.

**Checked again by a fresh pass, same day.** 34 rules broken one at a time on the worktree, each with its own tests: 33 failed as they should. One survived: the closing-line recompute's row filter, because its tests left the bet alone in the table, so the check that decides whether to run at all stopped first. In production another bet is nearly always waiting, so that filter is what keeps an in-play price out of the close. A test with a second bet beside it now fails with either half of the filter removed. The doc halves of two findings had no test and now do: a misdated postseason row can be bet by the other four models until the schedule lists the game, and a played game's bet is never voided. Reads against production, by primary key: the Cup final is stored for 2026-12-11 (`0062600001`, no teams yet); all 12,018 schedule rows carry a well-formed start, so the recompute's new casts cannot fail on stored data, and none is postponed; the 11-07 Mexico City game has only the league schedule's row. The lead figures in `docs/thresholds.md` reproduce exactly from the research files. Tests: 1,265 passed, 18 skipped (the research cache is not on this machine), 1 failed in `tests/test_picks_unique_row.py`, which fails the same way on master (the NFL card's `ON CONFLICT` sits 1,005 characters past its insert and the test allows 1,000); then 166 passed on `tests/test_nba_clv_feed_start.py`, the docs tests and the scheduler tests.

**Open.** Whether the Cup final's row should count toward a finalist's rest is mike's call, and the code is unchanged (`docs/followups.md`). The read of the first neutral-court game's feed id is 11-05 to 11-07.

**Session summary (2026-11-03 — team_stats_asof_integrity: a young live snapshot series is not the leak):**

Daily pipeline `61acad408c5f47e6b7fcd1316255d055` (2026-11-03 11:00–11:39 UTC) finished `ok=false` with `failed_steps=health_check`. The 12:25:23Z `system_health_checks` set had `team_stats_asof_integrity` STALE/CRIT: `NBA: impossible=0 thin=[(2027, 1)]`. The 04:58Z set the same morning was OK (`NBA: 33628 rows ok`).

**Measured.** `nba_team_stats` season 2026: 930 rows, 31 as-of dates, 2026-06-18..2026-08-30, max games_played 82. Season 2027: 30 rows, one as_of_date 2026-11-03, max games_played 8, `created_at` 2026-11-03 11:04:53.476505Z. `games` NBA 2027: 107 finals, 14 dates, 2026-10-20..2026-11-02, span 13 days. Table total now 33,658 rows, of which 30 are 2027, so 33,628 is the table before that insert — the 04:58 check. A season with zero rows is not thin (`seasons_with_too_few_snapshots` only counts seasons present in the stats table); the first 2027 snapshot created the thin season.

**Why the grace missed it.** PR #852 excuses a live season while its finals span fewer than 5 days. 13 is not fewer than 5. The snapshot series itself started that morning.

**What changed.** `verify` also excuses the live season when its own snapshot series is young and still current: the earliest as_of_date is fewer than 5 days before the check's UTC date, and the newest is that date or one day behind (the pipeline stamps America/New_York; a UTC morning still sees yesterday). A series whose first row is older than that has collapsed. A series whose newest row is two or more days behind has stopped. Historical seasons are not excused. An empty games read, including a blank game date, is not a grace. No production write, no rebuild, no pause, no threshold change.

**The ingest gap, not fixed here.** `nba_stats` is not in the scheduled worker pipeline (`run_pipeline.py`: stats.nba.com blocks datacenter IPs). `_nba_season_for_date` is not gated off October — 2026-10-01 is season 2027. **Session summary (2026-11-02 — `ncaab_first_half_spread` LIVE: the NCAAB first-half spread, and the live first-half capture it needs):**

mike: *"we need to build 1H and game lines ML and Spread no matter what"*, and *"we do not need to wait 10 games ... I want these models live now"*. Model update, `Updated-By: mike`. The research's round 4 cell (family A, `spreads_h1`, close, best book, cut 0.02, every game): the bettable books' full-game consensus spread mapped to a first-half mean (a + b x M, one fit per season on the earlier seasons, frozen), plus a shrunk team first-half tendency (k 782.2); every bettable book's first-half spread priced through a kernel table of past first-half margins; the single best quote bet when its edge is above 0.02. **It did not clear the research bar** (pooled 2023-24..2025-26: 1,253 bets, +68.07 units, +4.91%, interval [-0.30, 10.63]). It is live on mike's instruction.

**What changed.**
- `models/ncaab_first_half_spread.py`: the research's pricing ported exactly (kernel table, probabilities, both sides' raw edge, the best quote with ties to the alphabetically first book), the as-of consensus (one function for the decision-time consensus and each finished game's closing consensus), the halves rule, the team term, the decision.
- `scripts/ncaab_first_half_spread_build.py` and `models/saved/ncaab_first_half_spread_2027.json` (fitted on 2021-2026: a -0.037, b 0.557; 5,448 table pairs from 32,007 games; 365 teams' 2025-26 sums). The build replays 2023-24..2025-26 through the model's functions, refitting before each season on the earlier ones.
- `data/ingestors/ncaab_first_half_odds.py`: one per-event call per listed game at 15:00 UTC on its date and 180, 120, 60, 50, 40, 30, 20 and 10 minutes before tip, markets `h2h_h1`, `spreads_h1`, `totals_h1`, `team_totals` (all stored, each book's main number under the market and other rungs under `_alternate`; `split_main_rungs` extended to `spreads_h1`), source `odds_api_ncaab_first_half_live` (prune-protected), ledger `ncaab_first_half_fetches` with each call's billed credits and the fetch time it served. ESPN orientation through the NCAAB resolver, the purchase's swap for a flipped event. A balance floor of one day of the measured burn. Kill switch `RUN_NCAAB_FIRST_HALF`.
- `scripts/ncaab_first_half_window.py` and `scheduler.run_ncaab_first_half_window` (job `ncaab_first_half_window`, every minute): the plan, the fetch, then the card, then publish.
- `scripts/ncaab_first_half_spread_card.py`: decides each game once on its tip-minus-10 fetch (5 to 10.5 minutes before tip; retries every minute; no fallback to the 20-minute fetch); `ncaab_first_half_spread_decisions` with every book's priced quote; `ncaab_first_half_team_games` keeps each finished game's halves and closing lines so a run reads the odds of only the games finished since. BET rows only, at the bet book's own number and price; DraftKings' price only at the same number, else `line_book`.
- Settlement: `spreads_h1` settles on period 1 of `ncaab_period_scores` (no overtime; a level half on a whole number pushes; pending while the periods are missing or do not add up). Closing value reads first-half spreads from 'close' rows only (in `_closing_odds` and the CLV migration, function comment bumped), so its picks are unmeasured rather than 0.0 against their own price.
- Label "Duke @ Kansas — First Half Duke +3.5" (`_build_pick_label`), checked by `pick_integrity` (first-half spread must say First Half; its team and signed number are checked after that phrase).
- Config: cut 0.02, `ACTION_THRESHOLDS` 0.0 / 0.02, EV floor 0.03 (smallest replay EV +0.0346), price floor -200, own probability, rule. Market mappings in the scorer, the Claude-mobile prompt and `clv_game_market`. Worker-only tables, heartbeat (20 minutes), probe list, goalie-guard allowance, an undecided-game alert in the failure alerter.
- App: `gameMarketForModel` -> `spreads_h1` (it read the full-game spread before, through the 'spread' substring), the signals headline "First Half Duke +3.5", the team page counts it with first halves, its prices read with the main game markets, chip "1H Spread", "NCAAB First Half Spreads", page copy that says it posts five to ten minutes before tip, no picker slot.

**Verified.**
- Replay: 1,253 bets, identical to the research's on game, side, book, line and price for all 1,253 (largest edge difference 3e-16); 2023-24 477 bets +31.44 units +6.06%, 2024-25 533 +16.47 +2.80%, 2025-26 243 +20.16 +7.19%. Each season's a, b, alpha and beta equal the research's fits. `ncaab_period_scores` equals the research's ESPN halves on all 35,307 graded games.
- Tests: `tests/test_ncaab_first_half_spread.py`, `test_ncaab_first_half_odds.py` (mocked feed, no paid call), `test_ncaab_first_half_spread_card_pg.py` (real Postgres: fetch, ledger, the rows' created_at equal to the ledger's instant, the plan, the card's bet and decision, decided once, dry run writes nothing, a missed decision fetch raises), `test_mobile_ncaab_first_half.py` (the app modules under node). The Postgres test found a pandas merge-key dtype mismatch (a team name from Postgres against one built in pandas) that the unit tests could not; fixed.

Taken: the chip spelled out ("1H Spread", like "F5 Spread"); the team page's footnote says "first-half bet" not "first-half total bet"; a first half's row title may take two lines so a long school name is not cut out of the middle; the page copy says five to ten minutes; a probability note that a push is left out. Not taken: a label fallback in the signals headline when the card has no team names (the full-game spread has the same gap). Flagged, not this change: parlay and daily-results rows clip long labels, and the model page's history rows print dollars (`formatCurrencySigned`), against the units rule.
- After rebasing onto master (a22cfee1): 1,181 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed on this change's tests and every file it or the commits it was rebased over touch (the model's four test files, the app's first-half tests, config, context budget, market mappings, threshold parity, EV floor, dashboard rules, monitoring, goalie guards, pick integrity, first-half market, the NBA first-half, team-total and totals models, the NCAAB purchase and prune tests, the failure alerter, worker-only tables, everything-in-Supabase, settlement timing, the paper tracker, heartbeats and the scheduler).
